# Batch 695 Independent Academic Review Package

## Review status

- Total questions: **276** (IDs **9124–9399**).
- All items are **PENDING** independent subject-expert review. The proposed answer and explanation are author submissions, not approvals.
- Coverage is grouped by board, subject, topic, and concept. Keep each question ID unchanged when returning decisions.
- Review each cited official source directly. The locator and proposition below are an author-side evidence crosswalk, not a verbatim source quotation.

## Review workflow and decision fields

For each question, replace PENDING only after review. Record concerns and revisions in the fields provided. Do not record an approval without an authenticated reviewer identity and date.

- ANSWER_CORRECTNESS: PENDING (suggested values: SUPPORTED, NOT_SUPPORTED, UNCERTAIN, PENDING)
- DISTRACTOR_QUALITY: PENDING (suggested values: SOUND, NEEDS_REVISION, UNCERTAIN, PENDING)
- CURRICULUM_ALIGNMENT: PENDING (suggested values: ALIGNED, NOT_ALIGNED, UNCERTAIN, PENDING)
- SOURCE_SUPPORT: PENDING (suggested values: SUPPORTED, NOT_SUPPORTED, UNCERTAIN, PENDING)
- AMBIGUITY: PENDING (suggested values: NONE_FOUND, PRESENT, UNCERTAIN, PENDING)
- RECOMMENDED_REVISION: PENDING
- REVIEWER: PENDING
- REVIEW_DATE: PENDING
- FINAL_DECISION: PENDING (APPROVE, REVISE, REJECT, ESCALATE, or PENDING)

## Questions

## Board: Licensure Examination for Architects

### Subject: Architecture Utilities and Construction

#### Topic: Accessible Routes and Floor Details

##### Concept: Continuous Accessible Route

###### Question 9124

- **Question ID:** 9124
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Architects / Architecture Utilities and Construction / Accessible Routes and Floor Details / Continuous Accessible Route

**Stem**

A planned accessible route to a public learning room crosses a short change in level that is currently drawn as a step. What is the strongest design response?

**Options**

A. Provide an applicable step-free transition and verify the full route and relevant ramp or mechanical-access provisions in the current BP 344 rules.
B. Keep the step and add a sign telling users to request assistance.
C. Rely on a portable board without reviewing its suitability or route conditions.
D. Move the room label while leaving the level change on the accessible path.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Provide an applicable step-free transition and verify the full route and relevant ramp or mechanical-access provisions in the current BP 344 rules.

**Explanation as submitted:** The 2024 BP 344 IRR states that level changes require a ramp except where specified alternatives serve them. A route review must check the whole path and the exact applicable conditions.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [Revised 2024 Rules and Regulations Implementing Batas Pambansa Blg. 344](https://ncda.gov.ph/revised-2024-rules-and-regulations-implementing-batas-pambansa-344/) — National Council on Disability Affairs; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; Revised 2024 BP 344 IRR
  - Source-level relevant sections: Minimum accessibility requirements: ramps; floor surfaces; channel covers and floor protrusions; wiring/outlets; gratings; accessible routes
- [Program for the June 8 and 10, 2026 Licensure Examination for Architects](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/Program%20for%20June%202026%20LEA%28Architect%29_rev.pdf) — Professional Regulation Commission; Professional Regulatory Board of Architecture; OFFICIAL_PROGRAM; June 2026 Architecture examination program
  - Source-level relevant sections: Subject block: Utilities, Structural Conceptualization, Building Materials and Technology; PRC 2026 examination schedule
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** NCDA 2024 BP 344 IRR §1; PRC Architecture exam scope.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Resolve steps, level changes, and interruptions along a planned accessible route using the current applicable rules.

**Study lesson reference**

- Building Systems, Construction Coordination, and Life Safety — 5. Accessible routes and vertical transitions
- Material ID: architecture-building-systems-and-construction; section ID: 5-accessible-routes-and-vertical-transitions
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): The 2024 BP 344 IRR states that changes in level require an accessible ramp except where a dropped sidewalk, curb ramp, elevator, or other mechanical device serves the change. The route must be considered continuously through the site and building: a compliant entrance followed by a step, blocked corridor, or inaccessible destination does not resolve the route. The official rule includes ramp dimensions, gradients, landings, handrails, tactile warnings, and other conditions; verify each against the current table and its exceptions. Do not infer full compliance from one measured feature.

##### Concept: Grating and Channel-Cover Detail

###### Question 9125

- **Question ID:** 9125
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Architects / Architecture Utilities and Construction / Accessible Routes and Floor Details / Grating and Channel-Cover Detail

**Stem**

A floor grate on an accessible path is level with the adjacent finish and slip-resistant, but the gaps measure 16 mm. What revision addresses the identified deficiency?

**Options**

A. Keep the grate because levelness and slip resistance satisfy every requirement.
B. Specify a grate with spacing no greater than 13 mm and verify levelness and slip resistance after installation.
C. Cover the grate with a loose mat that can shift during use.
D. Move the grate to another part of the accessible path without checking its opening size.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Specify a grate with spacing no greater than 13 mm and verify levelness and slip resistance after installation.

**Explanation as submitted:** The current NCDA rule limits spacing between grates to 13 mm; the other features remain separate checks.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [Revised 2024 Rules and Regulations Implementing Batas Pambansa Blg. 344](https://ncda.gov.ph/revised-2024-rules-and-regulations-implementing-batas-pambansa-344/) — National Council on Disability Affairs; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; Revised 2024 BP 344 IRR
  - Source-level relevant sections: Minimum accessibility requirements: ramps; floor surfaces; channel covers and floor protrusions; wiring/outlets; gratings; accessible routes
- [Program for the June 8 and 10, 2026 Licensure Examination for Architects](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/Program%20for%20June%202026%20LEA%28Architect%29_rev.pdf) — Professional Regulation Commission; Professional Regulatory Board of Architecture; OFFICIAL_PROGRAM; June 2026 Architecture examination program
  - Source-level relevant sections: Subject block: Utilities, Structural Conceptualization, Building Materials and Technology; PRC 2026 examination schedule
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** NCDA 2024 BP 344 IRR §§2–4.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Coordinate level, slip-resistant grating and flush service covers with controlled openings.

**Study lesson reference**

- Building Systems, Construction Coordination, and Life Safety — 6. Accessible floor and service details
- Material ID: architecture-building-systems-and-construction; section ID: 6-accessible-floor-and-service-details
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): The current NCDA rules require accessible floor surfaces to be level, stable, and slip-resistant in wet and dry conditions. Channel covers should be flush with the floor, with holes no greater than 13 mm; no projection or unexpected level change should create a hazard along the route. Gratings must be level and slip-resistant, and spacing between grates must not exceed 13 mm. Wiring, outlets, and similar elements must not create trip hazards and should be positioned away from accessible paths and corridors. Check finish performance, drainage, maintenance, and service routing together.

##### Concept: Services Away from Accessible Routes

###### Question 9126

- **Question ID:** 9126
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Architects / Architecture Utilities and Construction / Accessible Routes and Floor Details / Services Away from Accessible Routes

**Stem**

An electrical outlet and temporary cable are shown across a required accessible corridor. What is the most appropriate coordination action?

**Options**

A. Add a floor sign and retain the cable crossing as drawn.
B. Ask building users to lift the cable when passing.
C. Reroute the wiring and locate the outlet away from the accessible path, then verify the corridor remains free of trip hazards.
D. Keep the layout because the outlet is needed near the corridor.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Reroute the wiring and locate the outlet away from the accessible path, then verify the corridor remains free of trip hazards.

**Explanation as submitted:** The 2024 BP 344 IRR says wiring and outlets must not create trip hazards and should be positioned away from accessible paths and corridors.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [Revised 2024 Rules and Regulations Implementing Batas Pambansa Blg. 344](https://ncda.gov.ph/revised-2024-rules-and-regulations-implementing-batas-pambansa-344/) — National Council on Disability Affairs; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; Revised 2024 BP 344 IRR
  - Source-level relevant sections: Minimum accessibility requirements: ramps; floor surfaces; channel covers and floor protrusions; wiring/outlets; gratings; accessible routes
- [Program for the June 8 and 10, 2026 Licensure Examination for Architects](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/Program%20for%20June%202026%20LEA%28Architect%29_rev.pdf) — Professional Regulation Commission; Professional Regulatory Board of Architecture; OFFICIAL_PROGRAM; June 2026 Architecture examination program
  - Source-level relevant sections: Subject block: Utilities, Structural Conceptualization, Building Materials and Technology; PRC 2026 examination schedule
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** NCDA 2024 BP 344 IRR, §3.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Locate outlets and wiring to avoid trip hazards in accessible paths and corridors.

**Study lesson reference**

- Building Systems, Construction Coordination, and Life Safety — 6. Accessible floor and service details
- Material ID: architecture-building-systems-and-construction; section ID: 6-accessible-floor-and-service-details
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): The current NCDA rules require accessible floor surfaces to be level, stable, and slip-resistant in wet and dry conditions. Channel covers should be flush with the floor, with holes no greater than 13 mm; no projection or unexpected level change should create a hazard along the route. Gratings must be level and slip-resistant, and spacing between grates must not exceed 13 mm. Wiring, outlets, and similar elements must not create trip hazards and should be positioned away from accessible paths and corridors. Check finish performance, drainage, maintenance, and service routing together.

#### Topic: Utility Systems Integration

##### Concept: Utility Systems Coordination

###### Question 9127

- **Question ID:** 9127
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Architects / Architecture Utilities and Construction / Utility Systems Integration / Utility Systems Coordination

**Stem**

During design review, mechanical, electrical, sanitary, and plumbing routes conflict with a clear circulation path. Which response best reflects the architect’s scope and coordination role?

**Options**

A. Leave each system where drawn because only contractors resolve design conflicts.
B. Remove the circulation requirement to make room for every utility route.
C. Select a utility route without consulting the responsible specialist.
D. Coordinate a revised utility layout with the relevant specialists and verify that system performance and accessible circulation are preserved.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Coordinate a revised utility layout with the relevant specialists and verify that system performance and accessible circulation are preserved.

**Explanation as submitted:** RA 9266 includes utility system layouting in architecture services. Resolving interfaces requires coordination with the relevant technical professionals and applicable rules.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [Republic Act No. 9266, The Architecture Act of 2004](https://lawphil.net/statutes/repacts/ra2004/ra_9266_2004.html) — Republic of the Philippines; PRIMARY_LAW; Republic Act No. 9266
  - Source-level relevant sections: Sections 3 and 14; architecture practice scope and examination subject areas
- [Program for the June 8 and 10, 2026 Licensure Examination for Architects](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/Program%20for%20June%202026%20LEA%28Architect%29_rev.pdf) — Professional Regulation Commission; Professional Regulatory Board of Architecture; OFFICIAL_PROGRAM; June 2026 Architecture examination program
  - Source-level relevant sections: Subject block: Utilities, Structural Conceptualization, Building Materials and Technology; PRC 2026 examination schedule
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** RA 9266 §3; PRC Architecture exam scope.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Coordinate mechanical, electrical, electronic, sanitary, plumbing, and communication layouts within architectural practice.

**Study lesson reference**

- Building Systems, Construction Coordination, and Life Safety — 7. Integrated utilities and access coordination
- Material ID: architecture-building-systems-and-construction; section ID: 7-integrated-utilities-and-access-coordination
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Use overlay drawings and model reviews to see how services interact with structure, ceiling space, accessible routes, and equipment maintenance. A cable tray, floor outlet, or access hatch may be technically convenient but unsafe or obstructive in its proposed position. Resolve the system route with the relevant consultant and retain a clear, buildable access path. RA 9266 includes utilities layouting within architectural services, so architectural coordination is part of design responsibility; detailed engineering compliance remains subject to the relevant specialist and code.

## Board: Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET)

### Subject: Culture and Arts Education

#### Topic: Context, Identity, and Heritage

##### Concept: Cultural Context and Artistic Evidence

###### Question 9128

- **Question ID:** 9128
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Context, Identity, and Heritage / Cultural Context and Artistic Evidence

**Stem**

A class views a contemporary sculpture with repeated curved forms and open spaces. Which first response is grounded in artistic evidence?

**Options**

A. Describe the repeated curves and open spaces before proposing what the work may communicate.
B. State that the sculpture represents the whole community where it was displayed.
C. Assume the artist intended a specific message without consulting any source.
D. Rank the sculpture by how closely it resembles a familiar object.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Describe the repeated curves and open spaces before proposing what the work may communicate.

**Explanation as submitted:** Formal description identifies observable features; interpretation should be distinguished from unsupported claims about meaning.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Music and Arts Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/MATATAG-MUSIC-and-ARTS_CG-2023_GRADE-4-and-7.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Music and Arts Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 3–5, 8–15; learning area standards, identity and cultural diversity, creative work, performance, media and assessment
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex C; MATATAG Music & Arts pp. 3–5, 8–15.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Distinguish direct formal evidence from contextual interpretation of an artistic work.

**Study lesson reference**

- Arts Learning: Elements, Making, Context, and Reflection — 5. Context, identity, and interpretation
- Material ID: let-culture-arts-learning-foundation; section ID: 5-context-identity-and-interpretation
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): The MATATAG Music and Arts guide connects creation and interpretation to individual, community, regional, and Filipino identities, while also opening learning to wider artistic expressions. Treat this as an invitation to investigate context, not a formula that every work must represent a single identity. Compare a work’s features with reliable contextual evidence; distinguish artist intention, audience response, and your own interpretation. Acknowledge uncertainty where the available source does not establish a meaning.

###### Question 9129

- **Question ID:** 9129
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Context, Identity, and Heritage / Cultural Context and Artistic Evidence

**Stem**

Two learners interpret the same image differently and each points to details in the composition. What should the teacher ask?

**Options**

A. Which learner should replace their view with the teacher’s opinion.
B. How does each visible feature support the interpretation, and what context could help refine it?
C. Which interpretation sounds more confident regardless of evidence.
D. Whether the class can choose one meaning by majority vote.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. How does each visible feature support the interpretation, and what context could help refine it?

**Explanation as submitted:** More than one interpretation may be defensible when learners connect it to evidence and relevant context.

###### Question 9130

- **Question ID:** 9130
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Context, Identity, and Heritage / Cultural Context and Artistic Evidence

**Stem**

A student says an artwork must be joyful because it uses bright colors. What is the best critique prompt?

**Options**

A. Assume bright color signals happiness without considering composition or cultural context.
B. Which artist used the most colors in the image.
C. Which features, including color relationships and composition, support or complicate that reading?
D. Can the student replace the interpretation with a fixed definition.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Which features, including color relationships and composition, support or complicate that reading?

**Explanation as submitted:** The prompt encourages close looking and avoids assuming a single emotional meaning from one feature.

###### Question 9131

- **Question ID:** 9131
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Context, Identity, and Heritage / Cultural Context and Artistic Evidence

**Stem**

A teacher wants learners to discuss the social meaning of a song performance. Which preparation is most sound?

**Options**

A. Infer the performance’s full history from the clothing alone.
B. Treat one performer’s view as the only valid interpretation.
C. Avoid contextual research because music can only be judged by sound.
D. Use the performance as evidence and add reliable context about its source, purpose, and setting.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Use the performance as evidence and add reliable context about its source, purpose, and setting.

**Explanation as submitted:** The PRC scope includes art as social and cultural expression; context should be sourced and bounded.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9132

- **Question ID:** 9132
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Context, Identity, and Heritage / Cultural Context and Artistic Evidence

**Stem**

Learners compare two artworks from different periods. What should be the basis for a defensible comparison?

**Options**

A. Specific choices in each work together with reliable evidence about their contexts.
B. A claim that all artists in each period used the same style.
C. Which work is more popular with the class.
D. A list of dates without reference to the works themselves.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Specific choices in each work together with reliable evidence about their contexts.

**Explanation as submitted:** Comparison uses both features of the works and relevant contextual evidence without overgeneralizing.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9133

- **Question ID:** 9133
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Context, Identity, and Heritage / Cultural Context and Artistic Evidence

**Stem**

A source gives limited information about the maker and use of a community artwork. How should learners frame an interpretation?

**Options**

A. Fill in the missing history using a familiar stereotype.
B. State what can be observed, identify what the source confirms, and mark broader claims as unresolved.
C. Assume the artwork represents every member of the community.
D. Avoid describing the artwork because its context is incomplete.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. State what can be observed, identify what the source confirms, and mark broader claims as unresolved.

**Explanation as submitted:** Clear limits make an interpretation more accurate; missing context does not prevent careful observation.

##### Concept: Regional Identity and Artifact Study

###### Question 9134

- **Question ID:** 9134
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Context, Identity, and Heritage / Regional Identity and Artifact Study

**Stem**

A teacher displays one locally made craft and calls it the standard design of the entire region. What is the strongest revision?

**Options**

A. Keep the label because one local object is enough to define a region.
B. Remove all local works and use only international examples.
C. Identify its documented maker and context, then invite comparison with other sourced works rather than treating it as representative.
D. Ask learners to copy the design before discussing where it came from.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Identify its documented maker and context, then invite comparison with other sourced works rather than treating it as representative.

**Explanation as submitted:** One artifact cannot represent all makers or communities; contextual attribution and comparison avoid flattening diversity.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex C; MATATAG Music & Arts pp. 9–11.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Study specific artistic works with contextual evidence without generalizing one example to a whole community.

###### Question 9135

- **Question ID:** 9135
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Context, Identity, and Heritage / Regional Identity and Artifact Study

**Stem**

Learners compare two artworks associated with different local communities. Which task best develops cultural literacy?

**Options**

A. Choose one work as more authentically Filipino than the other.
B. Assume both communities use the same symbols because they are nearby.
C. Memorize the region names without studying the artworks.
D. Compare material, form, and documented context while noting differences within and across communities.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Compare material, form, and documented context while noting differences within and across communities.

**Explanation as submitted:** Specific comparison respects community distinctions and avoids ranking authenticity.

###### Question 9136

- **Question ID:** 9136
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Context, Identity, and Heritage / Regional Identity and Artifact Study

**Stem**

A learner says a regional art form has not changed over time. How can the teacher prompt evidence-based revision?

**Options**

A. Compare documented examples from different dates and ask what continuity and change are visible.
B. Treat one documented version as timeless and disregard evidence of later change.
C. Assume every contemporary version is less authentic.
D. Ask learners to choose which example is oldest by appearance alone.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Compare documented examples from different dates and ask what continuity and change are visible.

**Explanation as submitted:** The curriculum includes conventional, contemporary, emerging, and sustainable practices; evidence can show continuity and change.

###### Question 9137

- **Question ID:** 9137
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Context, Identity, and Heritage / Regional Identity and Artifact Study

**Stem**

A public museum label identifies an artwork’s maker and origin, while a social media caption gives a conflicting claim. What should learners do?

**Options**

A. Use the social media claim because it is more recent.
B. Compare the claims with reliable source information and state which details remain supported.
C. Combine both claims into one account without checking.

###### Question 9138

- **Question ID:** 9138
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Context, Identity, and Heritage / Regional Identity and Artifact Study

**Stem**

A student wants to present a community performance as a single uniform tradition. Which teaching prompt is most helpful?

**Options**

A. Why must one example speak for every community member.
B. Which costume best symbolizes the whole country.
C. What evidence shows who performs it, in what context, and whether the source describes variation?
D. Can the student replace the presentation with a national flag.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. What evidence shows who performs it, in what context, and whether the source describes variation?

**Explanation as submitted:** Specific questions about practice and context help preserve variation rather than flattening a tradition.

###### Question 9139

- **Question ID:** 9139
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Context, Identity, and Heritage / Regional Identity and Artifact Study

**Stem**

A class has time to study only one artwork from a diverse community. How can the teacher avoid implying that it is the only or definitive example?

**Options**

A. Present it as the complete account because the class has limited time.
B. Avoid naming its source so learners do not ask about other examples.
C. Ask learners to invent details to make the study feel complete.
D. Frame it as one documented work and explicitly note what additional sources or community perspectives would broaden the study.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Frame it as one documented work and explicitly note what additional sources or community perspectives would broaden the study.

**Explanation as submitted:** Transparent scope shows what the chosen work can teach and where further perspectives are needed.

#### Topic: Ethical Cultural Engagement

##### Concept: Ethical Community Engagement

###### Question 9140

- **Question ID:** 9140
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Ethical Cultural Engagement / Ethical Community Engagement

**Stem**

A teacher finds an online image of a community design with no creator or context listed. What is the best next step before using it as a model?

**Options**

A. Seek a reliable source and appropriate community context, or choose a documented alternative.
B. Use the image because public access means every use is appropriate.
C. Ask learners to copy the design before checking its source.
D. Describe it as traditional without identifying the community.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Seek a reliable source and appropriate community context, or choose a documented alternative.

**Explanation as submitted:** Public availability does not establish context or appropriate use; documented sourcing protects accurate and respectful teaching.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [Republic Act No. 8371: Indigenous Peoples’ Rights Act of 1997](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/2/2562) — Republic of the Philippines; Supreme Court E-Library; PRIMARY_LAW; Republic Act No. 8371
  - Source-level relevant sections: Sections 2 and 32; recognition of distinct identity; community intellectual rights and cultural practice
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex C; RA 8371 §§2, 32; MATATAG Music & Arts.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Seek appropriate knowledge, attribution, and respectful use when teaching living cultural traditions.

**Study lesson reference**

- Arts Learning: Elements, Making, Context, and Reflection — 6. Ethical cultural engagement
- Material ID: let-culture-arts-learning-foundation; section ID: 6-ethical-cultural-engagement
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): When a lesson draws on a living cultural practice, consult appropriate knowledge holders, respect the community’s description of the practice, identify attribution, and clarify what can be shared in a classroom. The rights of Indigenous communities include cultural and intellectual rights; respectful teaching requires more than copying a design or performance without context. Avoid inventing a generic “traditional” pattern. A learner may study a publicly documented example, but public visibility alone does not establish that every use is appropriate.

###### Question 9141

- **Question ID:** 9141
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Ethical Cultural Engagement / Ethical Community Engagement

**Stem**

A community arts educator tells the class that a particular practice should not be reproduced outside its intended context. What should the teacher do?

**Options**

A. Ask learners to reproduce the practice but avoid naming its source.
B. Respect that guidance and plan a different activity using material clearly shared for classroom learning.
C. Treat the guidance as one opinion and continue with the planned copy exercise.
D. Use a similar design from another community instead.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Respect that guidance and plan a different activity using material clearly shared for classroom learning.

**Explanation as submitted:** The RA 8371 recognizes cultural and intellectual rights; a respectful lesson follows the stated boundary rather than substituting another tradition.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

  - Source-level relevant sections: Sections 2 and 32; recognition of distinct identity; community intellectual rights and cultural practice
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex C; RA 8371 §§2, 32; MATATAG Music & Arts.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Seek appropriate knowledge, attribution, and respectful use when teaching living cultural traditions.

- Material ID: let-culture-arts-learning-foundation; section ID: 6-ethical-cultural-engagement
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): When a lesson draws on a living cultural practice, consult appropriate knowledge holders, respect the community’s description of the practice, identify attribution, and clarify what can be shared in a classroom. The rights of Indigenous communities include cultural and intellectual rights; respectful teaching requires more than copying a design or performance without context. Avoid inventing a generic “traditional” pattern. A learner may study a publicly documented example, but public visibility alone does not establish that every use is appropriate.

###### Question 9142

- **Question ID:** 9142
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Ethical Cultural Engagement / Ethical Community Engagement

**Stem**

Learners will create work inspired by a living tradition discussed with a community knowledge holder. What should the teacher establish?

**Options**

A. Assume one conversation authorizes every future classroom use.
B. Ask learners to imitate the form without explaining the source.
C. Clarify the learning purpose, attribution, what material may be shared, and how the community wishes to be represented.
D. Present the tradition as anonymous so the lesson remains neutral.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Clarify the learning purpose, attribution, what material may be shared, and how the community wishes to be represented.

**Explanation as submitted:** Ethical engagement requires attribution and attention to the source’s stated scope, not assumed blanket permission.

###### Question 9143

- **Question ID:** 9143
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Ethical Cultural Engagement / Ethical Community Engagement

**Stem**

A student’s artwork uses a motif copied from an online image. Which critique question supports ethical practice?

**Options**

A. How can the student hide the copied source from classmates.
B. Why should every online image be treated as free of attribution.
C. Which copied motif will earn the most points.
D. Who made the source image, what context is documented, and how will the student acknowledge or transform the source appropriately?

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Who made the source image, what context is documented, and how will the student acknowledge or transform the source appropriately?

**Explanation as submitted:** Attribution and context are part of responsible arts work; the teacher can guide original transformation.

###### Question 9144

- **Question ID:** 9144
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Ethical Cultural Engagement / Ethical Community Engagement

**Stem**

A lesson uses one artist to introduce a diverse cultural community. What addition best improves representation?

**Options**

A. Include additional sourced perspectives and let learners compare the artist’s individual view with broader evidence.
B. Describe the artist as speaking for everyone in the community.
C. Remove the artist’s name and present the work as generic.
D. Ask learners to decide what the entire community believes.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Include additional sourced perspectives and let learners compare the artist’s individual view with broader evidence.

**Explanation as submitted:** An artist’s work is an individual perspective; additional evidence prevents unsupported generalization.

###### Question 9145

- **Question ID:** 9145
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Ethical Cultural Engagement / Ethical Community Engagement

**Stem**

A student proposes a performance based on a sacred practice but cannot find a source showing it is intended for public performance. What is the most responsible response?

**Options**

A. Treat classroom use as permitted because the purpose is educational, despite the missing context.
B. Pause the plan, seek appropriate guidance, and choose a source explicitly intended for public performance.
C. Ask a public web forum whether the material is shared, then proceed if users agree.
D. Recreate the style from a different community’s similar practice instead.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Pause the plan, seek appropriate guidance, and choose a source explicitly intended for public performance.

**Explanation as submitted:** Uncertainty about use should be resolved with appropriate guidance; a different public source is a sound alternative. The replacement must itself be documented and suitable for classroom use.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

#### Topic: Making, Media, and Revision

##### Concept: Contemporary Media Literacy

###### Question 9176

- **Question ID:** 9176
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Making, Media, and Revision / Contemporary Media Literacy

**Stem**

A student combines found audio, a photograph, and a digital drawing in one composition. What should the class examine?

**Options**

A. How each medium contributes to meaning and how the sources are attributed.
B. Which medium is most modern and therefore most valuable.
C. Whether using several media removes the need for an artistic idea.
D. How to hide the sources so the composition feels original.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. How each medium contributes to meaning and how the sources are attributed.

**Explanation as submitted:** Multimedia work involves both meaning-making and responsible attribution.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Music and Arts Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/MATATAG-MUSIC-and-ARTS_CG-2023_GRADE-4-and-7.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Music and Arts Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 3–5, 8–15; learning area standards, identity and cultural diversity, creative work, performance, media and assessment
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex C; MATATAG Music & Arts pp. 3–5, 9–15.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Evaluate how medium, tools, audience, and context shape meaning in contemporary and emerging work.

**Study lesson reference**

- Arts Learning: Elements, Making, Context, and Reflection — 7. Studio process, media, and revision
- Material ID: let-culture-arts-learning-foundation; section ID: 7-studio-process-media-and-revision
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Make the relationship between idea and medium explicit. A learner designing a poster may test contrast and hierarchy; a sound composition may compare timbre and rhythm; a sculpture may explore balance and material response. Provide a safe, accessible technique demonstration and a chance to experiment. Ask learners to explain how a revision changes meaning or audience effect. Contemporary and emerging media can be studied alongside conventional forms without implying that new tools automatically make a work more meaningful.

###### Question 9177

- **Question ID:** 9177
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Making, Media, and Revision / Contemporary Media Literacy

**Stem**

A digital work uses an effect that draws attention away from the intended subject. What should the artist test?

**Options**

A. Add more effects until the subject becomes visible.
B. Compare the work with and without the effect and decide which version better serves the intended focus.
C. Keep the effect because digital work should appear complex.
D. Remove the subject and assess only the filter.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Compare the work with and without the effect and decide which version better serves the intended focus.

**Explanation as submitted:** The medium is a choice; comparison helps determine whether it supports the intended communication.

###### Question 9178

- **Question ID:** 9178
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Making, Media, and Revision / Contemporary Media Literacy

**Stem**

A class views a short online video artwork with no context about its creator. What is a responsible discussion opening?

**Options**

A. Assume the video is authentic because it appears on an arts channel.
B. Infer the creator’s identity from style and present the guess as fact.
C. Describe the work, check its source information, and separate observation from unverified interpretation.
D. Discuss an intended meaning as established even though no source identifies the maker.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Describe the work, check its source information, and separate observation from unverified interpretation.

**Explanation as submitted:** Media literacy includes source awareness and careful separation of observation from inference. Learners can discuss visible and audible features while marking any unsupported context as unknown.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9179

- **Question ID:** 9179
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Making, Media, and Revision / Contemporary Media Literacy

**Stem**

A learner wants to remix a publicly posted image into a new artwork. What should the teacher ask first?

**Options**

A. How can the learner remove the creator’s name from the file.
B. Whether public posting means attribution is unnecessary.
C. How to reproduce the image exactly before adding a title.
D. What is the source and permitted context, how will it be attributed, and how will the learner’s own contribution change its meaning?

**Proposed correct answer:** D. What is the source and permitted context, how will it be attributed, and how will the learner’s own contribution change its meaning?

**Explanation as submitted:** The question prompts source attribution and original transformation without assuming every reuse is permitted.

##### Concept: Iterative Making and Revision

###### Question 9152

- **Question ID:** 9152
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Making, Media, and Revision / Iterative Making and Revision

**Stem**

A learner makes a small color study before painting a full poster. What purpose does the study serve?

**Options**

A. It lets the learner test color relationships and revise a choice before committing more materials.
B. It proves the learner cannot create without a model.
C. It replaces the need to explain artistic decisions.
D. It is a final score that ranks the learner against peers.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. It lets the learner test color relationships and revise a choice before committing more materials.

**Explanation as submitted:** A small test is part of experimentation and can guide informed revision.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9153

- **Question ID:** 9153
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Making, Media, and Revision / Iterative Making and Revision

**Stem**

A student says a first sketch must remain unchanged because revision means the idea was wrong. What should the teacher explain?

**Options**

A. Revision is required only when the teacher dislikes the result.
B. Revision can refine an idea after the learner sees how materials, audience, or composition affect it.
C. Tell the learner to keep the first sketch unless the teacher requests a change.
D. Revision means copying a professional example exactly.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Revision can refine an idea after the learner sees how materials, audience, or composition affect it.

**Explanation as submitted:** Revision is a reasoned artistic decision, not a judgment that the initial idea had no value.

###### Question 9154

- **Question ID:** 9154
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Making, Media, and Revision / Iterative Making and Revision

**Stem**

A sculpture repeatedly tips over. What is the most informative next experiment?

**Options**

A. Change every feature at once so the result is surprising.
B. Add decoration to cover the instability.
C. Change one structural feature, test the balance, and compare the result with the original.
D. Ask another learner to rebuild it without explaining the issue.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Change one structural feature, test the balance, and compare the result with the original.

**Explanation as submitted:** Changing one feature at a time helps the learner understand how the material and structure affect balance.

###### Question 9155

- **Question ID:** 9155
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Making, Media, and Revision / Iterative Making and Revision

**Stem**

A group receives critique that its performance rhythm is difficult to follow. Which revision plan is best?

**Options**

A. Replace the entire performance without discussing the feedback.
B. Dismiss the listeners because the group already rehearsed.
C. Increase volume without checking rhythm or transitions.
D. Identify the section causing confusion, test a clearer transition, and ask listeners whether the sequence is easier to hear.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Identify the section causing confusion, test a clearer transition, and ask listeners whether the sequence is easier to hear.

**Explanation as submitted:** A targeted test responds to evidence and lets the group evaluate whether the revision improves communication.

###### Question 9156

- **Question ID:** 9156
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Making, Media, and Revision / Iterative Making and Revision

**Stem**

A learner changes a collage after noticing that the focal image is hidden by text. Which explanation shows reflective practice?

**Options**

A. The text competed with the focal image, so I moved it to make the main idea easier to see.
B. I changed it because the teacher said my original was wrong.
C. I moved the text because everyone else used a different layout.
D. I made a change but did not look at the image again.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. The text competed with the focal image, so I moved it to make the main idea easier to see.

**Explanation as submitted:** The learner connects a formal choice with audience effect and explains the reason for revision.

###### Question 9157

- **Question ID:** 9157
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Making, Media, and Revision / Iterative Making and Revision

**Stem**

A group has conflicting feedback: some viewers find a dance sequence clear, others do not. What is the strongest next step?

**Options**

A. Accept only the feedback that supports the group’s first idea.
B. Ask which moments viewers found unclear, compare their observations with the choreography, and test a focused revision.
C. Change the whole sequence based on a majority vote.
D. Conclude that viewers should have understood without further evidence.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Ask which moments viewers found unclear, compare their observations with the choreography, and test a focused revision.

**Explanation as submitted:** Specific feedback helps locate the issue while preserving the group’s intention and supporting a testable revision.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

##### Concept: Material and Medium Selection

###### Question 9146

- **Question ID:** 9146
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Making, Media, and Revision / Material and Medium Selection

**Stem**

Learners want to build a stable sculpture with lightweight recycled materials. Which planning step comes first?

**Options**

A. Choose a display location before checking whether the sculpture stands.
B. Require every learner to use the same material regardless of the design.
C. Test how the available materials join and balance before selecting the final construction method.
D. Assess only the sculpture’s color palette.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Test how the available materials join and balance before selecting the final construction method.

**Explanation as submitted:** Material behavior affects stability and technique, so an early test supports an intentional process.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex C; MATATAG Music & Arts pp. 3–5, 13–15.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Match medium and process to intended meaning, available resources, material properties, and safe use.

###### Question 9147

- **Question ID:** 9147
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Making, Media, and Revision / Material and Medium Selection

**Stem**

A learner wants to communicate movement in a still image. Which medium decision is most relevant?

**Options**

A. Use the most expensive available material.
B. Select a medium only because it is familiar to the teacher.
C. Add unrelated decoration until the image looks complete.
D. Choose compositional, line, or repeated-shape techniques that can suggest motion in the selected medium.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Choose compositional, line, or repeated-shape techniques that can suggest motion in the selected medium.

**Explanation as submitted:** A medium and technique should serve the intended effect. The learner can test how line direction, repeated forms, and composition imply movement, then choose the treatment that makes the idea clearest.

###### Question 9148

- **Question ID:** 9148
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Making, Media, and Revision / Material and Medium Selection

**Stem**

A class plans a sound collage but has limited recording equipment. What is a sound design response?

**Options**

A. Choose available safe sound sources and plan how their sequence and contrast will communicate the idea.
B. Cancel sound creation because professional equipment is unavailable.
C. Record any sounds without considering the intended message.
D. Use a prerecorded song and claim it as the class’s original work.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Choose available safe sound sources and plan how their sequence and contrast will communicate the idea.

**Explanation as submitted:** Creative choices can use accessible materials while preserving intention, attribution, and safe practice.

###### Question 9149

- **Question ID:** 9149
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Making, Media, and Revision / Material and Medium Selection

**Stem**

A learner’s clay form cracks during drying. Which teacher response best supports material understanding?

**Options**

A. Tell the learner the design failed and must be discarded.
B. Review thickness, joins, and drying conditions, then invite a small test before revising the final form.
C. Change the project to painting without examining the clay.
D. Ask the learner to hide the crack before reflection.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Review thickness, joins, and drying conditions, then invite a small test before revising the final form.

**Explanation as submitted:** The material response provides evidence for an informed revision and future process choice.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Music and Arts Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/MATATAG-MUSIC-and-ARTS_CG-2023_GRADE-4-and-7.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Music and Arts Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 3–5, 8–15; learning area standards, identity and cultural diversity, creative work, performance, media and assessment
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex C; MATATAG Music & Arts pp. 3–5, 13–15.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Match medium and process to intended meaning, available resources, material properties, and safe use.

**Study lesson reference**

- Arts Learning: Elements, Making, Context, and Reflection — 7. Studio process, media, and revision
- Material ID: let-culture-arts-learning-foundation; section ID: 7-studio-process-media-and-revision
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Make the relationship between idea and medium explicit. A learner designing a poster may test contrast and hierarchy; a sound composition may compare timbre and rhythm; a sculpture may explore balance and material response. Provide a safe, accessible technique demonstration and a chance to experiment. Ask learners to explain how a revision changes meaning or audience effect. Contemporary and emerging media can be studied alongside conventional forms without implying that new tools automatically make a work more meaningful.

###### Question 9150

- **Question ID:** 9150
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Making, Media, and Revision / Material and Medium Selection

**Stem**

A digital illustration uses a tool that produces an unintended flat effect. What should the learner try?

**Options**

A. Keep the first setting because digital tools cannot be revised.
B. Ask the teacher to select every digital setting.
C. Test another tool or layer treatment and compare how each changes depth and meaning.
D. Add more effects without comparing their impact.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Test another tool or layer treatment and compare how each changes depth and meaning.

**Explanation as submitted:** Experimenting and comparing tools helps the learner make deliberate choices.

###### Question 9151

- **Question ID:** 9151
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Making, Media, and Revision / Material and Medium Selection

**Stem**

A large group must create a performance with a small accessible stage. Which medium and process decision is strongest?

**Options**

A. Keep the original staging and ask performers to squeeze into the available space.
B. Remove performers who need more space without adapting the design.
C. Replace the performance with a written report.
D. Plan movement scale, pathways, sound, and roles around the space while preserving the intended expressive idea.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Plan movement scale, pathways, sound, and roles around the space while preserving the intended expressive idea.

**Explanation as submitted:** Medium and staging choices should fit site conditions and access while retaining the creative goal.

#### Topic: Performance, Critique, and Assessment

##### Concept: Accessible Arts Learning

###### Question 9170

- **Question ID:** 9170
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Performance, Critique, and Assessment / Accessible Arts Learning

**Stem**

A lesson asks learners to analyze shape and balance, but the only response option is a written paragraph. Which addition improves access?

**Options**

A. Remove shape analysis for learners who choose a different response.
B. Require the paragraph before accepting any evidence.
C. Allow an annotated drawing or spoken explanation that identifies the same shape and balance evidence.
D. Ask a peer to complete the response on the learner’s behalf.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Allow an annotated drawing or spoken explanation that identifies the same shape and balance evidence.

**Explanation as submitted:** Alternative response modes can preserve the visual-analysis competency when writing is not the target.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Music and Arts Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/MATATAG-MUSIC-and-ARTS_CG-2023_GRADE-4-and-7.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Music and Arts Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 3–5, 8–15; learning area standards, identity and cultural diversity, creative work, performance, media and assessment
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex C; MATATAG Music & Arts pp. 13–15.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Offer accessible ways to experience, create, and respond while preserving the artistic learning aim.

**Study lesson reference**

- Arts Learning: Elements, Making, Context, and Reflection — 9. Critique, assessment, and access
- Material ID: let-culture-arts-learning-foundation; section ID: 9-critique-assessment-and-access
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Align assessment with the learning target. A rubric may address technique, composition, process decisions, cultural/contextual evidence, collaboration, and reflection when these are taught. Use portfolio pieces, observation, performance, written or spoken analysis, and self-reflection as appropriate. Give formative feedback early enough for revision. Offer accessible response modes where language or motor performance is not the artistic target; maintain the requirement when the target is specifically that mode.

###### Question 9171

- **Question ID:** 9171
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Performance, Critique, and Assessment / Accessible Arts Learning

**Stem**

A classroom display is too high for some learners to examine closely. What should the teacher change?

**Options**

A. Ask learners to rely on classmates’ descriptions only.
B. Remove the display from the lesson.
C. Assess learners on memory of the image rather than observation.
D. Provide the images at an accessible height or offer a close-viewing copy while keeping the same analysis task.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Provide the images at an accessible height or offer a close-viewing copy while keeping the same analysis task.

**Explanation as submitted:** Accessible presentation lets learners use the artwork as evidence for the shared task.

###### Question 9172

- **Question ID:** 9172
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Performance, Critique, and Assessment / Accessible Arts Learning

**Stem**

A learner cannot use a standard brush comfortably during a painting task. What is a fair next step?

**Options**

A. Offer an adapted tool or another application method and assess the intended color or composition choices.
B. Complete the painting for the learner.
C. Replace the arts objective with a written summary.
D. Score the learner lower for using a different tool.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Offer an adapted tool or another application method and assess the intended color or composition choices.

**Explanation as submitted:** An adapted tool can provide access while preserving the intended artistic evidence.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9173

- **Question ID:** 9173
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Performance, Critique, and Assessment / Accessible Arts Learning

**Stem**

A music activity asks learners to identify repeating rhythm, but the class relies only on a sound that one learner cannot access. What should the teacher add?

**Options**

A. Remove the rhythm goal for the learner.
B. Provide an accessible visual or tactile representation of the rhythm while maintaining the pattern-identification task.
C. Ask a peer to identify the pattern for the learner.
D. Use a faster sound so the pattern is easier to hear.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Provide an accessible visual or tactile representation of the rhythm while maintaining the pattern-identification task.

**Explanation as submitted:** A second representation can support access without changing the rhythm pattern being assessed.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9174

- **Question ID:** 9174
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Performance, Critique, and Assessment / Accessible Arts Learning

**Stem**

A group critique requires spontaneous public speaking, though the goal is to give evidence-based feedback. Which option supports access?

**Options**

A. Remove the feedback goal for learners who prefer not to speak.
B. Require unprepared speaking because it is the usual format.
C. Allow prepared written, recorded, or partner-shared feedback that names evidence and a next step.
D. Ask the teacher to give all feedback instead.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Allow prepared written, recorded, or partner-shared feedback that names evidence and a next step.

**Explanation as submitted:** The mode of response is separate from the feedback competency. The teacher can keep the same evidence and next-step criteria while offering a format that lets each learner participate.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9175

- **Question ID:** 9175
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Performance, Critique, and Assessment / Accessible Arts Learning

**Stem**

A teacher adapts a dance sequence for a learner who uses a mobility device. What should guide the adaptation?

**Options**

A. Require the learner to copy every standing movement.
B. Assign the learner a nonparticipating observer role by default.
C. Change the learning goal to equipment maintenance.
D. Ask the learner, preserve the intended rhythm or movement concept, and assess meaningful contribution in an accessible form.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Ask the learner, preserve the intended rhythm or movement concept, and assess meaningful contribution in an accessible form.

**Explanation as submitted:** Adaptation should reflect learner input and preserve the artistic movement aim while enabling participation.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

##### Concept: Authentic Arts Assessment

###### Question 9164

- **Question ID:** 9164
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Performance, Critique, and Assessment / Authentic Arts Assessment

**Stem**

A learner’s objective is to explain how texture changes a visual composition. Which assessment is best aligned?

**Options**

A. Ask the learner to identify a texture choice and explain its effect using the artwork as evidence.
B. Score the learner on how neatly the art table was cleaned.
C. Ask the learner to name every artist in the textbook.
D. Rank the artwork by whether the teacher would display it.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Ask the learner to identify a texture choice and explain its effect using the artwork as evidence.

**Explanation as submitted:** The assessment directly elicits the stated understanding of texture and effect.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Music and Arts Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/MATATAG-MUSIC-and-ARTS_CG-2023_GRADE-4-and-7.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Music and Arts Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 3–5, 8–15; learning area standards, identity and cultural diversity, creative work, performance, media and assessment
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex C; MATATAG Music & Arts pp. 5, 8, 13–15.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Align assessment criteria with artistic process, technique, intention, presentation, and reflection.

**Study lesson reference**

- Arts Learning: Elements, Making, Context, and Reflection — 9. Critique, assessment, and access
- Material ID: let-culture-arts-learning-foundation; section ID: 9-critique-assessment-and-access
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Align assessment with the learning target. A rubric may address technique, composition, process decisions, cultural/contextual evidence, collaboration, and reflection when these are taught. Use portfolio pieces, observation, performance, written or spoken analysis, and self-reflection as appropriate. Give formative feedback early enough for revision. Offer accessible response modes where language or motor performance is not the artistic target; maintain the requirement when the target is specifically that mode.

###### Question 9165

- **Question ID:** 9165
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Performance, Critique, and Assessment / Authentic Arts Assessment

**Stem**

A portfolio shows several drafts and a final composition. What question best assesses the learner’s process?

**Options**

A. Which draft looks most like the teacher’s sample.
B. Which revision most changed the composition, and what evidence led to that decision?
C. How many pages are in the portfolio.
D. Which learner finished first.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Which revision most changed the composition, and what evidence led to that decision?

**Explanation as submitted:** Process evidence supports reflection on artistic decisions and development.

###### Question 9166

- **Question ID:** 9166
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Performance, Critique, and Assessment / Authentic Arts Assessment

**Stem**

A dance rubric includes “stage presence” but the taught objective is rhythmic sequence. What should the teacher do?

**Options**

A. Keep stage presence as the highest-weight criterion because it is visible.
B. Remove all criteria and assign one overall impression.
C. Score rhythm and sequence as the main criteria and include presence only if it was taught as an objective.
D. Add a costume score to balance the rubric.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Score rhythm and sequence as the main criteria and include presence only if it was taught as an objective.

**Explanation as submitted:** The criteria should reflect taught outcomes rather than unrelated presentation preferences.

###### Question 9167

- **Question ID:** 9167
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Performance, Critique, and Assessment / Authentic Arts Assessment

**Stem**

A learner can explain a design decision but the final artwork has an unfinished edge. How can assessment remain informative?

**Options**

A. Use the unfinished edge to disregard all process evidence.
B. Give full credit because the learner explains the idea confidently.
C. Score the learner only against classmates’ finished work.
D. Use separate criteria for the artistic reasoning and the required technical finish.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Use separate criteria for the artistic reasoning and the required technical finish.

**Explanation as submitted:** Distinct criteria make reasoning and technical execution visible without letting one erase the other.

###### Question 9168

- **Question ID:** 9168
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Performance, Critique, and Assessment / Authentic Arts Assessment

**Stem**

A teacher gives critique only after a final performance, leaving no chance to apply feedback. What would improve the assessment cycle?

**Options**

A. Include a formative rehearsal check with one criterion-linked next step before the final performance.
B. Keep critique until all performances are complete.
C. Replace performance with a written quiz.
D. Give feedback only to learners who ask publicly.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Include a formative rehearsal check with one criterion-linked next step before the final performance.

**Explanation as submitted:** Formative feedback can guide improvement while learning is in progress.

###### Question 9169

- **Question ID:** 9169
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Performance, Critique, and Assessment / Authentic Arts Assessment

**Stem**

A group project is assessed with one shared grade, although members contributed in different ways. What is the strongest assessment design?

**Options**

A. Give every member the same score based only on the final display.
B. Assess shared collaboration criteria and each learner’s individual evidence of the stated artistic goals.
C. Grade the group by the most skilled member’s performance.
D. Replace the project with individual copying tasks.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Assess shared collaboration criteria and each learner’s individual evidence of the stated artistic goals.

**Explanation as submitted:** A combined view can recognize group creation and individual learning evidence.
