##### Concept: Performing Arts Interpretation

###### Question 9158

- **Question ID:** 9158
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Performance, Critique, and Assessment / Performing Arts Interpretation

**Stem**

A music group varies dynamics in a repeated section. What question best supports interpretation?

**Options**

A. Which performer made the fewest mistakes during rehearsal.
B. Why should every repeated section use the same volume.
C. What expressive effect did the change in volume create, and what musical evidence supports that choice?
D. Which instrument is most popular with the audience.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. What expressive effect did the change in volume create, and what musical evidence supports that choice?

**Explanation as submitted:** The question connects a performance choice to an intended effect and audible evidence.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Music and Arts Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/MATATAG-MUSIC-and-ARTS_CG-2023_GRADE-4-and-7.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Music and Arts Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 3–5, 8–15; learning area standards, identity and cultural diversity, creative work, performance, media and assessment
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex C; MATATAG Music & Arts pp. 4–5, 9–13.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Use musical, movement, and contextual evidence to make and explain performance choices.

**Study lesson reference**

- Arts Learning: Elements, Making, Context, and Reflection — 8. Performance and collaborative creation
- Material ID: let-culture-arts-learning-foundation; section ID: 8-performance-and-collaborative-creation
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Music, dance, drama, and interdisciplinary performance require attention to structure, timing, space, relationship, role, and expression. A performer may adapt dynamics, pacing, or movement quality to communicate an interpretation; the teacher should ask for evidence behind that choice. Collaborative creation needs clear roles and shared decision-making, with room for learners to contribute in different capacities. Rehearsal is part of the learning process, not merely preparation for a showcase.

###### Question 9159

- **Question ID:** 9159
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Performance, Critique, and Assessment / Performing Arts Interpretation

**Stem**

A dance group changes formation during a phrase. Which reflection prompt connects movement and meaning?

**Options**

A. Which learner stood closest to the teacher.
B. Why must the formation match a single traditional pattern.
C. How many steps were in the phrase, without discussing its effect.
D. How did the change in spacing alter the relationships or focus visible to the audience?

**Proposed correct answer:** D. How did the change in spacing alter the relationships or focus visible to the audience?

**Explanation as submitted:** Space and relationships are movement choices that can shape interpretation.

###### Question 9160

- **Question ID:** 9160
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Performance, Critique, and Assessment / Performing Arts Interpretation

**Stem**

A drama group wants a scene to show hesitation without adding dialogue. Which performance choice is most relevant?

**Options**

A. Use a deliberate pause or movement change and discuss how the audience may read it.
B. Add a costume from an unrelated period.
C. Increase speaking speed during the silent moment.
D. Ask the audience to guess without showing a performance choice.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Use a deliberate pause or movement change and discuss how the audience may read it.

**Explanation as submitted:** Timing and movement can communicate meaning, and the group can test the audience effect.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9161

- **Question ID:** 9161
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Performance, Critique, and Assessment / Performing Arts Interpretation

**Stem**

A class is arranging a short musical sequence using sounds contributed by learners. What supports collaboration?

**Options**

A. Select one learner’s sound and exclude all others.
B. Agree on a shared structure and invite learners to explain how their sound fits the group’s idea.
C. Have each learner perform separately without listening.
D. Choose sounds randomly and avoid rehearsal.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Agree on a shared structure and invite learners to explain how their sound fits the group’s idea.

**Explanation as submitted:** A shared structure supports individual contribution and coherent collaboration.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Music and Arts Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/MATATAG-MUSIC-and-ARTS_CG-2023_GRADE-4-and-7.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Music and Arts Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 3–5, 8–15; learning area standards, identity and cultural diversity, creative work, performance, media and assessment
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex C; MATATAG Music & Arts pp. 4–5, 9–13.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Use musical, movement, and contextual evidence to make and explain performance choices.

**Study lesson reference**

- Arts Learning: Elements, Making, Context, and Reflection — 8. Performance and collaborative creation
- Material ID: let-culture-arts-learning-foundation; section ID: 8-performance-and-collaborative-creation
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Music, dance, drama, and interdisciplinary performance require attention to structure, timing, space, relationship, role, and expression. A performer may adapt dynamics, pacing, or movement quality to communicate an interpretation; the teacher should ask for evidence behind that choice. Collaborative creation needs clear roles and shared decision-making, with room for learners to contribute in different capacities. Rehearsal is part of the learning process, not merely preparation for a showcase.

###### Question 9162

- **Question ID:** 9162
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Performance, Critique, and Assessment / Performing Arts Interpretation

**Stem**

A performer asks to change a movement because the original gesture is uncomfortable and not essential to the learning goal. What should the teacher consider?

**Options**

A. Require the gesture because all performers must move identically.
B. Remove the learner from the performance.
C. Adapt the movement safely while discussing how the change can retain the intended expressive quality.
D. Change the whole performance without considering the learning intention.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Adapt the movement safely while discussing how the change can retain the intended expressive quality.

**Explanation as submitted:** An accessible adaptation can preserve the artistic intention while supporting participation.

###### Question 9163

- **Question ID:** 9163
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Culture and Arts Education / Performance, Critique, and Assessment / Performing Arts Interpretation

**Stem**

An audience interprets a performance differently from the creators’ stated intention. How should the teacher guide reflection?

**Options**

A. Tell the audience its interpretation is wrong because it differs from intention.
B. Ignore the audience and assess only the creators’ explanation.
C. Change the performance immediately to please every viewer.
D. Compare the creators’ intended message with audience observations and identify which performance features shaped each reading.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Compare the creators’ intended message with audience observations and identify which performance features shaped each reading.

**Explanation as submitted:** Reflection can examine the relationship among intent, performance evidence, and audience response.

### Subject: Early Childhood Education

#### Topic: Development and Learning

##### Concept: Guided Play Scaffolding

###### Question 9204

- **Question ID:** 9204
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Development and Learning / Guided Play Scaffolding

**Stem**

Children build a pretend bus station with blocks. Which teacher action best extends the play without taking it over?

**Options**

A. Ask where passengers will wait and offer a few picture route cards for children to use if they choose.
B. Redesign the station while children watch so it matches the teacher’s model.
C. Pause the play and require a worksheet about buses.
D. Assign each child a fixed role before asking what they want to build.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Ask where passengers will wait and offer a few picture route cards for children to use if they choose.

**Explanation as submitted:** An open question and optional material extend thinking while preserving child choice and play direction.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Kindergarten Curriculum Guide](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/MATATAG-Kindergarten-CG.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Kindergarten Curriculum Guide
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 2–9, 13, 16, 30; developmental domains, play-based approach, literacy/numeracy, assessment and family communication
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** DepEd KCG pp. 5, 13, 30; PRC Annex A.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Extend child-initiated play with timely prompts and materials while preserving agency.

**Study lesson reference**

- Early Childhood Learning, Assessment, and Inclusive Practice — 2. Play and guided learning
- Material ID: let-early-childhood-learning-and-inclusion; section ID: 2-play-and-guided-learning
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): The MATATAG Kindergarten Curriculum describes play as a central approach and combines child-initiated activity with teacher support. In guided play, children retain meaningful choice; the teacher watches for a learning opportunity, introduces a useful word, asks an open question, models a strategy, or changes a material. A prompt should extend the child’s thinking rather than turn play into a disguised worksheet. Observe the response and reduce or increase support accordingly.

###### Question 9205

- **Question ID:** 9205
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Development and Learning / Guided Play Scaffolding

**Stem**

A child repeatedly rolls a ball down a ramp and watches how far it travels. What is a useful guided-play prompt?

**Options**

A. Tell the child the correct result and end the exploration.
B. Ask what might make the ball travel farther and invite the child to change one ramp feature to test the idea.
C. Add several new objects at once so the child cannot compare conditions.
D. Require the child to copy a written prediction before touching the ramp.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Ask what might make the ball travel farther and invite the child to change one ramp feature to test the idea.

**Explanation as submitted:** A single-variable change supports prediction and inquiry while the child remains active in play.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9206

- **Question ID:** 9206
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Development and Learning / Guided Play Scaffolding

**Stem**

Several children use toy animals to create a rescue story but do not include a child who wants to join. What is the teacher’s best intervention?

**Options**

A. Assign the child the lead role without asking and require the others to agree.
B. Stop the story and move the group to a different activity.
C. Offer an open invitation to add a role or resource to the story and help the group consider how it could fit.
D. Tell the child to wait until the group finishes its play.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Offer an open invitation to add a role or resource to the story and help the group consider how it could fit.

**Explanation as submitted:** A flexible invitation supports entry and peer planning without taking control of the children’s story.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9207

- **Question ID:** 9207
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Development and Learning / Guided Play Scaffolding

**Stem**

A child is sorting pretend food by color. Which prompt encourages deeper thinking while respecting the play?

**Options**

A. Tell the child to sort only by the categories printed on a worksheet.
B. Move the foods into the teacher’s preferred groups without discussion.
C. Ask the child to count every item aloud before any further choice.
D. Ask whether the child can find another way to group the foods and explain the new rule.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Ask whether the child can find another way to group the foods and explain the new rule.

**Explanation as submitted:** The prompt expands classification and explanation while allowing the child’s own direction.

###### Question 9208

- **Question ID:** 9208
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Development and Learning / Guided Play Scaffolding

**Stem**

A teacher has modeled how to attach a cardboard roof, and a child now completes the steps without help. What should happen next?

**Options**

A. Reduce prompting and observe whether the child can adapt the construction when a new material is offered.
B. Continue giving the same instruction before every action.
C. End all construction opportunities because the child has finished the model.
D. Ask another learner to complete the next step for the child.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Reduce prompting and observe whether the child can adapt the construction when a new material is offered.

**Explanation as submitted:** Fading support and testing transfer help the teacher see whether the child can apply the idea independently.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9209

- **Question ID:** 9209
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Development and Learning / Guided Play Scaffolding

**Stem**

A play center has become quiet and repetitive, but several children still engage with the same props. What is the most defensible teacher move?

**Options**

A. Replace all the materials immediately with a teacher-designed task.
B. Observe what the children are trying to do, then introduce one relevant prop or question that opens a new possibility.
C. Assume repeated play has no learning value and close the center.
D. Ask children to complete a separate activity because the center is quiet.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Observe what the children are trying to do, then introduce one relevant prop or question that opens a new possibility.

**Explanation as submitted:** The teacher should use observation to decide whether a small extension would deepen the children’s current interest.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

#### Topic: Family, Culture, and Language

##### Concept: Home-Language Partnership

###### Question 9210

- **Question ID:** 9210
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Family, Culture, and Language / Home-Language Partnership

**Stem**

A caregiver shares that a child enjoys counting market items in a home language. How can the teacher use this knowledge?

**Options**

A. Ask the caregiver to stop the home counting routine during the school year.
B. Use the information to assign the child a lower number task.
C. Invite the child to count familiar objects in that language and connect the quantity to classroom number words.
D. Assume the child can count every classroom object in the same way without checking.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Invite the child to count familiar objects in that language and connect the quantity to classroom number words.

**Explanation as submitted:** Family knowledge offers a bridge to the concept; the teacher still observes how the child uses it in the classroom.

###### Question 9211

- **Question ID:** 9211
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Family, Culture, and Language / Home-Language Partnership

**Stem**

A caregiver cannot attend an in-person classroom event but wants to share the child’s favorite story. What is a respectful partnership response?

**Options**

A. Record the caregiver as uninvolved because the event was missed.
B. Require the caregiver to attend another event before using the story.
C. Use a story found online and tell the child it came from home.
D. Offer a flexible way to share the story or its key details and invite the caregiver to choose what is workable.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Offer a flexible way to share the story or its key details and invite the caregiver to choose what is workable.

**Explanation as submitted:** Partnership should allow realistic two-way communication rather than making one event the test of family engagement.

###### Question 9212

- **Question ID:** 9212
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Family, Culture, and Language / Home-Language Partnership

**Stem**

A child uses a familiar household routine to explain a sequencing activity. What should the teacher do with that contribution?

**Options**

A. Ask the child to describe the routine, connect it to before-and-after language, and invite classmates to compare sequences.
B. Replace the routine with a school example because home experiences are private.
C. Assume all families follow the same routine and teach it as universal.
D. Correct the child’s sequence before learning how the routine works.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Ask the child to describe the routine, connect it to before-and-after language, and invite classmates to compare sequences.

**Explanation as submitted:** A familiar routine can support temporal language while the teacher avoids assuming it represents every family.

###### Question 9213

- **Question ID:** 9213
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Family, Culture, and Language / Home-Language Partnership

**Stem**

A family describes a child’s interest in boat travel, while the teacher is planning a unit on local weather. Which plan uses the information well?

**Options**

A. Change the whole unit to boat travel without considering its learning goals.
B. Invite the family’s optional examples and use them to develop a weather observation question relevant to the child.
C. Ignore the interest because families should not influence classroom planning.
D. Ask the child to bring expensive materials from home for the class.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Invite the family’s optional examples and use them to develop a weather observation question relevant to the child.

**Explanation as submitted:** Family knowledge can make learning relevant while the teacher maintains curricular goals and avoids burdening the family.

###### Question 9214

- **Question ID:** 9214
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Family, Culture, and Language / Home-Language Partnership

**Stem**

A family reports that a child communicates more confidently through gestures at home. What is the best teacher response?

**Options**

A. Tell the family the child must stop gesturing to build speech.
B. Use gestures at home as the sole communication plan without checking how they work in other settings.
C. Ask which gestures are meaningful, observe how the child communicates in class, and plan consistent ways to recognize messages.
D. Ask peers to interpret every gesture without checking with the child.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Ask which gestures are meaningful, observe how the child communicates in class, and plan consistent ways to recognize messages.

**Explanation as submitted:** The family’s insight can inform observation and planning; the teacher should confirm meaning with the child and family.

###### Question 9215

- **Question ID:** 9215
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Family, Culture, and Language / Home-Language Partnership

**Stem**

A child’s family shares a personal routine that could enrich a class discussion, but the child is uncomfortable when it is mentioned publicly. What should the teacher do?

**Options**

A. Share the routine anyway because the family first offered it.
B. Ask classmates to persuade the child to discuss the routine.
C. Remove the learning goal because the child declined to share personal information.
D. Respect the child’s response, avoid sharing private details, and use another classroom context for the same learning goal.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Respect the child’s response, avoid sharing private details, and use another classroom context for the same learning goal.

**Explanation as submitted:** Family contribution does not override the child’s dignity and privacy; the learning goal can be met through another context.

#### Topic: Language, Literacy, and Everyday Numeracy

##### Concept: Dialogic Early Literacy

###### Question 9192

- **Question ID:** 9192
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Language, Literacy, and Everyday Numeracy / Dialogic Early Literacy

**Stem**

During a shared picture book, a child points to a character’s empty bowl. Which prompt best invites meaning-making?

**Options**

A. Ask what the picture suggests the character may do next and what clue supports the idea.
B. Ask the child to repeat the title until it is memorized.
C. Name the next event yourself and ask the child to echo it.
D. Ask the child to identify every printed letter before discussing the picture.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Ask what the picture suggests the character may do next and what clue supports the idea.

**Explanation as submitted:** An open prediction tied to a visual clue invites comprehension, language, and evidence-based thinking.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Kindergarten Curriculum Guide](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/MATATAG-Kindergarten-CG.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Kindergarten Curriculum Guide
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 2–9, 13, 16, 30; developmental domains, play-based approach, literacy/numeracy, assessment and family communication
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** DepEd KCG pp. 7–9; PRC Annex A.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Use shared text, conversation, print awareness, and meaning-making in early literacy.

**Study lesson reference**

- Early Childhood Learning, Assessment, and Inclusive Practice — 6. Emergent literacy and dialogic reading
- Material ID: let-early-childhood-learning-and-inclusion; section ID: 6-emergent-literacy-and-dialogic-reading
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Early literacy grows through meaningful encounters with oral language, stories, symbols, environmental print, and shared books. During reading, invite children to notice an illustration, predict what may happen, connect an event to experience, retell a part, or explain an idea. Point out print features when they serve the discussion, but keep comprehension and communication central. Re-reading a valued text can deepen prediction and vocabulary; it does not need to become a rapid drill of letter names.

###### Question 9193

- **Question ID:** 9193
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Language, Literacy, and Everyday Numeracy / Dialogic Early Literacy

**Stem**

A learner retells a story by naming the ending first, then the beginning. What is a useful next teacher prompt?

**Options**

A. Correct the child’s wording before asking about story events.
B. Invite the learner to place three picture cards in story order and explain how one event leads to another.
C. Ask the learner to copy a sentence from the last page.
D. Tell the learner to listen again without offering a way to revisit the story.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Invite the learner to place three picture cards in story order and explain how one event leads to another.

**Explanation as submitted:** Picture sequencing gives the learner a concrete way to organize events and explain temporal relationships.

###### Question 9194

- **Question ID:** 9194
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Language, Literacy, and Everyday Numeracy / Dialogic Early Literacy

**Stem**

Children recognize a familiar sign near the school gate but cannot read all of its words. How can the teacher use the sign for emergent literacy?

**Options**

A. Tell children that recognizing a sign is irrelevant until they can decode each word.
B. Cover the pictures and ask learners to memorize the sign’s exact text.
C. Discuss where the sign appears, what it communicates, and which print or symbol clues help identify it.
D. Use the sign only to practice handwriting its first letter.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Discuss where the sign appears, what it communicates, and which print or symbol clues help identify it.

**Explanation as submitted:** Environmental print has meaning in context; noticing words, symbols, and location supports print awareness.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9195

- **Question ID:** 9195
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Language, Literacy, and Everyday Numeracy / Dialogic Early Literacy

**Stem**

A child asks about an unfamiliar word in a story. Which response best supports vocabulary and comprehension?

**Options**

A. Give a dictionary definition and move immediately to the next page.
B. Ask the child to copy the word several times before hearing the story again.
C. Skip the word because vocabulary discussion interrupts reading.
D. Reread the surrounding sentence, connect the word to the illustration or action, and invite the child to explain a possible meaning.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Reread the surrounding sentence, connect the word to the illustration or action, and invite the child to explain a possible meaning.

**Explanation as submitted:** Context, illustration, and discussion help the learner infer and use a new word meaningfully.

###### Question 9196

- **Question ID:** 9196
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Language, Literacy, and Everyday Numeracy / Dialogic Early Literacy

**Stem**

A teacher wants to introduce rhyme using a familiar poem. Which activity is most appropriate for kindergarten learners?

**Options**

A. Read the poem with rhythm, invite children to notice or supply a rhyming word, and discuss the sound pattern.
B. Require every child to complete a written worksheet before hearing the poem.
C. Teach a list of rhyme definitions without connecting them to spoken words.
D. Ask children to identify the poem’s punctuation before listening to sound.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Read the poem with rhythm, invite children to notice or supply a rhyming word, and discuss the sound pattern.

**Explanation as submitted:** Hearing and playing with sound in a meaningful text develops phonological awareness without replacing experience with abstract drill.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9197

- **Question ID:** 9197
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Language, Literacy, and Everyday Numeracy / Dialogic Early Literacy

**Stem**

A learner draws several marks beside a picture and says the marks tell a grandparent what happened. What is a strong literacy response?

**Options**

A. Erase the marks and provide a tracing page with correct letter forms.
B. Ask the learner to explain the message, record the child’s words if useful, and discuss how marks and pictures carry meaning.
C. Tell the learner the message is not writing because it is not conventionally spelled.
D. Ask the learner to memorize the adult’s sentence and copy it exactly.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Ask the learner to explain the message, record the child’s words if useful, and discuss how marks and pictures carry meaning.

**Explanation as submitted:** The child is using marks and images to communicate meaning. The teacher can value the intent and model conventional print without dismissing the message.

##### Concept: Emergent Number Sense

###### Question 9186

- **Question ID:** 9186
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Language, Literacy, and Everyday Numeracy / Emergent Number Sense

**Stem**

At snack time, a child places one cup beside each of four classmates and notices one cup remains. What concept is the teacher best positioned to explore next?

**Options**

A. The written form of four independent of any quantity.
B. A timed count of number words with no objects present.
C. One-to-one matching and whether the number of cups matches the number of classmates.
D. A comparison of which child can distribute cups most quickly.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. One-to-one matching and whether the number of cups matches the number of classmates.

**Explanation as submitted:** The routine makes one-to-one correspondence and quantity meaningful; the teacher can ask how the child noticed the extra cup.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Kindergarten Curriculum Guide](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/MATATAG-Kindergarten-CG.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Kindergarten Curriculum Guide
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 2–9, 13, 16, 30; developmental domains, play-based approach, literacy/numeracy, assessment and family communication
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** DepEd KCG pp. 8–9; PRC Annex A.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Develop quantity, pattern, number, and measurement understanding through concrete everyday experiences.

**Study lesson reference**

- Early Childhood Learning, Assessment, and Inclusive Practice — 7. Everyday numeracy
- Material ID: let-early-childhood-learning-and-inclusion; section ID: 7-everyday-numeracy
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): The guide presents emergent literacy and basic numeracy as related learning. Build number and quantity ideas with objects children can move, compare, group, and count. Patterns can be noticed in steps, sounds, shells, or repeated classroom routines. Measurement and time make sense when connected to practical questions such as which container holds more or what happens before clean-up. Ask children to show how they know. Keep the representation tied to a real quantity or relation rather than relying on symbol copying alone.

###### Question 9187

- **Question ID:** 9187
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Language, Literacy, and Everyday Numeracy / Emergent Number Sense

**Stem**

A child counts the same set of shells twice after the teacher spreads them farther apart, giving a different total each time. What is a useful next activity?

**Options**

A. Ask the child to memorize the last total and repeat it without touching the shells.
B. Add more shells immediately so the child practices a larger sequence.
C. Show the correct total and ask the child to copy the numeral several times.
D. Let the child move each shell into a new row as it is counted, then compare the result with the original count.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Let the child move each shell into a new row as it is counted, then compare the result with the original count.

**Explanation as submitted:** Moving each object once links the count word sequence to one-to-one correspondence and helps the child check a changing arrangement.

###### Question 9188

- **Question ID:** 9188
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Language, Literacy, and Everyday Numeracy / Emergent Number Sense

**Stem**

Children make a sound pattern with clap, tap, clap, tap. Which prompt best advances pattern reasoning?

**Options**

A. Ask what sound comes next and what part of the sequence repeats.
B. Ask them to recite the number names while the pattern continues.
C. Tell them the next sound and have them repeat the whole sequence.
D. Ask which child made the loudest sound in the sequence.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Ask what sound comes next and what part of the sequence repeats.

**Explanation as submitted:** Predicting and identifying the repeating unit makes the pattern structure explicit.

###### Question 9189

- **Question ID:** 9189
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Language, Literacy, and Everyday Numeracy / Emergent Number Sense

**Stem**

A group measures a block tower with linking cubes and compares it with a second tower. Which procedure makes the comparison meaningful?

**Options**

A. Use whatever blocks are nearby and estimate from the tallest point.
B. Use equal-sized cubes and begin each measurement at the same base level.
C. Place cubes with gaps so learners can finish the measurement sooner.
D. Compare the number of colors rather than the units covering each tower.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Use equal-sized cubes and begin each measurement at the same base level.

**Explanation as submitted:** A consistent unit and shared starting point make the measures comparable.

###### Question 9190

- **Question ID:** 9190
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Language, Literacy, and Everyday Numeracy / Emergent Number Sense

**Stem**

In a pretend market, children arrange picture cards for opening, shopping, paying, and closing. Which question integrates everyday numeracy and sequence?

**Options**

A. Ask each child to write a clock time for every event before the play begins.
B. Ask which card looks most colorful and remove the other cards.
C. Ask which event comes before payment and how many item cards are in the basket.
D. Ask the children to count the people rather than interpret the event order.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Ask which event comes before payment and how many item cards are in the basket.

**Explanation as submitted:** Order and count are meaningful within the play context, with concrete representations rather than unsupported clock notation.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

###### Question 9191

- **Question ID:** 9191
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Language, Literacy, and Everyday Numeracy / Emergent Number Sense

**Stem**

The class has 12 buttons for sorting. One learner groups by color, another by size, and a third makes a repeating color pattern. What is the strongest teacher response?

**Options**

A. Require everyone to use color because the first learner began that way.
B. Choose one learner’s arrangement as correct and have the others copy it.
C. Count the buttons aloud and stop the activity before comparing the groupings.
D. Ask each learner to explain the rule used and invite the group to test whether every button fits that rule.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Ask each learner to explain the rule used and invite the group to test whether every button fits that rule.

**Explanation as submitted:** Multiple attributes can support different valid classifications. Explanation and testing make the rule visible without imposing a single arbitrary strategy.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

##### Concept: Responsive Language Scaffolding

###### Question 9180

- **Question ID:** 9180
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Language, Literacy, and Everyday Numeracy / Responsive Language Scaffolding

**Stem**

During a nature-sorting activity, a child explains in Ilokano why two leaves belong together. Which response best supports language and concept learning?

**Options**

A. Acknowledge the explanation, restate its idea, and add a related classroom-language word while inviting the child to continue.
B. Ask the child to wait until able to give the explanation only in English.
C. Translate the answer for the class, then move on without returning to the child.
D. Treat the language choice as evidence that the child has not understood the sorting idea.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Acknowledge the explanation, restate its idea, and add a related classroom-language word while inviting the child to continue.

**Explanation as submitted:** The kindergarten guide recognizes the child’s first language as a resource. Restating meaning and adding vocabulary supports both participation and language development.

###### Question 9181

- **Question ID:** 9181
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Language, Literacy, and Everyday Numeracy / Responsive Language Scaffolding

**Stem**

While retelling a familiar story, a learner moves between Filipino and a home language and loses the group’s attention. What is the strongest teaching move?

**Options**

A. Require the learner to restart in one language before classmates may respond.
B. Invite the learner to continue the retelling, then model one useful connecting phrase and ask a peer to build on the story idea.
C. Replace the retelling with a vocabulary worksheet so the group stays on schedule.
D. Ask the family to stop using the home language during story time.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Invite the learner to continue the retelling, then model one useful connecting phrase and ask a peer to build on the story idea.

**Explanation as submitted:** The child is communicating meaning. A modeled phrase and peer response extend oral language without treating multilingual expression as a deficit.

###### Question 9182

- **Question ID:** 9182
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Language, Literacy, and Everyday Numeracy / Responsive Language Scaffolding

**Stem**

A child understands a picture-based sorting task but rarely answers the teacher’s spoken questions in the classroom language. Which next step best checks understanding while supporting language?

**Options**

A. Repeat the same question louder until the child gives a spoken response.
B. Conclude that the child cannot sort until fluent classroom-language speech develops.
C. Offer a choice to point, place an object, or answer with a familiar word, then model the classroom-language term in context.
D. Give the child a separate easier sorting task without checking the intended concept.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Offer a choice to point, place an object, or answer with a familiar word, then model the classroom-language term in context.

**Explanation as submitted:** A response mode can reveal the concept while the teacher builds classroom language in context; speech fluency is not the sorting target.

###### Question 9183

- **Question ID:** 9183
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Language, Literacy, and Everyday Numeracy / Responsive Language Scaffolding

**Stem**

A caregiver shares a short home-language song that children use during a daily routine. How can the teacher use it to extend oral language appropriately?

**Options**

A. Use the song as a reward only after children complete English pronunciation drills.
B. Present the song as a generic tradition shared by all families in the class.
C. Avoid the song because home-language material does not belong in literacy learning.
D. Invite children to notice repeated sounds or actions, learn the song with family attribution, and connect its words to the routine.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Invite children to notice repeated sounds or actions, learn the song with family attribution, and connect its words to the routine.

**Explanation as submitted:** A familiar song can connect sound, meaning, routine, and relationship; the teacher should attribute its source and avoid generalizing it.

###### Question 9184

- **Question ID:** 9184
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Language, Literacy, and Everyday Numeracy / Responsive Language Scaffolding

**Stem**

During a discussion, one child watches closely and points to the object named by a classmate but does not answer aloud. What should the teacher infer?

**Options**

A. The pointing is evidence of participation; offer a supported way to add an idea and observe language across other contexts.
B. The child has mastered all vocabulary because the correct object was selected once.
C. The child does not understand because no spoken answer was produced.
D. The child should be moved away from group discussion until speech improves.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. The pointing is evidence of participation; offer a supported way to add an idea and observe language across other contexts.

**Explanation as submitted:** A single nonverbal response is useful evidence but does not establish complete mastery or lack of understanding; collect further evidence.

###### Question 9185

- **Question ID:** 9185
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Language, Literacy, and Everyday Numeracy / Responsive Language Scaffolding

**Stem**

A team plans a new unit because several children use unfamiliar words for local plants. Which evidence-based language plan is strongest?

**Options**

A. Replace the children’s words with standardized labels before discussing the plants.
B. Ask families about the children’s terms and experiences, connect them to accurate curriculum vocabulary, and use both during hands-on observation.
C. Assume each unfamiliar word refers to the same plant and prepare one translation list.
D. Delay the unit until all children use identical vocabulary for the plants.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Ask families about the children’s terms and experiences, connect them to accurate curriculum vocabulary, and use both during hands-on observation.

**Explanation as submitted:** Family knowledge can clarify meaning and connect language to experience; the teacher can add accurate curriculum terms without erasing children’s vocabulary.

#### Topic: Observation to Responsive Planning

##### Concept: Integrated Developmental Planning

###### Question 9234

- **Question ID:** 9234
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Observation to Responsive Planning / Integrated Developmental Planning

**Stem**

A teacher plans a nature walk where children collect leaves, describe their colors, and sort them by shape. Which feature makes this an integrated learning experience?

**Options**

A. Plan isolated objectives for each domain even though the walk can connect them through a shared learning task.
B. The teacher evaluates only how quickly children walk.
C. One shared activity supports observation, oral language, classification, and movement in a meaningful context.
D. The walk replaces observation with a lecture about leaf names.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. One shared activity supports observation, oral language, classification, and movement in a meaningful context.

**Explanation as submitted:** The activity connects several domains through a shared experience and provides observable learning evidence.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Kindergarten Curriculum Guide](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/MATATAG-Kindergarten-CG.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Kindergarten Curriculum Guide
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 2–9, 13, 16, 30; developmental domains, play-based approach, literacy/numeracy, assessment and family communication
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** DepEd KCG pp. 5–9, 16; PRC Annex A.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Connect developmental domains, context, observation, and responsive next steps.

**Study lesson reference**

- Early Childhood Learning, Assessment, and Inclusive Practice — 10. Responsive planning and inclusion
- Material ID: let-early-childhood-learning-and-inclusion; section ID: 10-responsive-planning-and-inclusion
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Plan from the intended learning and observed barrier. A child may show understanding by speaking, pointing, building, moving, drawing, or using a familiar symbol; choose a response mode that still demonstrates the target skill. Combine domains in an integrated activity when the connections are meaningful, such as sorting natural objects, describing a pattern, and collaborating on a display. If one child is not participating, examine access, clarity, pace, materials, and relationship before attributing the difficulty to the child.

###### Question 9235

- **Question ID:** 9235
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Observation to Responsive Planning / Integrated Developmental Planning

**Stem**

A child is deeply engaged in block construction but has difficulty during a later pencil task about shapes. Which plan best responds to the evidence?

**Options**

A. Conclude the child cannot understand shapes because the pencil task was difficult.
B. Remove all shape vocabulary until the child can complete the worksheet.
C. Repeat the same pencil task with no change in support.
D. Use blocks to explore and name shape relationships, then observe whether understanding appears in another representation.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Use blocks to explore and name shape relationships, then observe whether understanding appears in another representation.

**Explanation as submitted:** Concrete materials may make the concept accessible; the teacher can use multiple representations and observe transfer.

###### Question 9236

- **Question ID:** 9236
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Observation to Responsive Planning / Integrated Developmental Planning

**Stem**

A class activity includes counting, drawing, and sharing, but the teacher has not decided what evidence to observe. What is the best preparation?

**Options**

A. Name the intended learning for each part and select an observable behavior or product that can show it.
B. Wait until the activity ends and give one overall participation score.
C. Use a single age-based milestone to evaluate all parts.
D. Record only the children who finish first.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Name the intended learning for each part and select an observable behavior or product that can show it.

**Explanation as submitted:** Assessment should be aligned to the learning intentions and produce usable evidence for planning.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9237

- **Question ID:** 9237
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Observation to Responsive Planning / Integrated Developmental Planning

**Stem**

A teacher must choose the next unit after reviewing observations, work samples, and family input. Which decision process is strongest?

**Options**

A. Select the unit that is easiest to prepare, then score children against it.
B. Identify shared learning needs and interests, plan accessible experiences across relevant domains, then monitor how the planned supports work.
C. Plan separate activities based only on each child’s age.
D. Use family input to replace all curriculum outcomes with unrelated topics.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Identify shared learning needs and interests, plan accessible experiences across relevant domains, then monitor how the planned supports work.

**Explanation as submitted:** Integrated planning respects evidence and learner context while retaining curriculum goals and checking the effects of support.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Kindergarten Curriculum Guide](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/MATATAG-Kindergarten-CG.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Kindergarten Curriculum Guide
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 2–9, 13, 16, 30; developmental domains, play-based approach, literacy/numeracy, assessment and family communication
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** DepEd KCG pp. 5–9, 16; PRC Annex A.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Connect developmental domains, context, observation, and responsive next steps.

**Study lesson reference**

- Early Childhood Learning, Assessment, and Inclusive Practice — 10. Responsive planning and inclusion
- Material ID: let-early-childhood-learning-and-inclusion; section ID: 10-responsive-planning-and-inclusion
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Plan from the intended learning and observed barrier. A child may show understanding by speaking, pointing, building, moving, drawing, or using a familiar symbol; choose a response mode that still demonstrates the target skill. Combine domains in an integrated activity when the connections are meaningful, such as sorting natural objects, describing a pattern, and collaborating on a display. If one child is not participating, examine access, clarity, pace, materials, and relationship before attributing the difficulty to the child.

##### Concept: Observation to Responsive Planning

###### Question 9198

- **Question ID:** 9198
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Observation to Responsive Planning / Observation to Responsive Planning

**Stem**

Which note is the clearest observation record during block play?

**Options**

A. “Mira is a natural engineer.”
B. “Mira was more advanced than the other children today.”
C. “At 9:10, Mira placed three blocks in a row and said, ‘This is the bridge.’”
D. “Mira understands every idea about building.”

**Proposed correct answer:** C. “At 9:10, Mira placed three blocks in a row and said, ‘This is the bridge.’”

**Explanation as submitted:** The record gives a time, action, and direct words without converting one event into a broad judgment.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Kindergarten Curriculum Guide](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/MATATAG-Kindergarten-CG.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Kindergarten Curriculum Guide
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 2–9, 13, 16, 30; developmental domains, play-based approach, literacy/numeracy, assessment and family communication
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** DepEd KCG p. 16; PRC Annex A.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Use clear, repeated documentation to choose a next teaching experience rather than infer from one event.

**Study lesson reference**

- Early Childhood Learning, Assessment, and Inclusive Practice — 3. Observation and assessment
- Material ID: let-early-childhood-learning-and-inclusion; section ID: 3-observation-and-assessment
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Separate evidence from inference. “Placed three shells in a row and said ‘same’” is observable; “understands patterns” is an interpretation to check. Combine repeated observations, conversation, work samples, portfolios, and suitable checklists. The DepEd guide describes continuing documentation and use of the ECD checklist to identify learners who may need further assessment; a checklist is not a diagnosis. Choose the next activity from evidence, then check whether it helps. Share progress with families respectfully and protect confidential information.

###### Question 9199

- **Question ID:** 9199
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Observation to Responsive Planning / Observation to Responsive Planning

**Stem**

A child leaves the group most mornings just before clean-up. What should the teacher examine before drawing a conclusion?

**Options**

A. Whether the child should lose a preferred activity for leaving the group.
B. Whether the child has a fixed attention problem based on this pattern alone.
C. Whether other children can be asked to bring the child back each morning.
D. Repeated observations of the transition, cues, noise, task demands, and the child’s response after different supports.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Repeated observations of the transition, cues, noise, task demands, and the child’s response after different supports.

**Explanation as submitted:** A repeated pattern calls for examination of context and response to support, not a diagnosis or punishment based on one behavior.

###### Question 9200

- **Question ID:** 9200
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Observation to Responsive Planning / Observation to Responsive Planning

**Stem**

A portfolio includes one painting but no date, prompt, or learner explanation. What addition most improves its value for planning?

**Options**

A. Add the date, activity context, relevant observation, and the learner’s own explanation of the work.
B. Add a score comparing the painting with classmates’ work.
C. Replace the painting with a teacher-written summary of the child’s personality.
D. Add the child’s age and assume the painting shows age-level mastery.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Add the date, activity context, relevant observation, and the learner’s own explanation of the work.

**Explanation as submitted:** Context and the learner’s explanation support interpretation and comparison over time.

###### Question 9201

- **Question ID:** 9201
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Observation to Responsive Planning / Observation to Responsive Planning

**Stem**

A kindergarten checklist flags that a learner may need further assessment. What is the teacher’s best next step?

**Options**

A. Tell the family the checklist proves the learner has a developmental disorder.
B. Discuss the documented concern with the family and follow the school’s process for appropriate further assessment.
C. Repeat the checklist until a different result appears and discard earlier records.
D. Use the checklist result to remove the learner from the activity.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Discuss the documented concern with the family and follow the school’s process for appropriate further assessment.

**Explanation as submitted:** The curriculum guide presents the checklist as a way to identify possible need for further assessment, not a diagnosis or exclusion decision.

###### Question 9202

- **Question ID:** 9202
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Observation to Responsive Planning / Observation to Responsive Planning

**Stem**

A skill appears during outdoor play but not during a table task. Which interpretation best supports responsive teaching?

**Options**

A. The outdoor observation must be ignored because it was not a formal test.
B. The table result proves the learner has permanently lost the skill.
C. The difference may reflect context or task access, so gather more evidence and ask what features help the learner show the skill.
D. The learner should be assessed only in the setting with the lower score.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. The difference may reflect context or task access, so gather more evidence and ask what features help the learner show the skill.

**Explanation as submitted:** Performance can vary with materials, language, partners, and setting; compare conditions before planning.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9203

- **Question ID:** 9203
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Observation to Responsive Planning / Observation to Responsive Planning

**Stem**

A teacher has two dated work samples, direct observations, and family input that a child enjoys sorting at home. Which planning choice best uses the evidence?

**Options**

A. Repeat the same task until the child produces the teacher’s preferred answer.
B. Conclude that home performance guarantees the same classroom skill in every context.
C. Select a new activity based only on the child’s chronological age.
D. Offer a new sorting task with a modest challenge, record how the child explains the rule, and review whether participation grows.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Offer a new sorting task with a modest challenge, record how the child explains the rule, and review whether participation grows.

**Explanation as submitted:** Multiple evidence sources suggest a relevant next experience, while the new observation checks learning in the classroom context.

#### Topic: Social-Emotional Learning and Safeguarding

##### Concept: Inclusive Participation Design

###### Question 9222

- **Question ID:** 9222
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Social-Emotional Learning and Safeguarding / Inclusive Participation Design

**Stem**

A learner finds a small paintbrush hard to grip during an art activity. Which adjustment best preserves the learning goal of exploring color?

**Options**

A. Remove the learner from painting and ask them to watch.
B. Complete the painting for the learner so the class stays together.
C. Offer a larger-handled brush or another safe applicator and let the learner choose how to apply the color.
D. Change the activity to a handwriting task for every child.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Offer a larger-handled brush or another safe applicator and let the learner choose how to apply the color.

**Explanation as submitted:** The adjustment changes access to the tool while retaining the color-exploration goal.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Kindergarten Curriculum Guide](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/MATATAG-Kindergarten-CG.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Kindergarten Curriculum Guide
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 2–9, 13, 16, 30; developmental domains, play-based approach, literacy/numeracy, assessment and family communication
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex A; DepEd KCG pp. 5–8.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Remove avoidable environmental and response barriers while retaining a shared learning intention.

**Study lesson reference**

- Early Childhood Learning, Assessment, and Inclusive Practice — 10. Responsive planning and inclusion

A child may show understanding by speaking, pointing, building, moving, drawing, or using a familiar symbol; choose a response mode that still demonstrates the target skill. If one child is not participating, examine access, clarity, pace, materials, and relationship before attributing the difficulty to the child.

###### Question 9223

- **Question ID:** 9223
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Social-Emotional Learning and Safeguarding / Inclusive Participation Design

**Stem**

A class discussion requires children to answer aloud, but one learner can show the relevant idea by arranging picture cards. What should the teacher do?

**Options**

A. Insist that the learner answer aloud before the class may continue.
B. Give the learner unrelated coloring work during the discussion.
C. Count the sequence as incorrect because it was not spoken.
D. Accept the card sequence as evidence when oral speech is not the target, then invite an optional explanation.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Accept the card sequence as evidence when oral speech is not the target, then invite an optional explanation.

**Explanation as submitted:** A response mode should match the learning target; card arrangement can show understanding when speech is not being assessed.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

###### Question 9224

- **Question ID:** 9224
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Social-Emotional Learning and Safeguarding / Inclusive Participation Design

**Stem**

A crowded shelf narrows the path to a popular learning center. What should the teacher check first?

**Options**

A. Rearrange the furniture to create a clear path and observe whether children can reach and use the center independently.
B. Ask children to wait until the crowd becomes smaller before approaching.
C. Move the center farther away without checking the new route.
D. Tell the learner who has difficulty moving to choose a different center.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Rearrange the furniture to create a clear path and observe whether children can reach and use the center independently.

**Explanation as submitted:** The environment is a potential barrier; a clear path supports access for more learners and should be checked in use.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Kindergarten Curriculum Guide](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/MATATAG-Kindergarten-CG.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Kindergarten Curriculum Guide
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 2–9, 13, 16, 30; developmental domains, play-based approach, literacy/numeracy, assessment and family communication
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex A; DepEd KCG pp. 5–8.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Remove avoidable environmental and response barriers while retaining a shared learning intention.

**Study lesson reference**

- Early Childhood Learning, Assessment, and Inclusive Practice — 10. Responsive planning and inclusion
- Material ID: let-early-childhood-learning-and-inclusion; section ID: 10-responsive-planning-and-inclusion
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Plan from the intended learning and observed barrier. A child may show understanding by speaking, pointing, building, moving, drawing, or using a familiar symbol; choose a response mode that still demonstrates the target skill. Combine domains in an integrated activity when the connections are meaningful, such as sorting natural objects, describing a pattern, and collaborating on a display. If one child is not participating, examine access, clarity, pace, materials, and relationship before attributing the difficulty to the child.

###### Question 9225

- **Question ID:** 9225
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Social-Emotional Learning and Safeguarding / Inclusive Participation Design

**Stem**

Learners miss a multi-step instruction during a noisy activity. Which support is most likely to improve access?

**Options**

A. Repeat the same long instruction from across the room.
B. Present the steps clearly with a simple visual cue and check understanding before practice begins.
C. Ask peers to guess what each learner should do.
D. Reduce the learning goal without checking whether the directions caused the difficulty.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Present the steps clearly with a simple visual cue and check understanding before practice begins.

**Explanation as submitted:** A clear representation and comprehension check address an instruction-access barrier without changing the task goal.

###### Question 9226

- **Question ID:** 9226
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Social-Emotional Learning and Safeguarding / Inclusive Participation Design

**Stem**

A group project requires every child to speak to the class, though the intended competency is sorting materials by function. What is the best design revision?

**Options**

A. Keep public speaking as a hidden requirement because it is convenient to score.
B. Have the teacher sort the materials and ask learners to watch.
C. Allow learners to show the group’s sorting through an arranged display or supported explanation while assessing the classification.
D. Remove the classification task for learners who do not present orally.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Allow learners to show the group’s sorting through an arranged display or supported explanation while assessing the classification.

**Explanation as submitted:** The assessment should measure sorting if that is the stated competency; public speaking can be optional unless separately taught and assessed.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9227

- **Question ID:** 9227
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Social-Emotional Learning and Safeguarding / Inclusive Participation Design

**Stem**

During music and movement, one learner covers their ears when several instruments play at once but joins when the group uses a softer single rhythm. What should the teacher do?

**Options**

A. Require the learner to remain in the loudest part until they become used to it.
B. Exclude the learner from all music activities because of the reaction.
C. Conclude that the learner dislikes music and change their curriculum.
D. Plan a predictable lower-volume entry and ask the learner what helps, while observing participation in the shared rhythm task.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Plan a predictable lower-volume entry and ask the learner what helps, while observing participation in the shared rhythm task.

**Explanation as submitted:** A responsive environmental adjustment can support access while the teacher learns which conditions allow meaningful participation.
