##### Concept: Peer Interaction and Co-Regulation

###### Question 9216

- **Question ID:** 9216
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Social-Emotional Learning and Safeguarding / Peer Interaction and Co-Regulation

**Stem**

A child becomes upset when a caregiver leaves at arrival. Which response best supports co-regulation?

**Options**

A. Stay close, name the feeling gently, and guide the child toward a predictable settling routine.
B. Tell the child to stop crying before joining the class.
C. Leave the child alone until the emotion has passed.
D. Promise that the caregiver will return at a specific time the teacher cannot verify.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Stay close, name the feeling gently, and guide the child toward a predictable settling routine.

**Explanation as submitted:** A calm relationship and predictable routine help the child regain participation without dismissing the feeling or making an unsupported promise.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Kindergarten Curriculum Guide](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/MATATAG-Kindergarten-CG.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Kindergarten Curriculum Guide
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 2–9, 13, 16, 30; developmental domains, play-based approach, literacy/numeracy, assessment and family communication
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** DepEd KCG pp. 6–7; PRC Annex A.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Support emotion naming, relational routines, and peer participation through responsive teaching.

**Study lesson reference**

- Early Childhood Learning, Assessment, and Inclusive Practice — 8. Relationships and social-emotional learning
- Material ID: let-early-childhood-learning-and-inclusion; section ID: 8-relationships-and-social-emotional-learning
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Social-emotional learning develops within predictable, responsive relationships. A teacher can name a feeling tentatively, acknowledge a child’s experience, help the child notice a safe choice, and remain nearby while the child regains participation. Routines, visual cues, transition warnings, and calm spaces can reduce unnecessary uncertainty. Peer conflict is an opportunity to teach communication, turn-taking, and repair; public shame or forced apologies do not teach the missing skill.

###### Question 9217

- **Question ID:** 9217
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Social-Emotional Learning and Safeguarding / Peer Interaction and Co-Regulation

**Stem**

Two children reach for the same paint tray and begin arguing. What is the best teaching response?

**Options**

A. Remove the paint for the rest of the class period.
B. Acknowledge both interests and coach them to choose a turn-taking or shared-use plan.
C. Choose one child as the permanent owner of the tray.
D. Tell the children that the louder one may use it first.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Acknowledge both interests and coach them to choose a turn-taking or shared-use plan.

**Explanation as submitted:** The teacher can support language, self-regulation, and a workable peer solution while keeping access to the activity.

###### Question 9218

- **Question ID:** 9218
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Social-Emotional Learning and Safeguarding / Peer Interaction and Co-Regulation

**Stem**

Several learners become unsettled each time the class switches from play to story. Which environmental support is most useful to try?

**Options**

A. Add an unexpected timed race to make the transition exciting.
B. Remove the play materials without warning so the change is immediate.
C. Give a consistent transition cue and preview what will happen next before the change.
D. Require learners to sit silently before explaining the next activity.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Give a consistent transition cue and preview what will happen next before the change.

**Explanation as submitted:** A predictable cue and preview reduce uncertainty and make the routine easier to follow.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

###### Question 9219

- **Question ID:** 9219
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Social-Emotional Learning and Safeguarding / Peer Interaction and Co-Regulation

**Stem**

A learner becomes angry after losing a simple game and pushes the pieces away. What should the teacher teach after ensuring safety?

**Options**

A. Require an apology before discussing what happened.
B. Tell the learner that winning is unimportant and restart the game.
C. Remove the learner from all future games.
D. Help the learner name the frustration and practice a respectful way to request another turn or pause.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Help the learner name the frustration and practice a respectful way to request another turn or pause.

**Explanation as submitted:** Naming the feeling and rehearsing a useful communication response teaches a replacement skill and supports re-entry.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9220

- **Question ID:** 9220
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Social-Emotional Learning and Safeguarding / Peer Interaction and Co-Regulation

**Stem**

A group repeatedly leaves one child out of a pretend-play role. What is a constructive teacher move?

**Options**

A. Observe the interaction, support the children in planning roles, and check whether each learner can contribute meaningfully.
B. Assign the excluded child a role without asking what they want.
C. Tell the group to resolve it alone and leave the area.
D. Replace the play with individual seatwork for the whole group.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Observe the interaction, support the children in planning roles, and check whether each learner can contribute meaningfully.

**Explanation as submitted:** Teacher support can improve peer inclusion while respecting the children’s ideas and observing whether the change works.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

###### Question 9221

- **Question ID:** 9221
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Social-Emotional Learning and Safeguarding / Peer Interaction and Co-Regulation

**Stem**

A learner often uses a quiet corner during noisy group activities, then returns to participate. How should the teacher interpret the routine?

**Options**

A. Label it as avoidance and remove access to the quiet space.
B. Treat it as information about what supports regulation, ask what the learner needs, and plan a respectful return to participation.
C. Assume the child should remain in the corner for the whole activity.
D. Require the learner to explain the choice to the group each time.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Treat it as information about what supports regulation, ask what the learner needs, and plan a respectful return to participation.

**Explanation as submitted:** A quiet space can support regulation when it is not punitive or isolating; the learner’s voice and re-entry matter.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Kindergarten Curriculum Guide](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/MATATAG-Kindergarten-CG.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Kindergarten Curriculum Guide
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 2–9, 13, 16, 30; developmental domains, play-based approach, literacy/numeracy, assessment and family communication
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** DepEd KCG pp. 6–7; PRC Annex A.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Support emotion naming, relational routines, and peer participation through responsive teaching.

**Study lesson reference**

- Early Childhood Learning, Assessment, and Inclusive Practice — 8. Relationships and social-emotional learning
- Material ID: let-early-childhood-learning-and-inclusion; section ID: 8-relationships-and-social-emotional-learning
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Social-emotional learning develops within predictable, responsive relationships. A teacher can name a feeling tentatively, acknowledge a child’s experience, help the child notice a safe choice, and remain nearby while the child regains participation. Routines, visual cues, transition warnings, and calm spaces can reduce unnecessary uncertainty. Peer conflict is an opportunity to teach communication, turn-taking, and repair; public shame or forced apologies do not teach the missing skill.

##### Concept: Safeguarding Response and Boundaries

###### Question 9228

- **Question ID:** 9228
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Social-Emotional Learning and Safeguarding / Safeguarding Response and Boundaries

**Stem**

A child reports being repeatedly mocked and excluded by classmates because of a disability. Which school response best fits the documented policy scope?

**Options**

A. Recognize bullying and discrimination as child-protection concerns and use the school’s applicable protection process.
B. Treat the pattern only as a disagreement about game rules.
C. Wait until the child stops attending the activity before responding.
D. Ask classmates to decide whether the behavior is serious enough.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Recognize bullying and discrimination as child-protection concerns and use the school’s applicable protection process.

**Explanation as submitted:** DepEd Order 40 identifies bullying and discrimination among the harms addressed by the Child Protection Policy.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [DepEd Order No. 40, s. 2012: DepEd Child Protection Policy](https://www.deped.gov.ph/2012/05/14/do-40-s-2012-deped-child-protection-policy/) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; DO 40 s. 2012
  - Source-level relevant sections: Order and policy annex; child abuse, violence, exploitation, discrimination, bullying and other abuse; current DepEd child-protection index
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** DO 40 s. 2012; PRC Annex A.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Respond to a child-protection concern calmly, document observable facts, and use school procedures.

**Study lesson reference**

- Early Childhood Learning, Assessment, and Inclusive Practice — 9. Child protection and safeguarding
- Material ID: let-early-childhood-learning-and-inclusion; section ID: 9-child-protection-and-safeguarding
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): DepEd Order 40 identifies abuse, violence, exploitation, discrimination, bullying, and other forms of abuse as child-protection concerns in school. A teacher should recognize when a classroom issue falls within this scope and use the applicable school child-protection process. This lesson teaches the policy boundary and professional recognition only; exact reporting roles, timing, records, and follow-up must be checked in the current school procedure. Do not treat a covered concern as a routine behavior issue or make unsupported legal conclusions.

###### Question 9229

- **Question ID:** 9229
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Social-Emotional Learning and Safeguarding / Safeguarding Response and Boundaries

**Stem**

A learner reports a violent act by an adult in a school setting. Which issue should guide the teacher’s next decision?

**Options**

A. The report is only relevant if it interrupted a lesson.
B. The report describes violence, which is within the Child Protection Policy scope, so the teacher should use the applicable school protection process.
C. The teacher should wait for a second report before taking the concern seriously.
D. The concern should be discussed with classmates before school staff act.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. The report describes violence, which is within the Child Protection Policy scope, so the teacher should use the applicable school protection process.

**Explanation as submitted:** The official policy page explicitly includes violence among the harms covered; the school process governs follow-up.

###### Question 9230

- **Question ID:** 9230
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Social-Emotional Learning and Safeguarding / Safeguarding Response and Boundaries

**Stem**

A staff member says a report of possible exploitation should be handled as a private classroom matter. What is the appropriate professional response?

**Options**

A. Handle the matter privately without referring it through school procedures.
B. Ignore the concern unless it affects academic performance.
C. Recognize exploitation as covered by the Child Protection Policy and use the applicable school protection process.
D. Ask learners to determine whether the event meets a legal definition.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Recognize exploitation as covered by the Child Protection Policy and use the applicable school protection process.

**Explanation as submitted:** DepEd’s policy scope expressly includes exploitation; a teacher should not substitute an informal classroom decision for school procedure.

###### Question 9231

- **Question ID:** 9231
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Social-Emotional Learning and Safeguarding / Safeguarding Response and Boundaries

**Stem**

A teacher hears that a child was threatened repeatedly by peers. What is the strongest next step?

**Options**

A. Classify it as ordinary play without checking the circumstances.
B. Ask the child to settle the matter alone with the peers.
C. Wait for the next scheduled class meeting before considering safety.
D. Treat the recurring threat as a potential violence or bullying concern and follow the school’s applicable child-protection process.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Treat the recurring threat as a potential violence or bullying concern and follow the school’s applicable child-protection process.

**Explanation as submitted:** The policy covers violence and bullying; recurring threats warrant the school’s protection pathway rather than an unsupported private judgment.

###### Question 9232

- **Question ID:** 9232
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Social-Emotional Learning and Safeguarding / Safeguarding Response and Boundaries

**Stem**

A colleague suggests sharing details of a reported child-protection concern in a general class message so everyone can help. What should the teacher do?

**Options**

A. Use the applicable school protection process and avoid placing the concern into a general class communication channel.
B. Post the details without the child’s name.
C. Ask families in the class group to investigate what happened.
D. Share a summary with learners so they can vote on next steps.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Use the applicable school protection process and avoid placing the concern into a general class communication channel.

**Explanation as submitted:** The policy concerns serious harms to children; the issue should be handled through the school process rather than a general class channel.

###### Question 9233

- **Question ID:** 9233
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Early Childhood Education / Social-Emotional Learning and Safeguarding / Safeguarding Response and Boundaries

**Stem**

A school activity is planned for young learners, and one proposed task exposes some children to avoidable humiliation. Which professional principle should guide revision?

**Options**

A. Keep the task because all learners received the same instructions.
B. Protect each child’s rights and equitable participation while keeping the intended learning outcome.
C. Use public embarrassment to encourage faster participation.
D. Remove children who may need an adjustment from the activity.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Protect each child’s rights and equitable participation while keeping the intended learning outcome.

**Explanation as submitted:** The PRC ECE scope includes child rights and equity; protection and participation are part of professionally sound planning.

### Subject: Physical Education

#### Topic: Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression

##### Concept: Developmentally Safe Progression

###### Question 9256

- **Question ID:** 9256
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Developmentally Safe Progression

**Stem**

Learners are new to a throwing skill. Which sequence provides a manageable progression?

**Options**

A. Begin with a stable, close target and change one task demand after learners show control.
B. Start with the farthest target and the smallest available ball.
C. Add speed, distance, and moving defense in the first attempt.
D. Assess full-game performance before introducing the skill.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Begin with a stable, close target and change one task demand after learners show control.

**Explanation as submitted:** A stable starting task lets learners practice control before added complexity.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Physical Education and Health Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/PE-and-HEALTH_CG-2023_Grade-4-and-7.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Physical Education and Health Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 5–8, 10–16, 20–21; movement foundations, developmental progression, assessment, target games and safety
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex D; MATATAG PE & Health pp. 5–8, 15.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Adjust task complexity, workload and practice conditions to learners and the intended outcome.

**Study lesson reference**

- Physical Education: Movement, Fitness, Safety, and Assessment — 7. Safe progression and injury prevention
- Material ID: let-physical-education-movement-and-safety; section ID: 7-safe-progression-and-injury-prevention
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Safety is part of effective instruction. Before an activity, check the surface, spacing, equipment, heat or weather conditions, directions, and learner preparation. Build complexity after foundational control, and provide recovery and accessible options. Teach learners how to pause and communicate a concern. If pain, dizziness, breathing difficulty, or an injury occurs, stop the activity and follow established school response procedures; do not diagnose, minimize, or tell a learner to push through.

###### Question 9257

- **Question ID:** 9257
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Developmentally Safe Progression

**Stem**

A class uses repeated high-effort circuits but has little time to recover between rounds. What is a sound teacher adjustment?

**Options**

A. Increase the number of rounds to help learners adapt faster.
B. Review the activity demands and provide an appropriate pace and recovery before repeating the circuit.
C. Keep the schedule because all learners should work at the same pace.
D. Remove all recovery so the activity remains vigorous.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Review the activity demands and provide an appropriate pace and recovery before repeating the circuit.

**Explanation as submitted:** Safe progression considers demand and recovery; a teacher should adjust conditions to the learner group.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Developmentally Safe Progression

**Stem**

A game becomes chaotic when learners must run, pass, and defend at once. What should the teacher try?

**Options**

A. Add more rules before learners understand the current task.
B. End all game practice and return only to lecture.
C. Reduce players or space demands and focus on one decision while preserving the game objective.
D. Require faster play so learners adapt to the challenge.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Reduce players or space demands and focus on one decision while preserving the game objective.

**Explanation as submitted:** A smaller task can preserve the learning problem while reducing complexity and improving practice.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Physical Education and Health Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/PE-and-HEALTH_CG-2023_Grade-4-and-7.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Physical Education and Health Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 5–8, 10–16, 20–21; movement foundations, developmental progression, assessment, target games and safety
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex D; MATATAG PE & Health pp. 5–8, 15.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Adjust task complexity, workload and practice conditions to learners and the intended outcome.

**Study lesson reference**

- Physical Education: Movement, Fitness, Safety, and Assessment — 7. Safe progression and injury prevention
- Material ID: let-physical-education-movement-and-safety; section ID: 7-safe-progression-and-injury-prevention
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Safety is part of effective instruction. Before an activity, check the surface, spacing, equipment, heat or weather conditions, directions, and learner preparation. Build complexity after foundational control, and provide recovery and accessible options. Teach learners how to pause and communicate a concern. If pain, dizziness, breathing difficulty, or an injury occurs, stop the activity and follow established school response procedures; do not diagnose, minimize, or tell a learner to push through.

###### Question 9259

- **Question ID:** 9259
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Developmentally Safe Progression

**Stem**

A learner reports discomfort during a new movement activity. What should the teacher do?

**Options**

A. Encourage the learner to continue so the teacher can finish the lesson.
B. Ask classmates to decide whether the discomfort is serious.
C. Tell the learner to self-diagnose and return when ready.
D. Pause the learner’s participation and follow the school’s established response process.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Pause the learner’s participation and follow the school’s established response process.

**Explanation as submitted:** The teacher should not diagnose or minimize symptoms; school response procedures guide next steps.

###### Question 9260

- **Question ID:** 9260
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Developmentally Safe Progression

**Stem**

A warm outdoor surface becomes slippery after rain before a running activity. What is the best decision?

**Options**

A. Reassess the surface and choose a safer activity space or task before learners begin.
B. Keep the planned running route because it was inspected earlier.
C. Ask learners to run more slowly on the surface without checking it.
D. Continue because the activity is not scored.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Reassess the surface and choose a safer activity space or task before learners begin.

**Explanation as submitted:** Environmental conditions can change; the teacher should inspect and adjust before activity.

###### Question 9261

- **Question ID:** 9261
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Developmentally Safe Progression

**Stem**

A class includes learners with different levels of experience in a balance sequence. What progression best supports all learners?

**Options**

A. Use the most complex version for everyone to ensure equal treatment.
B. Offer stable starting options and increase challenge through a planned choice of base, direction, or movement.
C. Keep the simplest version for every learner regardless of progress.
D. Rank learners and assign permanent versions by initial score.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Offer stable starting options and increase challenge through a planned choice of base, direction, or movement.

**Explanation as submitted:** Differentiated progression keeps a shared goal while allowing appropriate, safe challenge.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

##### Concept: Fitness Component Interpretation

###### Question 9250

- **Question ID:** 9250
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Fitness Component Interpretation

**Stem**

A lesson objective concerns muscular endurance. Which activity evidence best matches that construct?

**Options**

A. Measure a single short sprint time.
B. Assess how far a learner can reach while seated.
C. Observe repeated controlled muscle actions over the stated task conditions.
D. Record how accurately the learner names a sports rule.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Observe repeated controlled muscle actions over the stated task conditions.

**Explanation as submitted:** Repeated controlled muscular work is more directly related to endurance than sprint speed or flexibility.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Physical Education and Health Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/PE-and-HEALTH_CG-2023_Grade-4-and-7.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Physical Education and Health Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 5–8, 10–16, 20–21; movement foundations, developmental progression, assessment, target games and safety
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex D; MATATAG PE & Health pp. 5–8, 15.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Match a fitness measure and learning response to the component it validly represents.

**Study lesson reference**

- Physical Education: Movement, Fitness, Safety, and Assessment — 6. Fitness, progression, and assessment
- Material ID: let-physical-education-movement-and-safety; section ID: 6-fitness-progression-and-assessment
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): The PRC TOS expects learners to understand fitness and health, bodily response to exercise, and age-appropriate criterion-referenced assessment. Match a measure to the component, use the current applicable standard, and interpret the result as information for instruction. Avoid using one score as a judgment of character or a clinical conclusion. Progress activity with manageable changes, clear purpose, recovery, and supervision. Questions about health status should be handled through qualified school processes.

###### Question 9251

- **Question ID:** 9251
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Fitness Component Interpretation

**Stem**

A teacher uses a flexibility measure to make a claim about cardiorespiratory endurance. What is the problem?

**Options**

A. The measure includes too much movement for a PE class.
B. The measure should be used only during team games.
C. The claim is valid whenever a learner completes a test.
D. The measure does not match the fitness component named in the claim.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. The measure does not match the fitness component named in the claim.

**Explanation as submitted:** Assessment evidence should represent the construct the teacher claims to measure.

###### Question 9252

- **Question ID:** 9252
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Fitness Component Interpretation

**Stem**

A learner records a longer distance in a running activity but says the task felt easier after several sessions. Which fitness interpretation is reasonable?

**Options**

A. The repeated activity may provide evidence related to endurance, but conditions and other evidence should be considered.
B. The result proves every health-related fitness component improved.
C. The result establishes a medical diagnosis.
D. The result should be compared only with the fastest classmate.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. The repeated activity may provide evidence related to endurance, but conditions and other evidence should be considered.

**Explanation as submitted:** A task can inform one aspect of fitness, but it does not support broad or clinical conclusions.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

###### Question 9253

- **Question ID:** 9253
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Fitness Component Interpretation

**Stem**

A class objective concerns balance, but the selected test records how quickly learners sprint between markers. What needs to change?

**Options**

A. Keep the sprint because every fitness measure assesses balance.
B. Select an assessment task that actually elicits balance performance.
C. Add a written quiz about balance and ignore movement evidence.
D. Score only participation in the sprint.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Select an assessment task that actually elicits balance performance.

**Explanation as submitted:** The test must provide evidence for the named skill or fitness component.

###### Question 9254

- **Question ID:** 9254
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Fitness Component Interpretation

**Stem**

A learner asks why a body-composition result does not show how well they coordinate a movement pattern. What is the best explanation?

**Options**

A. All fitness measures indicate the same ability.
B. Coordination cannot be assessed through movement.
C. Each measure represents a particular construct; movement coordination requires its own relevant performance evidence.
D. The learner should use the result to judge personal worth.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Each measure represents a particular construct; movement coordination requires its own relevant performance evidence.

**Explanation as submitted:** Different measures address different constructs; one should not be treated as a proxy for another.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

###### Question 9255

- **Question ID:** 9255
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Fitness Component Interpretation

**Stem**

A teacher is choosing among several tests for an age-specific fitness unit. What should guide the choice?

**Options**

A. Which test creates the widest score range.
B. Which test is easiest to rank from highest to lowest.
C. Which test is familiar even if it measures a different component.
D. The intended fitness component, learner group, current applicable criteria, and safe administration conditions.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. The intended fitness component, learner group, current applicable criteria, and safe administration conditions.

**Explanation as submitted:** Valid selection depends on construct, learner group, applicable standards, and safe conditions.

##### Concept: Injury Prevention and Response

###### Question 9286

- **Question ID:** 9286
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Injury Prevention and Response

**Stem**

Before class, a teacher finds loose equipment on the planned activity path. What is the best next step?

**Options**

A. Ask learners to avoid the equipment while moving quickly.
B. Begin the activity and monitor whether anyone trips.
C. Clear the hazard or choose another safe space before learners begin.
D. Move only the equipment that is easy to reach.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Clear the hazard or choose another safe space before learners begin.

**Explanation as submitted:** Inspection and correction before activity reduce foreseeable hazards. The teacher should make the movement space clear before participation, or move the activity to a suitable area.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Physical Education and Health Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/PE-and-HEALTH_CG-2023_Grade-4-and-7.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Physical Education and Health Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 5–8, 10–16, 20–21; movement foundations, developmental progression, assessment, target games and safety
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex D; MATATAG PE & Health pp. 5–8, 15.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Recognize unsafe conditions and respond to injury symptoms using school safety procedures.

**Study lesson reference**

- Physical Education: Movement, Fitness, Safety, and Assessment — 7. Safe progression and injury prevention
- Material ID: let-physical-education-movement-and-safety; section ID: 7-safe-progression-and-injury-prevention
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Safety is part of effective instruction. Before an activity, check the surface, spacing, equipment, heat or weather conditions, directions, and learner preparation. Build complexity after foundational control, and provide recovery and accessible options. Teach learners how to pause and communicate a concern. If pain, dizziness, breathing difficulty, or an injury occurs, stop the activity and follow established school response procedures; do not diagnose, minimize, or tell a learner to push through.

###### Question 9287

- **Question ID:** 9287
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Injury Prevention and Response

**Stem**

A learner says they feel dizzy during a vigorous activity. What should the teacher do?

**Options**

A. Ask the learner to finish the round and report back.
B. Tell the learner the feeling is normal for exercise.
C. Have classmates decide whether the learner should continue.
D. Stop the learner’s activity and follow the school’s established response procedure.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Stop the learner’s activity and follow the school’s established response procedure.

**Explanation as submitted:** The teacher should not diagnose or encourage continuing through symptoms; use school response procedures.

###### Question 9288

- **Question ID:** 9288
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Injury Prevention and Response

**Stem**

Several students slip during a game after the floor becomes wet. What should the teacher do?

**Options**

A. Stop play, address the surface hazard, and resume only when the space is suitable.
B. Continue but remove points for falling.
C. Ask students to run more carefully without changing the surface.
D. Move the game equipment while leaving the wet area in use.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Stop play, address the surface hazard, and resume only when the space is suitable.

**Explanation as submitted:** A changed surface requires reassessment and correction before activity resumes.

###### Question 9289

- **Question ID:** 9289
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Injury Prevention and Response

**Stem**

A minor injury occurs during a class activity, and learners gather around. What is the PE teacher’s priority?

**Options**

A. Ask the learner to continue so the class can finish.
B. Pause the activity, protect the learner’s privacy, and follow the school’s first-aid or emergency procedure.
C. Let peers decide what care is needed.
D. Discuss the injury publicly while continuing the game.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Pause the activity, protect the learner’s privacy, and follow the school’s first-aid or emergency procedure.

**Explanation as submitted:** The teacher should use established school response procedures and protect the learner’s dignity. The teacher should avoid public discussion or diagnosis and follow the school’s designated first-aid or emergency process.

##### Concept: Valid Fitness Assessment

###### Question 9262

- **Question ID:** 9262
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Valid Fitness Assessment

**Stem**

A PE teacher wants to assess whether learners can perform a movement pattern taught in class. Which evidence is most appropriate?

**Options**

A. Use a written quiz on the pattern name alone.
B. Compare learners’ body size with a chart.
C. Observe each learner demonstrate the pattern under clear, age-appropriate conditions.
D. Ask peers which learner looks most athletic.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Observe each learner demonstrate the pattern under clear, age-appropriate conditions.

**Explanation as submitted:** Direct performance observation aligns with a movement skill outcome. Clear conditions let the teacher see the taught actions and give feedback on the relevant criteria.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

###### Question 9263

- **Question ID:** 9263
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Valid Fitness Assessment

**Stem**

A teacher compares a learner’s fitness score with a chart from a different age group. What should be corrected?

**Options**

A. Keep the chart because all fitness scores are interchangeable.
B. Use the highest available standard to challenge the learner.
C. Replace the test with a class ranking.
D. Use the current criterion that applies to the learner group and the specific test.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Use the current criterion that applies to the learner group and the specific test.

**Explanation as submitted:** The PRC scope calls for applying the relevant fitness criteria. A chart for a different group may change the meaning of the comparison, so confirm the test and applicable current criteria before interpreting the result.

###### Question 9264

- **Question ID:** 9264
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Valid Fitness Assessment

**Stem**

A test is administered outdoors on a windy day even though it is usually conducted indoors. What should the teacher document?

**Options**

A. The changed testing conditions and whether they may affect interpretation of the result.
B. Only the learner’s final score.
C. The class average without mentioning the conditions.
D. A health conclusion based on the score.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. The changed testing conditions and whether they may affect interpretation of the result.

**Explanation as submitted:** Conditions matter when interpreting performance; document changes and avoid unsupported claims.

###### Question 9265

- **Question ID:** 9265
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Valid Fitness Assessment

**Stem**

A teacher wants to assess progress in a skill unit. Which plan gives more useful evidence than one final test?

**Options**

A. Use one final score and infer the learner’s starting point from that result.
B. Use initial, during-unit, and final skill evidence aligned to the outcome.
C. Use weekly class ranks without criterion-linked feedback.
D. Use learner self-ratings as the only evidence of movement skill.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Use initial, during-unit, and final skill evidence aligned to the outcome.

**Explanation as submitted:** Diagnostic, formative, and summative evidence can guide instruction and summarize learning. Each check should align to the outcome and give the teacher information for a next instructional step.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9266

- **Question ID:** 9266
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Valid Fitness Assessment

**Stem**

A learner’s score changes between two fitness checks, but the test instructions differed. What is the strongest next step?

**Options**

A. Assume the score change proves a lasting fitness change.
B. Discard every fitness result from the class.
C. Review administration conditions and use a consistent, applicable method before comparing results.
D. Choose whichever score better matches expectations.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Review administration conditions and use a consistent, applicable method before comparing results.

**Explanation as submitted:** A comparison requires attention to whether the test was administered consistently.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Physical Education and Health Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/PE-and-HEALTH_CG-2023_Grade-4-and-7.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Physical Education and Health Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 5–8, 10–16, 20–21; movement foundations, developmental progression, assessment, target games and safety
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex D; MATATAG PE & Health pp. 15, 20–21.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Use age-appropriate, criterion-aligned evidence and interpret it within the test’s limits.

**Study lesson reference**

- Physical Education: Movement, Fitness, Safety, and Assessment — 6. Fitness, progression, and assessment
- Material ID: let-physical-education-movement-and-safety; section ID: 6-fitness-progression-and-assessment

Match a measure to the component, use the current applicable standard, and interpret the result as information for instruction. Avoid using one score as a judgment of character or a clinical conclusion. Progress activity with manageable changes, clear purpose, recovery, and supervision. ###### Question 9267

- **Question ID:** 9267
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Fitness Assessment and Safe Progression / Valid Fitness Assessment

**Stem**

A learner’s performance is below the class median on a fitness task. How should the teacher use the result?

**Options**

A. Label the learner as unfit based on the class median.
B. Display the score so peers can motivate the learner.
C. Assume the result reflects effort rather than the task conditions.
D. Interpret it against the applicable criterion and use it privately to guide an achievable learning step.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Interpret it against the applicable criterion and use it privately to guide an achievable learning step.

**Explanation as submitted:** Class rank alone is not a valid educational judgment; criterion-based evidence can guide instruction while protecting dignity.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

#### Topic: Games, Dance, and Inclusive Participation

##### Concept: Games and Tactical Understanding

###### Question 9268

- **Question ID:** 9268
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Games, Dance, and Inclusive Participation / Games and Tactical Understanding

**Stem**

In a small-sided invasion game, learners crowd around the ball and leave space open. What tactical idea should the teacher emphasize?

**Options**

A. Move to useful open space to create a passing option.
B. Run toward the ball regardless of teammate positions.
C. Wait at the boundary until the teacher gives a new rule.
D. Keep the ball with one player for the entire round.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Move to useful open space to create a passing option.

**Explanation as submitted:** Moving into open space creates a visible passing option and helps spread players across the available space. The teacher can then observe whether learners use that space to support the ball carrier.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Physical Education and Health Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/PE-and-HEALTH_CG-2023_Grade-4-and-7.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Physical Education and Health Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 5–8, 10–16, 20–21; movement foundations, developmental progression, assessment, target games and safety
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex D; MATATAG PE & Health pp. 5–8, 20–21.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Use game-centered activities to develop decisions about space, timing, teamwork, and execution.

**Study lesson reference**

- Physical Education: Movement, Fitness, Safety, and Assessment — 8. Games, rhythm, and inclusive participation
- Material ID: let-physical-education-movement-and-safety; section ID: 8-games-rhythm-and-inclusive-participation
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Games can teach decisions as well as technique. A small-sided task may help learners notice space, timing, support, and choices while receiving many opportunities to act. Dance and rhythmic activities develop coordination, expressive movement, sequence, and relationship to sound. Adapt the space, equipment, tempo, role, or rules to increase equitable participation while retaining the target. Explain why rules changed and check that the adjusted activity still provides useful challenge.

###### Question 9269

- **Question ID:** 9269
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Games, Dance, and Inclusive Participation / Games and Tactical Understanding

**Stem**

A team can pass accurately in pairs but loses possession in a game. Which practice is most relevant?

**Options**

A. Repeat stationary pairs only for the rest of the unit.
B. Use a small-sided game that adds a manageable defender and asks learners to decide when and where to pass.
C. Practice sprinting without a ball.
D. Use a full-class game with no feedback or adaptation.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Use a small-sided game that adds a manageable defender and asks learners to decide when and where to pass.

**Explanation as submitted:** A game-like task bridges technique and tactical decisions while controlling complexity.

###### Question 9270

- **Question ID:** 9270
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Games, Dance, and Inclusive Participation / Games and Tactical Understanding

**Stem**

During a target game, learners throw toward the first target they see rather than considering the score and turn. What question can improve decision-making?

**Options**

A. Who can throw the hardest from the longest distance.
B. Which target color is most attractive.
C. Which target gives your team the best opportunity on this turn, and what information supports that choice?
D. Why should every player select the same target.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Which target gives your team the best opportunity on this turn, and what information supports that choice?

**Explanation as submitted:** The question prompts learners to reason about game state and strategy rather than only execution.

###### Question 9271

- **Question ID:** 9271
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Games, Dance, and Inclusive Participation / Games and Tactical Understanding

**Stem**

A team has difficulty maintaining possession in a passing game. What adjustment can help learners practice support?

**Options**

A. Increase the number of defenders to pressure the team further.
B. Remove passing from the game.
C. Score only the fastest player’s movement.
D. Reduce the playing area or number of defenders and cue teammates to move into passing lanes.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Reduce the playing area or number of defenders and cue teammates to move into passing lanes.

**Explanation as submitted:** A modified game can make support and passing-lane decisions visible while preserving the tactical goal.

###### Question 9272

- **Question ID:** 9272
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Games, Dance, and Inclusive Participation / Games and Tactical Understanding

**Stem**

A learner performs a good pass but immediately watches the ball instead of moving. Which feedback is most relevant?

**Options**

A. Cue the learner to move into a new supporting space after passing.
B. Correct the pass even though it reached the teammate.
C. Ask the learner to stay still until the play ends.
D. Focus on the learner’s uniform color.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Cue the learner to move into a new supporting space after passing.

**Explanation as submitted:** The observed gap is an off-ball tactical action, not the completed pass.

###### Question 9273

- **Question ID:** 9273
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Games, Dance, and Inclusive Participation / Games and Tactical Understanding

**Stem**

A team repeatedly chooses an open target that is too far away for the current skill level. What should the teacher prompt?

**Options**

A. Choose the farthest target to show confidence.
B. Compare the available targets with the team’s current range and select a reachable strategic option.
C. Avoid discussing strategy and focus only on speed.
D. Have the teacher choose every target for the team.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Compare the available targets with the team’s current range and select a reachable strategic option.

**Explanation as submitted:** Tactical decisions should account for both game conditions and learners’ current performance capabilities.

##### Concept: Inclusive Activity Design

###### Question 9280

- **Question ID:** 9280
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Games, Dance, and Inclusive Participation / Inclusive Activity Design

**Stem**

A target game requires a long throw, but several learners are not yet accurate at that distance. What adaptation best supports learning?

**Options**

A. Offer closer targets or lighter equipment while keeping the aiming decision.
B. Remove aiming from the activity for those learners.
C. Score only the strongest throw.
D. Have the teacher throw for each learner.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Offer closer targets or lighter equipment while keeping the aiming decision.

**Explanation as submitted:** The adjustment changes access and difficulty while preserving the target skill.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Games, Dance, and Inclusive Participation / Inclusive Activity Design

**Stem**

A learner with limited hand mobility is excluded from a passing game because the rules require a specific grip. What should the teacher consider?

**Options**

A. Keep the rule unchanged because fairness means identical equipment.
B. Ask the learner about a workable option and adjust the equipment or pass rule while preserving the passing goal.
C. Assign the learner as a scorekeeper without asking.
D. Remove the passing objective for the entire class.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Ask the learner about a workable option and adjust the equipment or pass rule while preserving the passing goal.

**Explanation as submitted:** Meaningful adaptation can preserve the shared goal and include learner input.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex D; MATATAG PE & Health pp. 5–8, 15.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Adapt space, equipment, rules, grouping, and response options for meaningful participation.

**Study lesson reference**

- Physical Education: Movement, Fitness, Safety, and Assessment — 8. Games, rhythm, and inclusive participation
- Material ID: let-physical-education-movement-and-safety; section ID: 8-games-rhythm-and-inclusive-participation

###### Question 9282

- **Question ID:** 9282
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Games, Dance, and Inclusive Participation / Inclusive Activity Design

**Stem**

A large class cannot safely use the full court for running drills. What is a useful activity redesign?

**Options**

A. Keep all learners running across the whole court at once.
B. Ask learners to move around obstacles without changing the layout.
C. Divide learners into smaller stations with clear pathways and supervision.
D. Cancel movement practice and use only a written explanation.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Divide learners into smaller stations with clear pathways and supervision.

**Explanation as submitted:** Stations can reduce crowding and improve access and safety while keeping learners active.

###### Question 9283

- **Question ID:** 9283
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Games, Dance, and Inclusive Participation / Inclusive Activity Design

**Stem**

A game relies on visual signals that one learner cannot access consistently. What change can support participation?

**Options**

A. Remove the learner from the game.
B. Ask another learner to move the learner whenever a signal occurs.
C. Make the visual signal smaller so everyone focuses.
D. Add a clear sound or tactile cue while keeping the same start-and-stop rules.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Add a clear sound or tactile cue while keeping the same start-and-stop rules.

**Explanation as submitted:** A second cue can make game information accessible while preserving the rules and learner agency.

###### Question 9284

- **Question ID:** 9284
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Games, Dance, and Inclusive Participation / Inclusive Activity Design

**Stem**

A team activity lets only the fastest learners touch the ball. Which redesign supports equitable participation?

**Options**

A. Use a rule or task constraint that creates more opportunities for each learner to receive and pass.
B. Keep the same pattern because it rewards the strongest players.
C. Remove the ball from the activity.
D. Assign slower learners to wait outside the play area.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Use a rule or task constraint that creates more opportunities for each learner to receive and pass.

**Explanation as submitted:** A task constraint can distribute practice opportunities and preserve the team decision goal.

###### Question 9285

- **Question ID:** 9285
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Games, Dance, and Inclusive Participation / Inclusive Activity Design

**Stem**

A learner can participate in a striking game when the ball is stationary but not when it is moving quickly. What should the teacher do?

**Options**

A. Use only the fastest moving ball to match the formal game.
B. Begin with the stationary-ball version, then increase movement speed as the learner shows control.
C. Remove the striking skill and ask the learner to observe.
D. Keep the learner on the easiest version permanently.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Begin with the stationary-ball version, then increase movement speed as the learner shows control.

**Explanation as submitted:** A planned progression makes the skill accessible while retaining a route toward the shared game challenge.

##### Concept: Rhythmic Movement and Dance

###### Question 9274

- **Question ID:** 9274
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Games, Dance, and Inclusive Participation / Rhythmic Movement and Dance

**Stem**

Learners move to a steady beat using a repeated two-step pattern. What should the teacher observe?

**Options**

A. Whether learners choose the same color clothing.
B. Whether the pattern matches a teacher’s preferred style.
C. Whether learners coordinate the pattern with the beat and maintain the sequence.
D. Whether learners can name every instrument in the room.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Whether learners coordinate the pattern with the beat and maintain the sequence.

**Explanation as submitted:** The intended evidence is the timing and order of the movement. Observing those features shows whether learners connect the repeated pattern to the steady beat.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

###### Question 9275

- **Question ID:** 9275
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Games, Dance, and Inclusive Participation / Rhythmic Movement and Dance

**Stem**

A dance phrase feels uneven because learners start at different counts. What support can help?

**Options**

A. Increase the tempo so the phrase finishes faster.
B. Remove the rhythm and have learners improvise separately.
C. Score learners on facial expression before timing is practiced.
D. Use a clear count-in and practice the opening cue before repeating the phrase.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Use a clear count-in and practice the opening cue before repeating the phrase.

**Explanation as submitted:** A shared count-in gives the group a common start cue. Rehearsing that cue first helps isolate the timing problem before the class repeats the full phrase.

###### Question 9276

- **Question ID:** 9276
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Games, Dance, and Inclusive Participation / Rhythmic Movement and Dance

**Stem**

A class creates a movement sequence using high and low levels. Which prompt develops movement concepts?

**Options**

A. How does changing level alter the shape and space used in the sequence?
B. Which learner reaches the highest level.
C. Why should every movement stay at one level.
D. How many shoes are visible in the dance space.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. How does changing level alter the shape and space used in the sequence?

**Explanation as submitted:** Level and shape are observable movement qualities that connect to space and expression.

###### Question 9277

- **Question ID:** 9277
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Games, Dance, and Inclusive Participation / Rhythmic Movement and Dance

**Stem**

A learner keeps the beat but cannot remember a long dance sequence. What is a useful teaching adjustment?

**Options**

A. Repeat the full sequence faster until it is memorized.
B. Teach the sequence in short phrases and connect them with a consistent transition cue.
C. Remove the rhythm so learners can focus only on the teacher.
D. Have a peer perform while the learner watches each time.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Teach the sequence in short phrases and connect them with a consistent transition cue.

**Explanation as submitted:** Short phrases reduce memory load while the learner practices the full movement sequence progressively.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

- **Question ID:** 9278
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Games, Dance, and Inclusive Participation / Rhythmic Movement and Dance

**Stem**

Learners create different movement responses to the same musical phrase. What should guide a fair assessment?

**Options**

A. Score only the response that copies the teacher exactly.
B. Rank movements by how familiar they look.
C. Use the taught criteria for rhythm, movement quality, and interpretation rather than requiring identical choreography.
D. Ignore the musical phrase and assess costume design.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Use the taught criteria for rhythm, movement quality, and interpretation rather than requiring identical choreography.

**Explanation as submitted:** Learners can express a shared prompt in varied ways while meeting the same learning criteria.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Physical Education and Health Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/PE-and-HEALTH_CG-2023_Grade-4-and-7.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Physical Education and Health Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 5–8, 10–16, 20–21; movement foundations, developmental progression, assessment, target games and safety
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex D; MATATAG PE & Health pp. 5–8, 10–11.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Connect movement qualities and sequences to rhythm, expression, and safe participation.

**Study lesson reference**

- Physical Education: Movement, Fitness, Safety, and Assessment — 8. Games, rhythm, and inclusive participation
- Material ID: let-physical-education-movement-and-safety; section ID: 8-games-rhythm-and-inclusive-participation
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Games can teach decisions as well as technique. A small-sided task may help learners notice space, timing, support, and choices while receiving many opportunities to act. Dance and rhythmic activities develop coordination, expressive movement, sequence, and relationship to sound. Adapt the space, equipment, tempo, role, or rules to increase equitable participation while retaining the target. Explain why rules changed and check that the adjusted activity still provides useful challenge.

###### Question 9279

- **Question ID:** 9279
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Games, Dance, and Inclusive Participation / Rhythmic Movement and Dance

**Stem**

A dance sequence includes a rapid turn that some learners cannot safely perform in the available space. What is the strongest design response?

**Options**

A. Keep the original turn and change speed only after a learner loses balance.
B. Replace all movement assessment with a written reflection.
C. Preserve the turn by shortening the sequence without checking space or access.
D. Adapt the turn or spacing, preserving the rhythm goal and safe participation.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Adapt the turn or spacing, preserving the rhythm goal and safe participation.

**Explanation as submitted:** The teacher can preserve the learning intention while adapting movement and space to conditions. The revised task should still provide evidence of the intended rhythm or movement concept.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

#### Topic: Movement Analysis and Motor Learning

##### Concept: Fundamental Movement Analysis

###### Question 9238

- **Question ID:** 9238
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Movement Analysis and Motor Learning / Fundamental Movement Analysis

**Stem**

A learner can move toward a target but repeatedly steps outside the marked lane. Which observation lens best identifies the issue?

**Options**

A. Measure the learner’s heart rate after the activity.
B. Assess the learner’s ability to name the target color.
C. Examine the learner’s pathway and spatial relationship to the lane.
D. Compare the learner’s uniform with classmates’ uniforms.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Examine the learner’s pathway and spatial relationship to the lane.

**Explanation as submitted:** Pathway and space are movement concepts that directly relate to staying within the lane.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Physical Education and Health Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/PE-and-HEALTH_CG-2023_Grade-4-and-7.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Physical Education and Health Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 5–8, 10–16, 20–21; movement foundations, developmental progression, assessment, target games and safety
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex D; MATATAG PE & Health pp. 5–8, 20–21.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Observe movement through body, effort, space, and relationships and select a useful instructional focus.

**Study lesson reference**

- Physical Education: Movement, Fitness, Safety, and Assessment — 5. Movement analysis and motor learning
- Material ID: let-physical-education-movement-and-safety; section ID: 5-movement-analysis-and-motor-learning
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): The DepEd PE guide describes movement through body, effort, space, and relationships. These lenses help a teacher observe a skill instead of giving vague feedback such as “try harder.” For a throwing task, the teacher might notice the learner’s stance, force, direction, and relation to a target. Select the most teachable feature, use a concise cue, and let the learner try again. Movement learning develops through practice and feedback; one observed attempt is limited evidence.

###### Question 9239

- **Question ID:** 9239
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Movement Analysis and Motor Learning / Fundamental Movement Analysis

**Stem**

A teacher observes a learner throw with good balance but inconsistent force. Which feedback focus is most useful?

**Options**

A. Correct the learner’s entire throwing sequence at once.
B. Change the target size before identifying the source of inconsistency.
C. Ask the learner to run faster before each throw.
D. Use a cue related to effort or force and observe the next attempt.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Use a cue related to effort or force and observe the next attempt.

**Explanation as submitted:** Effort or force is the movement feature most directly connected to the observed inconsistency.
