###### Question 9240

- **Question ID:** 9240
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Movement Analysis and Motor Learning / Fundamental Movement Analysis

**Stem**

A learner weaves through cones successfully while stationary but loses control when a partner moves nearby. What feature has changed?

**Options**

A. The movement relationship to another person and a changing space demand.
B. The learner’s knowledge of the cone colors.
C. The floor texture, even though the surface is unchanged.
D. The learner’s ability to count repetitions.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. The movement relationship to another person and a changing space demand.

**Explanation as submitted:** A moving partner changes spatial relationships and requires the learner to adapt movement.

###### Question 9241

- **Question ID:** 9241
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Movement Analysis and Motor Learning / Fundamental Movement Analysis

**Stem**

In a balance task, a learner stays upright on a wide base but wobbles when the base narrows. What is a useful next teaching focus?

**Options**

A. Increase speed so the learner has less time to notice the movement.
B. Let the learner compare a manageable base of support and notice how body position affects balance.
C. Add equipment before the learner can control the simpler task.
D. Score balance only by comparing the learner with the most stable peer.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Let the learner compare a manageable base of support and notice how body position affects balance.

**Explanation as submitted:** The changed base offers evidence about balance; a manageable progression helps the learner explore control.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9242

- **Question ID:** 9242
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Movement Analysis and Motor Learning / Fundamental Movement Analysis

**Stem**

A learner’s movement becomes less accurate when a ball is larger than the one used in practice. What should the teacher analyze?

**Options**

A. Whether the learner memorized the activity name.
B. Whether the learner should stop practicing with objects.
C. How the object changes the movement relationship and whether practice should progress across object sizes.
D. Whether the class should use only the largest ball.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. How the object changes the movement relationship and whether practice should progress across object sizes.

**Explanation as submitted:** The object and body relationship changes the task demands; varied, planned practice can support adaptation.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9243

- **Question ID:** 9243
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Movement Analysis and Motor Learning / Fundamental Movement Analysis

**Stem**

A student can perform a movement pattern in a straight line but not when asked to change direction on a cue. Which learning focus is most relevant?

**Options**

A. Replace the pattern with an unrelated endurance task.
B. Increase the number of cues before the student practices.
C. Assess only whether the student remembers the pattern’s name.
D. Practice the same movement with controlled direction changes and attend to space and timing cues.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Practice the same movement with controlled direction changes and attend to space and timing cues.

**Explanation as submitted:** The learner needs to apply the pattern under a changing space and timing demand.

##### Concept: Motor Learning Feedback

###### Question 9244

- **Question ID:** 9244
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Movement Analysis and Motor Learning / Motor Learning Feedback

**Stem**

A learner’s landing is unstable after a jump. Which cue is most useful for another practice attempt?

**Options**

A. “Land quietly with control, then hold your balance.”
B. “Try harder and do everything better.”
C. “Look at the scoreboard while you land.”
D. “Jump as far as possible on every attempt.”

**Proposed correct answer:** A. “Land quietly with control, then hold your balance.”

**Explanation as submitted:** A concise cue directs attention to a controllable feature linked to the observed skill.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

###### Question 9245

- **Question ID:** 9245
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Movement Analysis and Motor Learning / Motor Learning Feedback

**Stem**

A student misses a target repeatedly, and the teacher notices that the release point varies. What feedback should come first?

**Options**

A. Correct the student’s stance, grip, speed, and follow-through at once.
B. Give one cue about a consistent release point and observe whether accuracy changes.
C. Tell the student the target is too difficult without checking the release.
D. Demonstrate the whole skill again without identifying the observed variation.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Give one cue about a consistent release point and observe whether accuracy changes.

**Explanation as submitted:** One focused cue helps the learner attend to the feature most related to the difficulty.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [MATATAG Physical Education and Health Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7](https://www.deped.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/PE-and-HEALTH_CG-2023_Grade-4-and-7.pdf) — Department of Education, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; MATATAG Physical Education and Health Curriculum Guide, Grades 4 and 7
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 5–8, 10–16, 20–21; movement foundations, developmental progression, assessment, target games and safety
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex D; MATATAG PE & Health pp. 5–8.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Select a manageable cue and use practice evidence to adjust instruction and feedback.

**Study lesson reference**

- Physical Education: Movement, Fitness, Safety, and Assessment — 5. Movement analysis and motor learning
- Material ID: let-physical-education-movement-and-safety; section ID: 5-movement-analysis-and-motor-learning
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): The DepEd PE guide describes movement through body, effort, space, and relationships. These lenses help a teacher observe a skill instead of giving vague feedback such as “try harder.” For a throwing task, the teacher might notice the learner’s stance, force, direction, and relation to a target. Select the most teachable feature, use a concise cue, and let the learner try again. Movement learning develops through practice and feedback; one observed attempt is limited evidence.

###### Question 9246

- **Question ID:** 9246
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Movement Analysis and Motor Learning / Motor Learning Feedback

**Stem**

A learner performs a skill better after a demonstration than after a verbal explanation alone. What should the teacher plan?

**Options**

A. Use only more verbal explanation because modeling takes time.
B. Show the model repeatedly without allowing practice.
C. Combine a brief visible model with a clear cue, then allow practice and observe performance.
D. Assess whether the learner can describe the model rather than perform the skill.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Combine a brief visible model with a clear cue, then allow practice and observe performance.

**Explanation as submitted:** A model can make movement visible, but practice is needed to assess skill learning.

###### Question 9247

- **Question ID:** 9247
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Movement Analysis and Motor Learning / Motor Learning Feedback

**Stem**

A learner receives several corrections before each attempt and becomes unsure which to use. What should the teacher change?

**Options**

A. Increase the number of corrections so the learner has more information.
B. Stop giving feedback throughout the unit.
C. Have a peer give a different cue before every attempt.
D. Select one priority cue, let the learner practice, and add another cue only if needed.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Select one priority cue, let the learner practice, and add another cue only if needed.

**Explanation as submitted:** Too many cues can overload attention; a focused cue supports purposeful practice.

###### Question 9248

- **Question ID:** 9248
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Movement Analysis and Motor Learning / Motor Learning Feedback

**Stem**

A group improves a passing drill when the target is made visible. How should the teacher use this evidence?

**Options**

A. Keep the visual target while varying one manageable condition and observe whether accuracy transfers.
B. Remove the target immediately and assume the skill is learned.
C. Keep the drill unchanged for the remainder of the unit.
D. Change multiple conditions before checking the current learning.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Keep the visual target while varying one manageable condition and observe whether accuracy transfers.

**Explanation as submitted:** A visual target helps; gradual variation tests transfer while keeping the task interpretable.

###### Question 9249

- **Question ID:** 9249
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Physical Education / Movement Analysis and Motor Learning / Motor Learning Feedback

**Stem**

A learner succeeds only after a teacher repeats the cue during every movement. What should the teacher do to check independent learning?

**Options**

A. Keep repeating the cue indefinitely and record each attempt as independent.
B. Gradually reduce the cue and observe whether the learner can perform under the intended task conditions.
C. Stop all practice because the learner has not learned the skill.
D. Have a classmate move the learner’s body into position.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Gradually reduce the cue and observe whether the learner can perform under the intended task conditions.

**Explanation as submitted:** Fading prompts reveals whether the learner can use the skill with less support.

### Subject: Special Needs Education

#### Topic: Accessible Assessment and Individualized Planning

##### Concept: Assessment Accommodation and Validity

###### Question 9302

- **Question ID:** 9302
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Accessible Assessment and Individualized Planning / Assessment Accommodation and Validity

**Stem**

A science quiz assesses understanding of water-cycle stages, but the printed diagram is too small for a learner to access. Which support is most appropriate?

**Options**

A. Replace the quiz with an unrelated activity about classroom rules.
B. Score only the learner’s handwriting rather than the science response.
C. Provide an accessible enlarged or digital diagram while keeping the same water-cycle reasoning task.
D. Ask a classmate to choose the stages for the learner.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Provide an accessible enlarged or digital diagram while keeping the same water-cycle reasoning task.

**Explanation as submitted:** The diagram access barrier can be removed without changing the science construct or another person answering.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [Republic Act No. 11650: Inclusive Education Act](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/2/94483) — Republic of the Philippines; Supreme Court E-Library; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; Republic Act No. 11650
  - Source-level relevant sections: Sections 2–5, including inclusion policy, support/related services, general education access and individualized plans
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex B; RA 11650.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Remove irrelevant access barriers while preserving evidence of the intended competency.

**Study lesson reference**

- Inclusive Education: Access, Participation, and Support — 6. Assessment validity and accommodations
- Material ID: let-special-needs-inclusive-education; section ID: 6-assessment-validity-and-accommodations
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): First name the construct and the evidence that would demonstrate it. Then remove conditions that are irrelevant to that construct, such as inaccessible print when the target is mathematical reasoning. A reader, enlarged text, additional processing time, or alternative response tool may be appropriate depending on the learner and authorized plan. The same support is not automatically valid for every task. A support that changes the competency being measured must be recognized and interpreted as a modified outcome, not reported as equivalent evidence.

**Review form — leave each field PENDING until reviewed**

- DISTRACTOR_QUALITY: PENDING
- CURRICULUM_ALIGNMENT: PENDING

###### Question 9303

- **Question ID:** 9303
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Accessible Assessment and Individualized Planning / Assessment Accommodation and Validity

**Stem**

An assessment measures map-reading but requires extensive copying of labels. A learner can identify locations accurately by pointing. What is the strongest decision?

**Options**

A. Keep copying as part of the test even if writing is not in the map-reading objective.
B. Give the learner the answer key because the copying is difficult.
C. Change the target to coloring rather than location identification.
D. Permit pointing or a prepared marker response if it still reveals the learner’s map-reading knowledge.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Permit pointing or a prepared marker response if it still reveals the learner’s map-reading knowledge.

**Explanation as submitted:** An alternate response mode may preserve the map-reading construct when handwriting is incidental.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

###### Question 9304

- **Question ID:** 9304
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Accessible Assessment and Individualized Planning / Assessment Accommodation and Validity

**Stem**

A teacher allows a communication device during an assessment of a learner’s ideas. What should the teacher verify?

**Options**

A. That the device supports the learner’s response and does not supply the content being assessed.
B. That the device is removed before scoring so answers look uniform.
C. That a support person chooses the ideas on the learner’s behalf.
D. That the device changes the learning objective to technology use.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. That the device supports the learner’s response and does not supply the content being assessed.

**Explanation as submitted:** The support should provide access to expression while the learner supplies the evidence for the target.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

###### Question 9305

- **Question ID:** 9305
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Accessible Assessment and Individualized Planning / Assessment Accommodation and Validity

**Stem**

A learner receives additional processing time on a reading-comprehension task. Which interpretation is most appropriate?

**Options**

A. The result is automatically invalid because time was adjusted.
B. The teacher can interpret comprehension evidence while noting the assessment condition and applicable plan.
C. The added time proves the learner has mastered every reading skill.
D. The teacher should compare the learner only with scores from untimed tests.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. The teacher can interpret comprehension evidence while noting the assessment condition and applicable plan.

**Explanation as submitted:** Time can be an access condition; interpretation should consider the target and document how evidence was obtained.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

###### Question 9306

- **Question ID:** 9306
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Accessible Assessment and Individualized Planning / Assessment Accommodation and Validity

**Stem**

A practical task assesses safe tool handling. The learner is offered a verbal explanation instead of performing the handling steps. What must the teacher consider?

**Options**

A. Whether the learner’s explanation sounds confident.
B. Whether all learners can submit a written report instead.
C. Whether the verbal response demonstrates the intended performance or changes the task from doing to describing.
D. Whether the task can be removed from the assessment without review.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Whether the verbal response demonstrates the intended performance or changes the task from doing to describing.

**Explanation as submitted:** If the target is actual tool handling, explaining the procedure may not show the required performance.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [Republic Act No. 11650: Inclusive Education Act](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/2/94483) — Republic of the Philippines; Supreme Court E-Library; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; Republic Act No. 11650
  - Source-level relevant sections: Sections 2–5, including inclusion policy, support/related services, general education access and individualized plans
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex B; RA 11650.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Remove irrelevant access barriers while preserving evidence of the intended competency.

**Study lesson reference**

- Inclusive Education: Access, Participation, and Support — 6. Assessment validity and accommodations
- Material ID: let-special-needs-inclusive-education; section ID: 6-assessment-validity-and-accommodations
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): First name the construct and the evidence that would demonstrate it. Then remove conditions that are irrelevant to that construct, such as inaccessible print when the target is mathematical reasoning. A reader, enlarged text, additional processing time, or alternative response tool may be appropriate depending on the learner and authorized plan. The same support is not automatically valid for every task. A support that changes the competency being measured must be recognized and interpreted as a modified outcome, not reported as equivalent evidence.

###### Question 9307

- **Question ID:** 9307
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Accessible Assessment and Individualized Planning / Assessment Accommodation and Validity

**Stem**

A support makes a reading passage accessible but an item asks learners to decode the exact printed word aloud. What is the key assessment question?

**Options**

A. Whether the support is convenient for the teacher to administer.
B. Whether the learner has used the same support in another subject.
C. Whether every assessment must use identical materials.
D. Whether the support preserves access to the intended construct or changes the decoding demand named in the item.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Whether the support preserves access to the intended construct or changes the decoding demand named in the item.

**Explanation as submitted:** An accommodation is appropriate only in relation to the construct; decoding itself may be the target in this item.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

##### Concept: Collaborative Learner and Family Planning

###### Question 9314

- **Question ID:** 9314
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Accessible Assessment and Individualized Planning / Collaborative Learner and Family Planning

**Stem**

A learner explains that written instructions are hard to follow but pictures help. How should this information enter planning?

**Options**

A. Dismiss the view until a standardized assessment confirms it.
B. Ask the learner’s family to decide without the learner’s input.
C. Record the learner’s view and check which visual support helps with the actual classroom tasks.
D. Use pictures in every task without checking their fit.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Record the learner’s view and check which visual support helps with the actual classroom tasks.

**Explanation as submitted:** Learner voice is useful planning evidence, and the support should be tested against the learning context.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [Republic Act No. 11650: Inclusive Education Act](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/2/94483) — Republic of the Philippines; Supreme Court E-Library; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; Republic Act No. 11650
  - Source-level relevant sections: Sections 2–5, including inclusion policy, support/related services, general education access and individualized plans
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex B; RA 11650.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Use learner and family expertise and clarify shared roles in planning educational support.

**Study lesson reference**

- Inclusive Education: Access, Participation, and Support — 7. Individualized planning and collaboration
- Material ID: let-special-needs-inclusive-education; section ID: 7-individualized-planning-and-collaboration
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Individual planning draws on comprehensive assessment and input from people who know the learner and the learning context. State the learner’s strengths and needs in observable terms, select a functional goal, assign supports and responsibilities, and decide what evidence will show progress. Learner and family knowledge is part of the evidence, not an afterthought. The PRC TOS includes IEP, IFSP, BIP, and ITP planning; use the applicable plan and authorized team rather than treating these names as interchangeable paperwork.

###### Question 9315

- **Question ID:** 9315
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Accessible Assessment and Individualized Planning / Collaborative Learner and Family Planning

**Stem**

A family reports that a learner uses a schedule successfully at home. What is the most productive school response?

**Options**

A. Require the family to provide the same schedule materials for every class.
B. Assume the home schedule will work unchanged in every school setting.
C. Ignore the information because home practice is unrelated to learning.
D. Ask how the schedule is used and explore whether an adapted version could support school routines.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Ask how the schedule is used and explore whether an adapted version could support school routines.

**Explanation as submitted:** Family knowledge can inform a school support, but the team should adapt and evaluate it in context.

###### Question 9316

- **Question ID:** 9316
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Accessible Assessment and Individualized Planning / Collaborative Learner and Family Planning

**Stem**

A speech-language specialist recommends a communication support for classroom discussion. What is the teacher’s role?

**Options**

A. Coordinate classroom use with the specialist and learner, then observe whether it supports participation.
B. Diagnose the learner’s communication condition independently.
C. Leave the support unused because it was not chosen by the teacher.
D. Ask the specialist to lead every classroom discussion.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Coordinate classroom use with the specialist and learner, then observe whether it supports participation.

**Explanation as submitted:** The teacher collaborates on classroom implementation while staying within professional roles.

###### Question 9317

- **Question ID:** 9317
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Accessible Assessment and Individualized Planning / Collaborative Learner and Family Planning

**Stem**

A team is creating a behavior support plan. Which information should be brought to the discussion?

**Options**

A. A list of character labels assigned by several adults.
B. Observable patterns across situations, the learner’s communication and strengths, and input from people who know the context.
C. Only the most recent incident without any context.
D. A consequence selected before identifying what the learner needs.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Observable patterns across situations, the learner’s communication and strengths, and input from people who know the context.

**Explanation as submitted:** Comprehensive contextual evidence supports an individualized positive plan; labels do not explain the situation.

###### Question 9318

- **Question ID:** 9318
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Accessible Assessment and Individualized Planning / Collaborative Learner and Family Planning

**Stem**

Two teachers disagree about whether a visual checklist helps a learner begin a task. What is the best way to resolve the disagreement?

**Options**

A. Let the more senior teacher decide without gathering evidence.
B. Keep the checklist permanently because one teacher prefers it.
C. Agree on a brief observation plan and compare task-start evidence with and without the checklist under appropriate conditions.
D. Ask the learner to choose which teacher is correct.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Agree on a brief observation plan and compare task-start evidence with and without the checklist under appropriate conditions.

**Explanation as submitted:** A shared, respectful evidence check can clarify whether the support improves the intended participation.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9319

- **Question ID:** 9319
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Accessible Assessment and Individualized Planning / Collaborative Learner and Family Planning

**Stem**

A new classroom team receives an old plan but no record of which supports the learner found useful. What should the team do first?

**Options**

A. Apply every old support exactly as written without checking current fit.
B. Discard the plan because the previous team has changed.
C. Ask the learner to select every service without team discussion.
D. Review current information with the learner and family, confirm applicable plan roles, and observe supports in the new setting.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Review current information with the learner and family, confirm applicable plan roles, and observe supports in the new setting.

**Explanation as submitted:** Support should be continuous but responsive; current learner and family input plus observation helps the team confirm fit.

##### Concept: Data-Informed Individualized Planning

###### Question 9308

- **Question ID:** 9308
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Accessible Assessment and Individualized Planning / Data-Informed Individualized Planning

**Stem**

A learner’s reading score is low on one unfamiliar passage, while class observations show strong understanding during oral discussion. What should guide an individualized planning meeting?

**Options**

A. Review both sources and gather additional evidence about reading access and comprehension before selecting support.
B. Use the single score as the complete description of the learner.
C. Ignore the score because oral discussion was stronger.
D. Choose a support based only on the learner’s grade level.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Review both sources and gather additional evidence about reading access and comprehension before selecting support.

**Explanation as submitted:** Multiple sources can reveal whether the barrier is decoding, access, context, or comprehension; more evidence supports sound planning.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [Republic Act No. 11650: Inclusive Education Act](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/2/94483) — Republic of the Philippines; Supreme Court E-Library; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; Republic Act No. 11650
  - Source-level relevant sections: Sections 2–5, including inclusion policy, support/related services, general education access and individualized plans
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex B; RA 11650.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Combine comprehensive assessment and team input when selecting individualized learner support.

- Inclusive Education: Access, Participation, and Support — 7. Individualized planning and collaboration

###### Question 9309

- **Question ID:** 9309
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Accessible Assessment and Individualized Planning / Data-Informed Individualized Planning

**Stem**

A team is planning support for a learner who wants to read short instructions independently. Which goal is most useful?

**Options**

A. Write “improve reading” without a measurable behavior or review point.
B. State an observable reading action, the conditions or material, and how progress will be checked.
C. Set a goal that the learner will work harder than classmates.
D. Choose a support before identifying the reading task.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. State an observable reading action, the conditions or material, and how progress will be checked.

**Explanation as submitted:** A functional goal connects the learner’s priority to observable evidence and planned support.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

###### Question 9310

- **Question ID:** 9310
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Accessible Assessment and Individualized Planning / Data-Informed Individualized Planning

**Stem**

Assessment and family input both indicate that a learner participates more when instructions are broken into steps. What should the plan specify?

**Options**

A. That the learner should receive help whenever an adult remembers.
B. That the family should deliver all classroom directions.
C. The step format, when staff will use it, and what evidence will show whether it improves participation.
D. That the learner should stop attempting multi-step work.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. The step format, when staff will use it, and what evidence will show whether it improves participation.

**Explanation as submitted:** A useful plan names the support, responsibility, and evidence for review.

###### Question 9311

- **Question ID:** 9311
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Accessible Assessment and Individualized Planning / Data-Informed Individualized Planning

**Stem**

A learner meets a goal during one-to-one work but not during group work. What should the team examine?

**Options**

A. Whether the learner should lose access to group work.
B. Whether one-to-one success proves the group result is irrelevant.
C. Whether the learner should repeat the same one-to-one task indefinitely.
D. Differences in group directions, timing, noise, peer interaction, and available supports across settings.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Differences in group directions, timing, noise, peer interaction, and available supports across settings.

**Explanation as submitted:** Performance varies with setting; examine those conditions before deciding what support is needed.

###### Question 9312

- **Question ID:** 9312
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Accessible Assessment and Individualized Planning / Data-Informed Individualized Planning

**Stem**

A learner is highly motivated by a hands-on science task but has difficulty with lengthy written directions. Which plan is strongest?

**Options**

A. Use the learner’s strength to introduce the science task and make the directions accessible in clear, manageable steps.
B. Replace science learning with a handwriting intervention.
C. Remove the hands-on task because the directions are difficult.
D. Use the same long directions and wait for the learner to request help.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Use the learner’s strength to introduce the science task and make the directions accessible in clear, manageable steps.

**Explanation as submitted:** Planning can build on learner strengths while addressing the specific access barrier.

###### Question 9313

- **Question ID:** 9313
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Accessible Assessment and Individualized Planning / Data-Informed Individualized Planning

**Stem**

A support has been used consistently for several weeks, but repeated goal-aligned data show no change in participation. What should the team do?

**Options**

A. Keep the support unchanged because it is written in the plan.
B. Use the evidence and learner input to revise the support, then monitor again.
C. Conclude the learner is unwilling to learn.
D. Remove the goal without reviewing the evidence.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Use the evidence and learner input to revise the support, then monitor again.

**Explanation as submitted:** Lack of progress is a reason for collaborative review and adjustment, not blame or unexamined continuation. Confirm how the support was implemented, consider barriers and learner perspective, then document the next plan.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

#### Topic: Positive Support and Participation

##### Concept: Dignity, Choice, and Learner Voice

###### Question 9338

- **Question ID:** 9338
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Dignity, Choice, and Learner Voice

**Stem**

A learner’s support plan is being discussed in front of classmates. What should the teacher do?

**Options**

A. Continue because classmates may be curious about the plan.
B. Ask the learner to explain the plan to the group.
C. Move the discussion to an appropriate private setting and share only information needed for support.
D. Post the plan so substitute teachers can find it easily.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Move the discussion to an appropriate private setting and share only information needed for support.

**Explanation as submitted:** Learner privacy and dignity require appropriate confidentiality and limited sharing.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [Republic Act No. 11650: Inclusive Education Act](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/2/94483) — Republic of the Philippines; Supreme Court E-Library; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; Republic Act No. 11650
  - Source-level relevant sections: Sections 2–5, including inclusion policy, support/related services, general education access and individualized plans
- [CAST Universal Design for Learning Guidelines, Version 3.0](https://udlguidelines.cast.org/) — CAST; PROFESSIONAL_GUIDANCE; UDL Guidelines 3.0
  - Source-level relevant sections: Version 3.0, design options for engagement, representation, action/expression, accessible materials/technology, communication and learner strategy
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex B; RA 11650; CAST UDL 3.0.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Protect agency, privacy, choice, and meaningful participation in support decisions.

**Study lesson reference**

- Inclusive Education: Access, Participation, and Support — 10. Progress, transition, and learner voice
- Material ID: let-special-needs-inclusive-education; section ID: 10-progress-transition-and-learner-voice
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Progress monitoring uses repeated, goal-aligned evidence rather than impressions. Look for change over time, check whether the measure captures the intended skill, and adjust instruction when progress stalls. A planned transition identifies what information and supports need to travel with the learner and which receiving people need to coordinate. Invite learner choice and explain support in respectful language. The aim is participation, self-advocacy, learning, and access to the wider community, not compliance for its own sake.

###### Question 9339

- **Question ID:** 9339
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Dignity, Choice, and Learner Voice

**Stem**

A learner is offered two accessible ways to complete a project and chooses one. What should the teacher do next?

**Options**

A. Replace the choice with the teacher’s preferred method.
B. Assume the learner chose incorrectly because it differs from peers.
C. Remove the project because learner choice was offered.
D. Confirm that the chosen method supports the learning goal and let the learner proceed with planned support.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Confirm that the chosen method supports the learning goal and let the learner proceed with planned support.

**Explanation as submitted:** Choice supports agency when the option remains aligned with the learning intention.

###### Question 9340

- **Question ID:** 9340
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Dignity, Choice, and Learner Voice

**Stem**

A learner uses a communication device to answer, but an adult routinely responds for them. What should the teacher do?

**Options**

A. Address the learner directly, allow response time, and use the adult as support only where needed.
B. Continue speaking to the adult because it is faster.
C. Ask the adult to choose the learner’s preferred response.
D. Stop asking the learner questions during class.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Address the learner directly, allow response time, and use the adult as support only where needed.

**Explanation as submitted:** The learner is the communicator; the teacher should preserve direct participation and agency.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9341

- **Question ID:** 9341
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Dignity, Choice, and Learner Voice

**Stem**

A student declines to discuss a personal experience during a lesson on identity. Which response is most respectful?

**Options**

A. Require the student to share because personal reflection is useful.
B. Accept the choice and offer a different example or fictional scenario that meets the learning objective.
C. Ask peers to persuade the student to participate.
D. Remove the student from the lesson.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Accept the choice and offer a different example or fictional scenario that meets the learning objective.

**Explanation as submitted:** Learner voice includes the ability not to disclose private experience; another route can meet the objective.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

###### Question 9342

- **Question ID:** 9342
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Dignity, Choice, and Learner Voice

**Stem**

A teacher is unsure which disability-related term a learner prefers. What is the best action?

**Options**

A. Choose a label based on what other staff use for different learners.
B. Avoid speaking with the learner about their preferences.
C. Ask the learner or family respectfully and use the preferred language in the appropriate context.
D. Use a euphemism that obscures the learner’s support needs.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Ask the learner or family respectfully and use the preferred language in the appropriate context.

**Explanation as submitted:** Contemporary respectful practice uses the learner’s or family’s preference without assuming one term fits everyone.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

  - Source-level relevant sections: Sections 2–5, including inclusion policy, support/related services, general education access and individualized plans
- [CAST Universal Design for Learning Guidelines, Version 3.0](https://udlguidelines.cast.org/) — CAST; PROFESSIONAL_GUIDANCE; UDL Guidelines 3.0
  - Source-level relevant sections: Version 3.0, design options for engagement, representation, action/expression, accessible materials/technology, communication and learner strategy
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex B; RA 11650; CAST UDL 3.0.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Protect agency, privacy, choice, and meaningful participation in support decisions.

**Study lesson reference**

- Inclusive Education: Access, Participation, and Support — 10. Progress, transition, and learner voice
- Material ID: let-special-needs-inclusive-education; section ID: 10-progress-transition-and-learner-voice
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Progress monitoring uses repeated, goal-aligned evidence rather than impressions. Look for change over time, check whether the measure captures the intended skill, and adjust instruction when progress stalls. A planned transition identifies what information and supports need to travel with the learner and which receiving people need to coordinate. Invite learner choice and explain support in respectful language. The aim is participation, self-advocacy, learning, and access to the wider community, not compliance for its own sake.

###### Question 9343

- **Question ID:** 9343
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Dignity, Choice, and Learner Voice

**Stem**

A learner requests a break during a challenging activity, and the request is part of the learner’s plan. Which response best balances dignity and participation?

**Options**

A. Deny the request until the learner completes the entire activity.
B. End the learner’s participation for the rest of the day.
C. Announce the request to the class as a behavior lesson.
D. Honor the planned break, clarify how the learner can rejoin, and review whether the task remains accessible.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Honor the planned break, clarify how the learner can rejoin, and review whether the task remains accessible.

**Explanation as submitted:** The planned support respects communication and regulation while maintaining a clear, supportive route back to learning.

##### Concept: Positive Behavior Support

###### Question 9320

- **Question ID:** 9320
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Positive Behavior Support

**Stem**

A learner leaves a seat during long verbal directions but remains engaged when instructions are shown in short steps. What should the teacher examine first?

**Options**

A. Whether the direction format and task duration create barriers that can be adjusted.
B. Whether the learner should lose access to the activity.
C. Whether leaving the seat proves the learner is choosing to misbehave.
D. Whether peers should be asked to keep the learner seated.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Whether the direction format and task duration create barriers that can be adjusted.

**Explanation as submitted:** The difference across conditions suggests that task access and instruction may affect participation.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [Republic Act No. 11650: Inclusive Education Act](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/2/94483) — Republic of the Philippines; Supreme Court E-Library; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; Republic Act No. 11650
  - Source-level relevant sections: Sections 2–5, including inclusion policy, support/related services, general education access and individualized plans
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex B; RA 11650.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Understand behavior in context and teach/support safer, more effective participation without aversive practice.

**Study lesson reference**

- Inclusive Education: Access, Participation, and Support — 8. Positive behavior and environment
- Material ID: let-special-needs-inclusive-education; section ID: 8-positive-behavior-and-environment
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Support behavior by asking what the learner is communicating and what conditions precede or follow it. Review task difficulty, access to communication, predictability, sensory demands, peer interaction, and reinforcement. Teach a replacement skill that meets the same need more safely, such as requesting a break or asking for help. Adjust the environment and reinforce successful participation. The PRC scope specifically rejects aversive techniques; restraint or punishment is not a teaching strategy. Follow current school safeguarding and support procedures.

###### Question 9321

- **Question ID:** 9321
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Positive Behavior Support

**Stem**

A learner sometimes shouts when unable to get a turn with shared equipment. Which support is most constructive?

**Options**

A. Remove all shared equipment so the learner cannot request a turn.
B. Teach and practice a clear way to request a turn, then reinforce the communication when it occurs.
C. Use public reprimands each time the learner calls out.
D. Wait for shouting to stop without providing another communication option.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Teach and practice a clear way to request a turn, then reinforce the communication when it occurs.

**Explanation as submitted:** Teaching a replacement communication response addresses participation needs and avoids punitive escalation.

###### Question 9322

- **Question ID:** 9322
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Positive Behavior Support

**Stem**

A staff member proposes an aversive technique to stop a learner’s repeated calling out. What is the appropriate professional response?

**Options**

A. Try the technique briefly before notifying the team.
B. Use it only when other learners are watching.
C. Use positive, evidence-based support and follow the applicable learner plan and school procedures.
D. Make it part of the classroom routine for everyone.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Use positive, evidence-based support and follow the applicable learner plan and school procedures.

**Explanation as submitted:** The current PRC TOS calls for positive behavior support and excludes aversive techniques.

###### Question 9323

- **Question ID:** 9323
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Positive Behavior Support

**Stem**

A learner asks for help by pushing materials away because the help symbol is not within reach. Which adjustment is useful?

**Options**

A. Remove the materials whenever the learner pushes them away.
B. Wait for the learner to use spoken words before providing help.
C. Ask another student to decide whether the learner needs assistance.
D. Make the communication support available and teach or prompt its use during the task.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Make the communication support available and teach or prompt its use during the task.

**Explanation as submitted:** Access to a communication response can replace a behavior that currently signals difficulty.

###### Question 9324

- **Question ID:** 9324
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Positive Behavior Support

**Stem**

A learner’s participation drops when a task changes without warning. What is the strongest support to try?

**Options**

A. Use a predictable transition cue and a short preview, then observe whether engagement improves.
B. Increase surprise changes so the learner becomes accustomed to them.
C. Remove the next task without explaining what follows.
D. Treat reduced participation as evidence of low motivation.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Use a predictable transition cue and a short preview, then observe whether engagement improves.

**Explanation as submitted:** A clear preview may reduce an avoidable transition barrier; monitor its effect.

###### Question 9325

- **Question ID:** 9325
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Positive Behavior Support

**Stem**

A learner repeatedly taps materials during independent work, but the behavior stops during hands-on tasks with similar content. What is a sound next step?

**Options**

A. Conclude the learner is intentionally disrupting every lesson.
B. Compare task demands and access conditions, then plan a supportive alternative while collecting further observations.
C. Remove all materials to prevent tapping in any class.
D. Ignore the difference and apply the same consequence everywhere.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Compare task demands and access conditions, then plan a supportive alternative while collecting further observations.

**Explanation as submitted:** Different participation across task formats is evidence to examine context and function before choosing support.

##### Concept: Progress Monitoring and Adjustment

###### Question 9332

- **Question ID:** 9332
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Progress Monitoring and Adjustment

**Stem**

A learner’s goal is to begin a writing task using a visual checklist. Which evidence best monitors progress?

**Options**

A. Record whether and how often the learner starts each step with the checklist across planned opportunities.
B. Record whether the learner seemed cooperative that day.
C. Compare the learner’s handwriting with classmates’ work.
D. Ask only whether the checklist looks attractive.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Record whether and how often the learner starts each step with the checklist across planned opportunities.

**Explanation as submitted:** The measure is observable and directly connected to the task-start goal.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex B; RA 11650.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Use repeated goal-aligned evidence to decide whether instruction or support needs adjustment.

- Inclusive Education: Access, Participation, and Support — 10. Progress, transition, and learner voice

###### Question 9333

- **Question ID:** 9333
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Progress Monitoring and Adjustment

**Stem**

A learner’s weekly data show steady improvement in reading accuracy but not comprehension. What should the teacher do?

**Options**

A. Conclude that reading instruction is successful in every area.
B. Review which comprehension supports and questions are being used, then adjust instruction and monitor comprehension separately.
C. Change the accuracy goal because the learner is improving.
D. Stop collecting data until the next formal assessment.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Review which comprehension supports and questions are being used, then adjust instruction and monitor comprehension separately.

**Explanation as submitted:** Separate measures show different progress patterns; instruction should respond to the area still needing support.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

###### Question 9334

- **Question ID:** 9334
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Progress Monitoring and Adjustment

**Stem**

A single end-of-term score is available for a learner’s participation goal. What additional information would improve a decision about support?

**Options**

A. A comparison of the learner’s score with a class popularity rating.
B. A new goal chosen without reviewing the current one.
C. Repeated observations from different tasks and settings using the same participation definition.
D. A report of whether the learner appeared cheerful.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Repeated observations from different tasks and settings using the same participation definition.

**Explanation as submitted:** Repeated observations reveal change and context more reliably than one isolated score.

###### Question 9335

- **Question ID:** 9335
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Progress Monitoring and Adjustment

**Stem**

A learner uses a new self-advocacy phrase successfully with one teacher but not with other adults. What is a useful next step?

**Options**

A. Conclude the skill is mastered because it worked once.
B. Stop using the phrase so the learner becomes independent.
C. Require the learner to use it in every setting without support.
D. Practice the phrase across planned settings and check which conditions help the learner generalize it.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Practice the phrase across planned settings and check which conditions help the learner generalize it.

**Explanation as submitted:** Transfer to other settings requires practice and evidence; the team can plan gradual supported generalization.

###### Question 9336

- **Question ID:** 9336
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Progress Monitoring and Adjustment

**Stem**

A learner wants to track progress toward completing a multi-step project. Which tool best supports self-monitoring?

**Options**

A. A short learner-accessible checklist with steps the learner can mark and review with the teacher.
B. An adult-only spreadsheet that the learner cannot see.
C. A class leaderboard showing who completes work fastest.
D. A long narrative report written at the end of the term.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. A short learner-accessible checklist with steps the learner can mark and review with the teacher.

**Explanation as submitted:** An accessible checklist lets the learner participate in monitoring and planning next actions.

###### Question 9337

- **Question ID:** 9337
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Progress Monitoring and Adjustment

**Stem**

A goal is marked “good classroom behavior,” and staff disagree about whether progress occurred. What is the best improvement?

**Options**

A. Choose one staff member’s judgment as the only acceptable measure.
B. Rewrite the goal with a specific observable participation behavior and define how it will be recorded.
C. Keep the wording and collect more impressions from adults.
D. Replace the goal with a consequence for any disagreement.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Rewrite the goal with a specific observable participation behavior and define how it will be recorded.

**Explanation as submitted:** An observable definition makes evidence comparable and supports a meaningful review.

##### Concept: Transition and Community Participation

###### Question 9344

- **Question ID:** 9344
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Transition and Community Participation

**Stem**

A learner is moving to a new classroom. What should the sending and receiving teachers coordinate?

**Options**

A. Relevant learning goals, effective supports, access tools, and the learner’s transition priorities.
B. Every private detail from the learner’s full school record.
C. Only the learner’s past difficulties without successful supports.
D. A promise that the new classroom will be exactly the same.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Relevant learning goals, effective supports, access tools, and the learner’s transition priorities.

**Explanation as submitted:** A purposeful handoff carries information needed for access and learning while respecting privacy and learner priorities.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

- [Republic Act No. 11650: Inclusive Education Act](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/2/94483) — Republic of the Philippines; Supreme Court E-Library; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; Republic Act No. 11650
  - Source-level relevant sections: Sections 2–5, including inclusion policy, support/related services, general education access and individualized plans
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex B; RA 11650.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Coordinate planned transition supports toward participation and learner-selected goals.

**Study lesson reference**

- Inclusive Education: Access, Participation, and Support — 10. Progress, transition, and learner voice

Look for change over time, check whether the measure captures the intended skill, and adjust instruction when progress stalls. ###### Question 9345

- **Question ID:** 9345
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Transition and Community Participation

**Stem**

A learner says a transition goal matters because they want to join a community art club. What is the strongest planning response?

**Options**

A. Replace the goal with a standard adult-selected target.
B. Discuss the learner’s goal, identify relevant skills or supports, and coordinate a practical opportunity to practice them.
C. Assume the learner can join without exploring access needs.
D. Ask the family to handle all planning independently.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Discuss the learner’s goal, identify relevant skills or supports, and coordinate a practical opportunity to practice them.

**Explanation as submitted:** Learner-selected goals can guide coordinated practice and support community participation.

###### Question 9346

- **Question ID:** 9346
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Transition and Community Participation

**Stem**

A new support person will work with a learner during a community-based activity. What should be clarified?

**Options**

A. Only the schedule and arrival time.
B. The learner’s diagnosis without the activity expectations.
C. The learner’s communication preferences, task goals, safety procedures, and each person’s support role.
D. That the support person should make all decisions to avoid delay.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. The learner’s communication preferences, task goals, safety procedures, and each person’s support role.

**Explanation as submitted:** Clear roles and relevant access information support safety and meaningful participation.

###### Question 9347

- **Question ID:** 9347
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Positive Support and Participation / Transition and Community Participation

**Stem**

A transition meeting includes multiple adults but no opportunity for the learner to express a preference. What should the team add?

**Options**

A. A vote among the adults about what the learner will prefer.
B. A longer discussion after the learner leaves the meeting.
C. A fixed plan based only on previous year’s goals.
D. An accessible way for the learner to share goals, choices, and support needs before the plan is finalized.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. An accessible way for the learner to share goals, choices, and support needs before the plan is finalized.

**Explanation as submitted:** Learner voice is essential to individualized planning; communication should be made accessible.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

#### Topic: Universal Design and Access

##### Concept: Accessible Communication and Assistive Technology

###### Question 9296

- **Question ID:** 9296
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Universal Design and Access / Accessible Communication and Assistive Technology

**Stem**

A learner uses an augmentative communication device to answer class questions. What should the teacher plan before a group discussion?

**Options**

A. Ensure the device is available, charged, and has the vocabulary needed for the discussion.
B. Ask the learner to leave the device with an aide to avoid distraction.
C. Wait until after the discussion to ask what the learner wanted to say.
D. Use only questions that can be answered with a yes or no.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Ensure the device is available, charged, and has the vocabulary needed for the discussion.

**Explanation as submitted:** Consistent access to the learner’s communication tool supports participation and meaningful expression.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [Republic Act No. 11650: Inclusive Education Act](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/2/94483) — Republic of the Philippines; Supreme Court E-Library; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; Republic Act No. 11650
  - Source-level relevant sections: Sections 2–5, including inclusion policy, support/related services, general education access and individualized plans

###### Question 9297

- **Question ID:** 9297
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Universal Design and Access / Accessible Communication and Assistive Technology

**Stem**

A digital reading packet works with a screen reader only on some pages because headings are formatted inconsistently. What should the teacher do?

**Options**

A. Ask the learner to use a peer’s notes instead of the packet.
B. Provide a structured accessible file and check that navigation and reading order work with the learner’s tool.
C. Convert each page to an image so the layout looks identical.
D. Read only the first page aloud and omit the rest of the learning materials.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Provide a structured accessible file and check that navigation and reading order work with the learner’s tool.

**Explanation as submitted:** Accessible structure helps assistive technology convey content and navigation consistently.

###### Question 9298

- **Question ID:** 9298
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Universal Design and Access / Accessible Communication and Assistive Technology

**Stem**

A learner communicates more slowly using a speech-generating device during class discussion. Which response best supports access?

**Options**

A. Finish the learner’s message to keep the discussion moving.
B. Ask a nearby classmate to answer instead.
C. Allow processing time and plan turn-taking so the learner can complete the intended contribution.
D. Skip the learner’s turn whenever the response takes longer than peers.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Allow processing time and plan turn-taking so the learner can complete the intended contribution.

**Explanation as submitted:** The learner needs time to communicate; pacing and turn-taking can support participation without substituting another person’s answer.

###### Question 9299

- **Question ID:** 9299
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Universal Design and Access / Accessible Communication and Assistive Technology

**Stem**

A student with low vision receives a digital worksheet. Which check is most useful before the lesson?

**Options**

A. Assume magnification will work because the file is digital.
B. Print the same worksheet in the same font for everyone.
C. Replace the worksheet with an unrelated listening activity.
D. Ask the student which display features help and test the file with the student’s usual access method.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Ask the student which display features help and test the file with the student’s usual access method.

**Explanation as submitted:** Digital format alone does not guarantee access; check compatibility with the learner’s actual tools and preferences.

###### Question 9300

- **Question ID:** 9300
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Universal Design and Access / Accessible Communication and Assistive Technology

**Stem**

A communication board contains words needed for the science lesson but is stored in another room. What is the best preparation?

**Options**

A. Place the learner’s communication support where it is available during the science activity.
B. Bring it only for formal assessment so it is not overused.
C. Ask the learner to memorize the science vocabulary instead.
D. Use a classmate as the learner’s permanent interpreter.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Place the learner’s communication support where it is available during the science activity.

**Explanation as submitted:** A support cannot enable communication if it is not available at the moment the learner needs it.

###### Question 9301

- **Question ID:** 9301
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Universal Design and Access / Accessible Communication and Assistive Technology

**Stem**

A learner uses a switch to select answers. The assessment interface requires dragging items with a mouse. What should the teacher do?

**Options**

A. Score the learner on mouse speed because the interface is standardized.
B. Adapt the response interface or provide an equivalent selection method and verify it still measures the intended skill.
C. Ask another learner to operate the mouse and choose the answers.
D. Cancel the assessment without seeking an accessible response option.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Adapt the response interface or provide an equivalent selection method and verify it still measures the intended skill.

**Explanation as submitted:** An interface barrier unrelated to the target should be addressed while preserving learner control and valid evidence.

**Authoritative sources and locators**
