##### Concept: Environmental Access and Participation

###### Question 9326

- **Question ID:** 9326
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Universal Design and Access / Environmental Access and Participation

**Stem**

A learner cannot reach frequently used classroom materials from the assigned seat. What is the most appropriate environmental change?

**Options**

A. Ask classmates to retrieve every item for the learner.
B. Move the materials farther away to keep the aisle clear.
C. Place the materials within an accessible reach and check that the learner can use them independently or with planned support.
D. Replace the hands-on task with a listening activity.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Place the materials within an accessible reach and check that the learner can use them independently or with planned support.

**Explanation as submitted:** A change to layout can remove a physical barrier while keeping the learning task available.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [Republic Act No. 11650: Inclusive Education Act](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/2/94483) — Republic of the Philippines; Supreme Court E-Library; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; Republic Act No. 11650
  - Source-level relevant sections: Sections 2–5, including inclusion policy, support/related services, general education access and individualized plans
- [CAST Universal Design for Learning Guidelines, Version 3.0](https://udlguidelines.cast.org/) — CAST; PROFESSIONAL_GUIDANCE; UDL Guidelines 3.0
  - Source-level relevant sections: Version 3.0, design options for engagement, representation, action/expression, accessible materials/technology, communication and learner strategy
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex B; CAST UDL 3.0; RA 11650.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Identify and change physical, sensory, communication, and procedural barriers to participation.

**Study lesson reference**

- Inclusive Education: Access, Participation, and Support — 5. UDL, access, and action
- Material ID: let-special-needs-inclusive-education; section ID: 5-udl-access-and-action
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): CAST UDL Guidelines 3.0 organize design options around engagement, representation, and action and expression. In practice, a science explanation might be available as clear print and audio, while students can respond with a diagram, model, or spoken explanation when the learning target permits. Options should be purposeful, accessible, and equivalent in what they reveal. The goal is learner agency, not giving every learner every possible choice at once. Anticipate barriers, offer a manageable path, and invite learner feedback.

###### Question 9327

- **Question ID:** 9327
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Universal Design and Access / Environmental Access and Participation

**Stem**

A student misses directions when multiple groups speak at once but follows them in a quieter setting. What should the teacher test?

**Options**

A. Increase the volume of all group conversations.
B. Give the same directions only once from a different room.
C. Remove group participation from the student’s schedule.
D. Reduce competing sound during instructions and provide a clear visual or written cue.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Reduce competing sound during instructions and provide a clear visual or written cue.

**Explanation as submitted:** The setting difference points to a possible auditory access barrier; the teacher can modify conditions and check the outcome.

###### Question 9328

- **Question ID:** 9328
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Universal Design and Access / Environmental Access and Participation

**Stem**

A learner using a mobility device cannot enter a project group because desks block the route. What should the teacher do?

**Options**

A. Rearrange the desks to create a usable route and shared workspace for the group.
B. Ask the learner to work separately because the groups are already formed.
C. Move the learner’s materials while leaving the blocked route unchanged.
D. Ask peers to carry the learner’s device into the group.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Rearrange the desks to create a usable route and shared workspace for the group.

**Explanation as submitted:** The barrier is in the room arrangement; changing it supports shared participation without separating the learner.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

###### Question 9329

- **Question ID:** 9329
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Universal Design and Access / Environmental Access and Participation

**Stem**

A science activity uses spoken directions, but learners cannot revisit the steps during independent work. Which change improves access?

**Options**

A. Repeat all directions more quickly before distributing equipment.
B. Provide concise written or visual steps that remain available while learners work.
C. Expect learners to remember every step after one explanation.
D. Replace independent work with teacher demonstration only.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Provide concise written or visual steps that remain available while learners work.

**Explanation as submitted:** Persistent directions can support recall and independent participation without changing the science task.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [Republic Act No. 11650: Inclusive Education Act](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/2/94483) — Republic of the Philippines; Supreme Court E-Library; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; Republic Act No. 11650
  - Source-level relevant sections: Sections 2–5, including inclusion policy, support/related services, general education access and individualized plans
- [CAST Universal Design for Learning Guidelines, Version 3.0](https://udlguidelines.cast.org/) — CAST; PROFESSIONAL_GUIDANCE; UDL Guidelines 3.0
  - Source-level relevant sections: Version 3.0, design options for engagement, representation, action/expression, accessible materials/technology, communication and learner strategy
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex B; CAST UDL 3.0; RA 11650.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Identify and change physical, sensory, communication, and procedural barriers to participation.

**Study lesson reference**

- Inclusive Education: Access, Participation, and Support — 5. UDL, access, and action
- Material ID: let-special-needs-inclusive-education; section ID: 5-udl-access-and-action

Anticipate barriers, offer a manageable path, and invite learner feedback.

###### Question 9330

- **Question ID:** 9330
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Universal Design and Access / Environmental Access and Participation

**Stem**

A learner is assigned a peer helper in every task, even when the learner can complete it independently. What should the teacher reconsider?

**Options**

A. Whether the helper should make more decisions for the learner.
B. Rotate to a different peer helper while keeping support assigned throughout the day.
C. Whether the support is necessary for the specific task and whether it limits independence or peer reciprocity.
D. Whether all learners should receive a permanent helper.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Whether the support is necessary for the specific task and whether it limits independence or peer reciprocity.

**Explanation as submitted:** Support should remove barriers and preserve agency; an unnecessary helper can create dependence or social imbalance.

###### Question 9331

- **Question ID:** 9331
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Universal Design and Access / Environmental Access and Participation

**Stem**

A learner participates in group discussion when given a preview of key vocabulary but not when new terms are introduced without context. Which plan is strongest?

**Options**

A. Give the learner a different discussion topic each time.
B. Remove new vocabulary from the lesson entirely.
C. Wait until the learner asks for every unfamiliar word.
D. Preview relevant vocabulary with accessible examples and check whether the learner can contribute to the same discussion goal.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Preview relevant vocabulary with accessible examples and check whether the learner can contribute to the same discussion goal.

**Explanation as submitted:** A planned vocabulary preview can reduce a barrier while retaining the shared discussion and its content.

##### Concept: Flexible Engagement, Representation, and Expression

###### Question 9290

- **Question ID:** 9290
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Universal Design and Access / Flexible Engagement, Representation, and Expression

**Stem**

A lesson objective asks learners to explain how a plant grows, but the only resource is dense printed text. Which design choice best anticipates learner variability?

**Options**

A. Keep the printed text unchanged and provide help only after a learner fails.
B. Replace the plant-growth objective with a coloring task for the entire class.
C. Pair clear text with a labeled diagram or oral explanation and allow learners to show understanding in an aligned format.
D. Let learners choose any activity, even when it does not address the objective.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Pair clear text with a labeled diagram or oral explanation and allow learners to show understanding in an aligned format.

**Explanation as submitted:** UDL anticipates access barriers through purposeful options while keeping the shared learning goal visible.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

###### Question 9291

- **Question ID:** 9291
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Universal Design and Access / Flexible Engagement, Representation, and Expression

**Stem**

A history assessment requires an oral presentation, though the stated outcome is to compare two sources. Which revision is strongest?

**Options**

A. Keep public speaking as an unstated requirement because it is easy to observe.
B. Remove the comparison requirement for learners who prefer another format.
C. Replace the source task with a participation score.
D. Allow a written comparison, recorded explanation, or structured visual response if each shows the same source-comparison reasoning.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Allow a written comparison, recorded explanation, or structured visual response if each shows the same source-comparison reasoning.

**Explanation as submitted:** Multiple expression options can preserve the comparison competency when speaking is not itself the target.

###### Question 9292

- **Question ID:** 9292
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Universal Design and Access / Flexible Engagement, Representation, and Expression

**Stem**

A teacher gives learners a choice between two equivalent texts and a teacher-read version, then asks all to identify the same main idea. What makes this a sound UDL decision?

**Options**

A. The options vary access to information while preserving a common learning intention.
B. The learners have different objectives because the materials are not identical.
C. The teacher is avoiding assessment because choice was offered.
D. The formats should be removed so everyone sees the same page.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. The options vary access to information while preserving a common learning intention.

**Explanation as submitted:** UDL can vary representation without changing what the learner is expected to understand.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9293

- **Question ID:** 9293
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Universal Design and Access / Flexible Engagement, Representation, and Expression

**Stem**

During a long worksheet, several learners stop responding, but they engage during short problem demonstrations. What is a useful design response?

**Options**

A. Increase the number of worksheet questions to build endurance.
B. Break the task into clear chunks with brief checks and a meaningful choice of practice item.
C. Assume the learners do not value mathematics and reduce the objective.
D. Make the demonstration optional and keep the worksheet unchanged.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Break the task into clear chunks with brief checks and a meaningful choice of practice item.

**Explanation as submitted:** Chunking and checks address task design and support strategy while retaining the math goal.

###### Question 9294

- **Question ID:** 9294
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Universal Design and Access / Flexible Engagement, Representation, and Expression

**Stem**

A teacher uses captions, diagrams, and hands-on models for a concept but does not explain how they connect. What should be improved?

**Options**

A. Remove all but one representation to avoid learner choice.
B. Add more media without discussing relationships among them.
C. Explicitly link the representations so learners can see the same idea across each form.
D. Assess only who can name the media used in class.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Explicitly link the representations so learners can see the same idea across each form.

**Explanation as submitted:** Multiple representations are most useful when learners can connect them to the underlying concept.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

###### Question 9295

- **Question ID:** 9295
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Special Needs Education / Universal Design and Access / Flexible Engagement, Representation, and Expression

**Stem**

A learner says a digital response form is difficult to navigate and asks to use a familiar communication tool. What is the strongest next step?

**Options**

A. Require the digital form because all learners received the same link.
B. Assume the learner’s preferred tool is unsuitable without checking the objective.
C. Remove the learner from the assessment until the system is changed.
D. Check whether the familiar tool can provide valid evidence of the objective and arrange access through the learner’s plan.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Check whether the familiar tool can provide valid evidence of the objective and arrange access through the learner’s plan.

**Explanation as submitted:** Learner input is evidence about access; evaluate the tool against the learning target and planned supports.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66

  - Source-level relevant sections: Sections 2–5, including inclusion policy, support/related services, general education access and individualized plans
- [CAST Universal Design for Learning Guidelines, Version 3.0](https://udlguidelines.cast.org/) — CAST; PROFESSIONAL_GUIDANCE; UDL Guidelines 3.0
  - Source-level relevant sections: Version 3.0, design options for engagement, representation, action/expression, accessible materials/technology, communication and learner strategy
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex B; CAST UDL 3.0; RA 11650.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Anticipate barriers and provide meaningful ways to engage, access information, and express learning.

**Study lesson reference**

- Inclusive Education: Access, Participation, and Support — 5. UDL, access, and action
- Material ID: let-special-needs-inclusive-education; section ID: 5-udl-access-and-action
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): CAST UDL Guidelines 3.0 organize design options around engagement, representation, and action and expression. In practice, a science explanation might be available as clear print and audio, while students can respond with a diagram, model, or spoken explanation when the learning target permits. Options should be purposeful, accessible, and equivalent in what they reveal. The goal is learner agency, not giving every learner every possible choice at once. Anticipate barriers, offer a manageable path, and invite learner feedback.

### Subject: Technical-Vocational Teacher Education

#### Topic: Competency Outcomes and Technical Instruction

##### Concept: Demonstration and Guided Practice

###### Question 9354

- **Question ID:** 9354
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Competency Outcomes and Technical Instruction / Demonstration and Guided Practice

**Stem**

Learners will use a machine control for the first time. What is the best first teaching sequence?

**Options**

A. Give learners the controls and ask them to discover the sequence unsupervised.
B. Describe the machine history before showing how it operates.
C. Explain the task and hazards, model the control sequence, then supervise learner practice.
D. Ask one learner to perform while the rest only take notes.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Explain the task and hazards, model the control sequence, then supervise learner practice.

**Explanation as submitted:** A safety-aware demonstration makes the process visible before each learner practices under supervision.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [2025 TVET Glossary](https://www.tesda.gov.ph/Uploads/File/Glossary/2025%20TVET%20Glossary.pdf) — Technical Education and Skills Development Authority, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; 2025 TVET Glossary
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 25–27, 37–40, 89, 103; competency standards, CBC, CBLM, CBT, assessment evidence and moderation
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex A; TESDA Glossary pp. 25–27.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Model technical process and safety, then scaffold learner practice and transfer.

**Study lesson reference**

- Competency-Based Technical and Vocational Teaching — 6. Demonstration, practice, and transfer
- Material ID: let-tvted-competency-based-teaching; section ID: 6-demonstration-practice-and-transfer
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Technical learning requires deliberate, repeated practice that bridges a model and authentic performance. Break a complex procedure into teachable steps without hiding how the steps work together. Model the task, let learners rehearse under support, then vary materials or conditions so they can transfer the skill. Ask learners to explain decisions and perform critical checks. Keep workspaces and learner groups safe, and ensure each trainee gets direct practice rather than only watching a partner.

###### Question 9355

- **Question ID:** 9355
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Competency Outcomes and Technical Instruction / Demonstration and Guided Practice

**Stem**

During a cutting demonstration, learners can see the tool but not the teacher’s measurement marks. What should the trainer change?

**Options**

A. Repeat the explanation at the same angle and begin assessment.
B. Ask learners to memorize the measurement without seeing it.
C. Skip measurement practice because it slows the demonstration.
D. Reposition the work and demonstrate the measurement checkpoint from a visible angle before practice.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Reposition the work and demonstrate the measurement checkpoint from a visible angle before practice.

**Explanation as submitted:** Learners need to observe the performance feature that the task requires.

###### Question 9356

- **Question ID:** 9356
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Competency Outcomes and Technical Instruction / Demonstration and Guided Practice

**Stem**

A learner can follow a procedure with prompts but misses a check when completing it alone. What is a useful next practice step?

**Options**

A. Practice the check as an explicit step, then gradually reduce prompts while observing performance.
B. Remove the check from the task so the learner can finish independently.
C. Give the learner the answer before each attempt.
D. Assess only the parts completed with teacher assistance.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Practice the check as an explicit step, then gradually reduce prompts while observing performance.

**Explanation as submitted:** Focused rehearsal and fading support help the learner transfer the procedure to independent performance.

###### Question 9357

- **Question ID:** 9357
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Competency Outcomes and Technical Instruction / Demonstration and Guided Practice

**Stem**

A demonstration is long and includes several tool changes. How can the trainer help learners notice the critical sequence?

**Options**

A. Perform it once at full speed without pauses.
B. Break the demonstration at meaningful checkpoints and ask learners to predict the next safe step.
C. Provide a written list after the practical session.
D. Allow learners to imitate only the final product.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Break the demonstration at meaningful checkpoints and ask learners to predict the next safe step.

**Explanation as submitted:** Checkpoints help learners follow procedure and reasoning while remaining connected to the whole task.

###### Question 9358

- **Question ID:** 9358
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Competency Outcomes and Technical Instruction / Demonstration and Guided Practice

**Stem**

One trainee completes a practice task while others wait for equipment. Which training design improves practice opportunity?

**Options**

A. Keep one demonstration performer and increase waiting time.
B. Have peers record the performer without rotating roles.
C. Create supervised stations or task roles so each learner gets direct practice and feedback.
D. Use a written quiz as a substitute for every practical attempt.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Create supervised stations or task roles so each learner gets direct practice and feedback.

**Explanation as submitted:** Each trainee needs an opportunity to perform the target skill, not only observe it.

###### Question 9359

- **Question ID:** 9359
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Competency Outcomes and Technical Instruction / Demonstration and Guided Practice

**Stem**

A learner performs well with a familiar material but struggles when the material size changes within the allowed work range. What should the trainer do?

**Options**

A. Keep all future practice on the familiar material.
B. Change the standard so the familiar material is the only acceptable input.
C. Provide the correct adjustment before each attempt and score the result as independent.
D. Use graduated practice with varied allowed materials and ask the learner to explain adjustments to the process.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Use graduated practice with varied allowed materials and ask the learner to explain adjustments to the process.

**Explanation as submitted:** Variation within the work range supports transfer and reveals whether the learner can adapt rather than merely repeat.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

##### Concept: Training Materials and Checklists

###### Question 9372

- **Question ID:** 9372
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Competency Outcomes and Technical Instruction / Training Materials and Checklists

**Stem**

Learners must prepare for a supervised equipment task. Which material helps them confirm readiness before practice?

**Options**

A. A concise information guide with a self-check linked to the task requirements.
B. A promotional brochure about the training center.
C. A long glossary unrelated to the equipment procedure.
D. A performance certificate issued before learners practice.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. A concise information guide with a self-check linked to the task requirements.

**Explanation as submitted:** A self-check helps learners review readiness, while later direct practice provides performance evidence.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [2025 TVET Glossary](https://www.tesda.gov.ph/Uploads/File/Glossary/2025%20TVET%20Glossary.pdf) — Technical Education and Skills Development Authority, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; 2025 TVET Glossary
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 25–27, 37–40, 89, 103; competency standards, CBC, CBLM, CBT, assessment evidence and moderation
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** TESDA Glossary pp. 26–27; PRC Annex A.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Sequence learning guides, task sheets, self-checks, and performance checklists around outcomes.

**Study lesson reference**

- Competency-Based Technical and Vocational Teaching — 8. Learning materials and training environment
- Material ID: let-tvted-competency-based-teaching; section ID: 8-learning-materials-and-training-environment
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): TESDA’s glossary describes competency-based learning materials that can include a learner guide, competency list, learning outcomes, information sheets, self-checks, task or operation sheets, job sheets, and performance checklists. Choose only the materials that support the target outcome and sequence them so learners can prepare, practice, check understanding, and demonstrate performance. Maintain equipment and work areas according to applicable procedures; replace a damaged tool rather than asking learners to work around a hazard.

A. A motivational quotation at the top of the sheet.
B. The tools, materials, work conditions, and safety controls needed to complete the task.
C. A list of unrelated units in the qualification.
D. A trainer-only scoring note learners cannot access.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. The tools, materials, work conditions, and safety controls needed to complete the task.

**Explanation as submitted:** Conditions and resources are part of clear task preparation and safe practice.

###### Question 9374

- **Question ID:** 9374
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Competency Outcomes and Technical Instruction / Training Materials and Checklists

**Stem**

A learner finishes a module self-check with all correct responses. What is the next step when the outcome requires practical performance?

**Options**

A. Record competence based on the self-check alone.
B. Skip practice because the learner answered questions correctly.
C. Provide the planned performance task and assess the learner’s actual work against criteria.
D. Ask another trainee to demonstrate and transfer the result.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Provide the planned performance task and assess the learner’s actual work against criteria.

**Explanation as submitted:** A self-check supports learning but cannot replace direct evidence when performance is the target.

###### Question 9375

- **Question ID:** 9375
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Competency Outcomes and Technical Instruction / Training Materials and Checklists

**Stem**

A job sheet contains every step but no quality checkpoints. What improvement will help learners monitor performance?

**Options**

A. Remove the steps so learners must guess the process.
B. Add a list of every possible tool without relating it to the task.
C. Provide the answer only after the task is submitted.
D. Add checkpoints that correspond to important criteria and show when to inspect the work.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Add checkpoints that correspond to important criteria and show when to inspect the work.

**Explanation as submitted:** Checkpoints link the procedure to its quality requirements while work is underway. Learners can inspect at meaningful stages and correct a problem before it affects later steps.

###### Question 9376

- **Question ID:** 9376
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Competency Outcomes and Technical Instruction / Training Materials and Checklists

**Stem**

A paper checklist is difficult to use in a noisy workshop and is often misplaced. What should the trainer consider?

**Options**

A. Use a durable, accessible format that remains available at the work station and preserves the same criteria.
B. Remove the checklist so no learner relies on it.
C. Replace it with an informal verbal reminder that varies by trainer.
D. Ask trainees to reconstruct the criteria from memory.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Use a durable, accessible format that remains available at the work station and preserves the same criteria.

**Explanation as submitted:** The checklist should be usable in the actual environment and consistently represent the criteria.

###### Question 9377

- **Question ID:** 9377
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Competency Outcomes and Technical Instruction / Training Materials and Checklists

**Stem**

A training packet has a learner guide, task sheet, self-check, and performance checklist, but the sections use different names for the same outcome. What should the teacher do?

**Options**

A. Keep the differences because learners should infer the connection.
B. Standardize the outcome language and map each section to the same competency and assessment criteria.
C. Remove the performance checklist to reduce repetition.
D. Add a second unrelated outcome to each section.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Standardize the outcome language and map each section to the same competency and assessment criteria.

**Explanation as submitted:** Consistent alignment helps learners see how study, practice, self-check, and assessment relate.

##### Concept: Workplace Standard to Learning Outcome

###### Question 9348

- **Question ID:** 9348
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Competency Outcomes and Technical Instruction / Workplace Standard to Learning Outcome

**Stem**

A workplace standard states that a trainee must measure stock, cut it to specification, and check the finished part. Which learning outcome is strongest?

**Options**

A. With specified tools, measure and cut stock to dimensions, then verify the finished part.
B. Describe why accurate measurement matters without performing the task or checking a part.
C. Observe a demonstration, then explain the cutting sequence without producing a measured part.
D. Measure and cut stock, then accept the result without checking dimensions against criteria.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. With specified tools, measure and cut stock to dimensions, then verify the finished part.

**Explanation as submitted:** The outcome states observable performance, conditions, and a verification step aligned to the standard. It asks the trainee to demonstrate the complete practical task rather than only describe or observe it.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [2025 TVET Glossary](https://www.tesda.gov.ph/Uploads/File/Glossary/2025%20TVET%20Glossary.pdf) — Technical Education and Skills Development Authority, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; 2025 TVET Glossary
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 25–27, 37–40, 89, 103; competency standards, CBC, CBLM, CBT, assessment evidence and moderation
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex A pp. 65–66; TESDA Glossary pp. 25–27, 40.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Translate a workplace competency standard into observable instructional outcomes and conditions.

**Study lesson reference**

- Competency-Based Technical and Vocational Teaching — 5. From competency standard to learning outcome
- Material ID: let-tvted-competency-based-teaching; section ID: 5-from-competency-standard-to-learning-outcome
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): TESDA describes competency-based curricula as specifying outcomes, content, learning conditions, methods, and assessment aligned with workplace requirements. Read the performance criteria and evidence guide before designing a lesson. Turn a criterion into a learner-facing outcome that states an observable action and condition. Then sequence the prerequisite knowledge and practice learners need. If a regulation or industry requirement changes, verify the current controlling text before teaching it as mandatory.

###### Question 9349

- **Question ID:** 9349
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Competency Outcomes and Technical Instruction / Workplace Standard to Learning Outcome

**Stem**

A trainer converts a standard requiring tool selection and safe operation into a lesson on tool names only. What is missing?

**Options**

A. A longer glossary of tool names.
B. Practice selecting and using the appropriate tool under the performance conditions, including safety criteria.
C. A score for attendance during the naming lesson.
D. A discussion of why technical work is valuable.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Practice selecting and using the appropriate tool under the performance conditions, including safety criteria.

**Explanation as submitted:** Knowing names alone does not demonstrate selection and operation competency.

###### Question 9350

- **Question ID:** 9350
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Competency Outcomes and Technical Instruction / Workplace Standard to Learning Outcome

**Stem**

An industry partner reports a new process, but the training regulation lists the older method. What should the teacher do before changing required assessment?

**Options**

A. Replace the standard immediately based on an informal conversation.
B. Continue using the older method without checking whether the current standard allows an equivalent method.
C. Verify the current official standard and document industry evidence before revising the outcome or assessment.
D. Ask learners to decide which method will be assessed.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Verify the current official standard and document industry evidence before revising the outcome or assessment.

**Explanation as submitted:** Industry evidence matters, but current requirements should be checked before treating a change as mandatory.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9351

- **Question ID:** 9351
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Competency Outcomes and Technical Instruction / Workplace Standard to Learning Outcome

**Stem**

A performance criterion says a learner will identify faults in a wiring diagram. Which assessment condition is needed for a fair test?

**Options**

A. Provide only a lecture summary and score note-taking.
B. Use an unrelated diagram with hidden faults not in the standard.
C. Allow one learner to complete the task while others observe.
D. Provide a representative diagram and the tools or references permitted by the competency conditions.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Provide a representative diagram and the tools or references permitted by the competency conditions.

**Explanation as submitted:** Assessment should match the standard’s task conditions and let each learner demonstrate the criterion.

###### Question 9352

- **Question ID:** 9352
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Competency Outcomes and Technical Instruction / Workplace Standard to Learning Outcome

**Stem**

A lesson plan lists many topics but does not state what learners will be able to do. How should it be revised?

**Options**

A. Identify the competency outcome first, then select content and practice that prepare learners to demonstrate it.
B. Add more topic headings so the plan looks complete.
C. Begin with the textbook sequence and omit performance checks.
D. Replace the plan with a list of required training hours.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Identify the competency outcome first, then select content and practice that prepare learners to demonstrate it.

**Explanation as submitted:** Competency-based curriculum starts from outcomes and aligns content, methods, conditions, and assessment.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9353

- **Question ID:** 9353
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Competency Outcomes and Technical Instruction / Workplace Standard to Learning Outcome

**Stem**

Two valid methods produce the required work result, but the trainer prefers one method. What is the defensible assessment approach?

**Options**

A. Require only the trainer’s preferred method even if the standard permits alternatives.
B. Assess both methods against the standard’s output, process, and safety criteria under the stated conditions.
C. Accept any method without checking the result or safety.
D. Score the learner’s confidence rather than performance evidence.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Assess both methods against the standard’s output, process, and safety criteria under the stated conditions.

**Explanation as submitted:** The standard governs; alternative methods can be valid if they meet required outcomes and conditions.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Translate a workplace competency standard into observable instructional outcomes and conditions.

**Study lesson reference**

- Competency-Based Technical and Vocational Teaching — 5. From competency standard to learning outcome
- Material ID: let-tvted-competency-based-teaching; section ID: 5-from-competency-standard-to-learning-outcome
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): TESDA describes competency-based curricula as specifying outcomes, content, learning conditions, methods, and assessment aligned with workplace requirements. Read the performance criteria and evidence guide before designing a lesson. Turn a criterion into a learner-facing outcome that states an observable action and condition. Then sequence the prerequisite knowledge and practice learners need. If a regulation or industry requirement changes, verify the current controlling text before teaching it as mandatory.

#### Topic: Feedback, Employability, and Enterprise

##### Concept: Employability and Enterprise Application

###### Question 9396

- **Question ID:** 9396
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Feedback, Employability, and Enterprise / Employability and Enterprise Application

**Stem**

A learner creates a small repair service as an entrepreneurship exercise. Which question best connects technical quality with customer value?

**Options**

A. What problem does the service solve, and what evidence will show that the repair is safe and reliable?
B. How can the learner advertise before checking whether the repair works?
C. Which logo will attract the most attention regardless of service quality?
D. How can the learner reduce the time spent explaining the service to customers?

**Proposed correct answer:** A. What problem does the service solve, and what evidence will show that the repair is safe and reliable?

**Explanation as submitted:** Enterprise practice should connect customer need with competent, trustworthy work.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [2025 TVET Glossary](https://www.tesda.gov.ph/Uploads/File/Glossary/2025%20TVET%20Glossary.pdf) — Technical Education and Skills Development Authority, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; 2025 TVET Glossary
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 25–27, 37–40, 89, 103; competency standards, CBC, CBLM, CBT, assessment evidence and moderation
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex A; TESDA Glossary.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Apply technical competence to customer needs, work quality, employability, and responsible enterprise.

**Study lesson reference**

- Competency-Based Technical and Vocational Teaching — 10. Industry links, feedback, and employability
- Material ID: let-tvted-competency-based-teaching; section ID: 10-industry-links-feedback-and-employability
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Industry input helps keep learning outcomes connected to work, but the curriculum must still remain coherent and teachable. Ask employers or practitioners which performance standards and changing practices matter, then verify them against the current training regulation or documented industry evidence. Feedback should identify a criterion, observed performance, and a useful next step. Enterprise activities can connect technical quality to customer need, cost, reliability, and ethical practice; do not reduce entrepreneurship to selling without understanding the service or product.

###### Question 9397

- **Question ID:** 9397
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Feedback, Employability, and Enterprise / Employability and Enterprise Application

**Stem**

A trainee proposes selling a product made from materials that may be unsafe for its intended use. What is the appropriate teaching response?

**Options**

A. Encourage sales first and review the materials after customer feedback.
B. Check the material’s suitability and current safety requirements before evaluating market potential.
C. Assume a popular product is suitable for any use.
D. Use lower-quality materials to reduce costs without informing buyers.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Check the material’s suitability and current safety requirements before evaluating market potential.

**Explanation as submitted:** Enterprise decisions remain bounded by safe and fit-for-purpose technical practice.

###### Question 9398

- **Question ID:** 9398
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Feedback, Employability, and Enterprise / Employability and Enterprise Application

**Stem**

An employer requests workers who can explain a completed job to customers. Which learning activity best supports this employability need?

**Options**

A. Replace practical work with a customer-service vocabulary list.
B. Ask learners to memorize a sales script unrelated to the task.
C. Have learners complete a technical task and practice a concise explanation of the work, result, and relevant care information.
D. Assess only whether learners speak confidently.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Have learners complete a technical task and practice a concise explanation of the work, result, and relevant care information.

**Explanation as submitted:** Communication should be practiced in connection with authentic technical work and accurate evidence.

###### Question 9399

- **Question ID:** 9399
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Feedback, Employability, and Enterprise / Employability and Enterprise Application

**Stem**

A learner’s enterprise plan appears profitable but depends on skipping a required quality check. What should the instructor emphasize?

**Options**

A. Accept the plan because profit is the central enterprise outcome.
B. Remove the quality check from the training standard.
C. Ask customers to accept the risk in exchange for a lower price.
D. Revise the plan so cost and time decisions preserve the required quality and safety standard.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Revise the plan so cost and time decisions preserve the required quality and safety standard.

**Explanation as submitted:** Responsible enterprise depends on competent work; market considerations do not override required criteria.

##### Concept: Feedback, Remediation, and Reassessment

###### Question 9390

- **Question ID:** 9390
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Feedback, Employability, and Enterprise / Feedback, Remediation, and Reassessment

**Stem**

A trainee misses one measurement criterion. Which feedback is most useful?

**Options**

A. Say only “do better next time.”
B. Compare the trainee with the fastest person in class.
C. Name the measurement criterion, describe the observed difference, and identify a practice step.
D. Mark the task unsuccessful without explaining the evidence.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Name the measurement criterion, describe the observed difference, and identify a practice step.

**Explanation as submitted:** Specific criterion-linked feedback gives a learner a clear next action.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 25–27, 37–40, 89, 103; competency standards, CBC, CBLM, CBT, assessment evidence and moderation
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** TESDA Glossary pp. 25–27, 89; PRC Annex A.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Give criterion-linked feedback and plan further practice and a fair new performance opportunity.

**Study lesson reference**

###### Question 9391

- **Question ID:** 9391
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Feedback, Employability, and Enterprise / Feedback, Remediation, and Reassessment

**Stem**

A trainee does not meet the standard after an assessment. What should the trainer do before reassessment?

**Options**

A. Repeat the same assessment immediately without support.
B. Record attendance at extra training as proof of competence.
C. Change the criterion so the earlier performance passes.
D. Explain the evidence gap, provide targeted practice, and arrange a new opportunity under fair conditions.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Explain the evidence gap, provide targeted practice, and arrange a new opportunity under fair conditions.

**Explanation as submitted:** Remediation should target the observed gap, and reassessment should gather new performance evidence.

###### Question 9392

- **Question ID:** 9392
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Feedback, Employability, and Enterprise / Feedback, Remediation, and Reassessment

**Stem**

A learner improved after feedback but still misses a process check. What should the trainer focus on in the next practice?

**Options**

A. Rehearse the missed check in context and observe whether it becomes consistent in the full task.
B. Repeat feedback about every part of the task at once.
C. Stop practice because improvement already occurred.
D. Assess a different competency instead of the missing check.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Rehearse the missed check in context and observe whether it becomes consistent in the full task.

**Explanation as submitted:** Focused practice targets the remaining criterion while later observation checks integration.

###### Question 9393

- **Question ID:** 9393
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Feedback, Employability, and Enterprise / Feedback, Remediation, and Reassessment

**Stem**

A trainee can explain a correction but has not performed the task again. What evidence is needed for a performance unit?

**Options**

A. A written statement that the trainee understands the correction.
B. A new observation of the trainee completing the required task under the stated conditions.
C. A note that the trainee attended the feedback meeting.
D. A peer’s demonstration of the corrected task.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. A new observation of the trainee completing the required task under the stated conditions.

**Explanation as submitted:** Understanding feedback is useful but direct performance evidence is needed when the unit requires doing.

###### Question 9394

- **Question ID:** 9394
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Feedback, Employability, and Enterprise / Feedback, Remediation, and Reassessment

**Stem**

Several trainees struggle with the same quality criterion despite completing the same lesson. What should the instructor review?

**Options**

A. Assume all trainees lack motivation.
B. Remove the criterion because many learners missed it.
C. The demonstration, practice conditions, materials, and clarity of the criterion before planning additional support.
D. Retest immediately without checking instruction.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. The demonstration, practice conditions, materials, and clarity of the criterion before planning additional support.

**Explanation as submitted:** A common gap may signal an instructional or material issue as well as a learner need.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

**Study lesson reference**

- Competency-Based Technical and Vocational Teaching — 10. Industry links, feedback, and employability
- Material ID: let-tvted-competency-based-teaching; section ID: 10-industry-links-feedback-and-employability
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Industry input helps keep learning outcomes connected to work, but the curriculum must still remain coherent and teachable. Ask employers or practitioners which performance standards and changing practices matter, then verify them against the current training regulation or documented industry evidence. Feedback should identify a criterion, observed performance, and a useful next step. Enterprise activities can connect technical quality to customer need, cost, reliability, and ethical practice; do not reduce entrepreneurship to selling without understanding the service or product.

###### Question 9395

- **Question ID:** 9395
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Feedback, Employability, and Enterprise / Feedback, Remediation, and Reassessment

**Stem**

A trainee passes after multiple practice attempts. What record best supports a defensible decision?

**Options**

A. The number of practice hours alone.
B. The trainer’s general impression of improvement.
C. The trainee’s promise to perform correctly at work.
D. The final performance evidence, the applicable criteria and conditions, and any required assessment record.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. The final performance evidence, the applicable criteria and conditions, and any required assessment record.

**Explanation as submitted:** The decision rests on evidence of the standard under required conditions, not effort or prediction.

#### Topic: Safety and Workplace Learning

##### Concept: Hazard Control and Safe Practice

###### Question 9378

- **Question ID:** 9378
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Safety and Workplace Learning / Hazard Control and Safe Practice

**Stem**

A trainee notices that a machine guard is loose before practice begins. What should happen?

**Options**

A. Begin at a slower speed and ask the trainer to watch.
B. Ask another trainee to hold the guard in place.
C. Do not start the task; report the condition and follow the training site’s procedure to make the equipment safe.
D. Skip the safety check and complete only the easy part.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Do not start the task; report the condition and follow the training site’s procedure to make the equipment safe.

**Explanation as submitted:** A known equipment hazard must be addressed before work proceeds under the applicable safety procedure.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [2025 TVET Glossary](https://www.tesda.gov.ph/Uploads/File/Glossary/2025%20TVET%20Glossary.pdf) — Technical Education and Skills Development Authority, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; 2025 TVET Glossary
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 25–27, 37–40, 89, 103; competency standards, CBC, CBLM, CBT, assessment evidence and moderation
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex A pp. 65–66; TESDA Glossary's OSH terms.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Recognize hazards, pause unsafe activity, apply controls, and resume only in safe conditions.

**Study lesson reference**

- Competency-Based Technical and Vocational Teaching — 9. Safety and workplace learning
- Material ID: let-tvted-competency-based-teaching; section ID: 9-safety-and-workplace-learning
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): Workplace learning connects training with authentic practice, but authenticity never excuses uncontrolled hazards or unclear responsibilities. Coordinate the assigned task, supervision, available equipment, safety orientation, and evidence to be collected. The PRC BTVTEd common competencies include occupational health, safety, and ethical values. TESDA circulars and trade standards may change, so verify current requirements for a specific qualification instead of using an old remembered rule. An unsafe task should be stopped and referred through the training site’s procedures.

###### Question 9379

- **Question ID:** 9379
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Safety and Workplace Learning / Hazard Control and Safe Practice

**Stem**

A spill appears near a workbench during a practical activity. What is the trainer’s immediate instructional response?

**Options**

A. Tell trainees to step around the spill and finish quickly.
B. Ignore it until the practical assessment ends.
C. Ask a trainee to clean it without checking the material or procedure.
D. Pause work in the affected area, secure it according to site procedure, and resume only when it is safe.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Pause work in the affected area, secure it according to site procedure, and resume only when it is safe.

**Explanation as submitted:** The area should be controlled and handled under site procedure before work continues.

###### Question 9380

- **Question ID:** 9380
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Safety and Workplace Learning / Hazard Control and Safe Practice

**Stem**

Learners arrive without the protective equipment required by their current task procedure. What is the right response?

**Options**

A. Hold the affected task until required equipment and safe conditions are available.
B. Allow a shorter task because exposure will be brief.
C. Ask learners to share equipment without checking fit or suitability.
D. Remove the safety criterion from the assessment.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Hold the affected task until required equipment and safe conditions are available.

**Explanation as submitted:** Training should not proceed without the required controls for the assigned task.

###### Question 9381

- **Question ID:** 9381
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Safety and Workplace Learning / Hazard Control and Safe Practice

**Stem**

A trainer sees a trainee reach across a moving work area to retrieve a dropped item. What should the trainer do?

**Options**

A. Wait until the task is complete to discuss the action.
B. Stop the action and reinforce the safe procedure for retrieving items from that work area.
C. Reward the trainee for finishing without delay.
D. Ask another trainee to retrieve items while the equipment continues running.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Stop the action and reinforce the safe procedure for retrieving items from that work area.

**Explanation as submitted:** Immediate correction protects learners and reinforces safety as part of performance.

###### Question 9382

- **Question ID:** 9382
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Safety and Workplace Learning / Hazard Control and Safe Practice

**Stem**

A task’s operating instructions changed after the training materials were printed. What should the teacher verify?

**Options**

A. Continue using the old guide because it is already issued.
B. Ask learners to compare the old and new instructions without supervision.
C. The current manufacturer or official task instructions and applicable workplace requirements before training resumes.
D. Use a remembered procedure from a different machine.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. The current manufacturer or official task instructions and applicable workplace requirements before training resumes.

**Explanation as submitted:** Time-sensitive operating and safety details require current authoritative verification.

###### Question 9383

- **Question ID:** 9383
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Safety and Workplace Learning / Hazard Control and Safe Practice

**Stem**

A trainee rushes through an otherwise correct task and skips a required safety check. How should the assessment be judged?

**Options**

A. Accept the result because the product meets dimensions.
B. Ignore the omitted check if no injury occurred.
C. Reward speed as evidence of higher competence.
D. Apply the stated safety criterion; provide further safe practice if it was missed.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Apply the stated safety criterion; provide further safe practice if it was missed.

**Explanation as submitted:** Safety criteria are part of the demonstrated competency when specified; outcome quality does not erase an unsafe process. The teacher should assess the stated evidence and avoid asking the trainee to repeat the unsafe shortcut.

##### Concept: Workplace Learning Coordination

###### Question 9384

- **Question ID:** 9384
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Safety and Workplace Learning / Workplace Learning Coordination

**Stem**

A learner is assigned workplace practice that matches a unit outcome. What should the school and workplace agree before the task begins?

**Options**

A. The task, supervision, safe conditions, learning purpose, and evidence to be observed.
B. Only the trainee’s arrival time.
C. That the workplace may assign any task regardless of training.
D. That the trainee will work independently without a named supervisor.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. The task, supervision, safe conditions, learning purpose, and evidence to be observed.

**Explanation as submitted:** Clear roles and conditions make workplace learning safe and connected to the competency.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Align supervised workplace experience, roles, and evidence with expected competencies.

###### Question 9385

- **Question ID:** 9385
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Safety and Workplace Learning / Workplace Learning Coordination

**Stem**

A workplace supervisor gives a learner a task unrelated to the unit being assessed. What should the trainer do?

**Options**

A. Count any workplace task as relevant evidence.
B. Discuss an alternative task that matches the learner’s training outcomes and supervision arrangements.
C. Ask the learner to complete the task alone to gain experience.
D. Remove the unit outcome from the training plan.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Discuss an alternative task that matches the learner’s training outcomes and supervision arrangements.

**Explanation as submitted:** Authentic experience should still align with the learning outcome and safe agreed placement conditions.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9386

- **Question ID:** 9386
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Safety and Workplace Learning / Workplace Learning Coordination

**Stem**

An employer says trainees need better troubleshooting skills but provides no examples. How can the program use this feedback?

**Options**

A. Add a troubleshooting exam immediately without reviewing tasks.
B. Dismiss the feedback because it is not a formal curriculum document.
C. Ask for representative task evidence, compare it with the current standards, and identify a teachable gap.
D. Replace the full curriculum with the employer’s preferred topic.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Ask for representative task evidence, compare it with the current standards, and identify a teachable gap.

**Explanation as submitted:** Feedback can signal a need for review, but the program should clarify the evidence and compare it with current requirements.

###### Question 9387

- **Question ID:** 9387
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Safety and Workplace Learning / Workplace Learning Coordination

**Stem**

A workplace supervisor and trainer use different assessment terms for the same performance. What is a useful coordination step?

**Options**

A. Allow each assessor to use a private definition.
B. Choose whichever term gives the higher result.
C. Stop collecting evidence at the workplace.
D. Review the common criteria together and agree how each will record observable evidence.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Review the common criteria together and agree how each will record observable evidence.

**Explanation as submitted:** A shared understanding of standards supports consistent observation and assessment.

###### Question 9388

- **Question ID:** 9388
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Safety and Workplace Learning / Workplace Learning Coordination

**Stem**

A workplace has a high-demand period that may limit trainee supervision. What should the coordinator consider?

**Options**

A. Whether the task and staffing still allow safe practice and meaningful observation, then adjust timing or assignment if needed.
B. Place the trainee in the busiest area because it is authentic.
C. Continue because the placement schedule is already published.
D. Ask the trainee to supervise themselves.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Whether the task and staffing still allow safe practice and meaningful observation, then adjust timing or assignment if needed.

**Explanation as submitted:** Authenticity does not override safe supervision and evidence requirements.

###### Question 9389

- **Question ID:** 9389
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Safety and Workplace Learning / Workplace Learning Coordination

**Stem**

Workplace feedback shows that learners can perform a process in a training shop but hesitate with real customer requirements. What response best connects training and employability?

**Options**

A. Replace technical practice with general customer-service lectures.
B. Add a supervised customer-oriented scenario that practices the same standard with communication and quality criteria.
C. Assume hesitation means learners are not technically competent.
D. Lower the work-quality criteria for workplace tasks.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Add a supervised customer-oriented scenario that practices the same standard with communication and quality criteria.

**Explanation as submitted:** A contextual scenario can develop transfer and communication without abandoning the technical standard.

#### Topic: Workplace Evidence and Assessment

##### Concept: Evidence-Based Performance Assessment

###### Question 9360

- **Question ID:** 9360
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Workplace Evidence and Assessment / Evidence-Based Performance Assessment

**Stem**

A learner is assessed on assembling a small product. Which evidence is most direct?

**Options**

A. Observation of the learner’s assembly and inspection of the completed product against criteria.
B. The learner’s attendance record for the assembly unit.
C. A peer’s description of the learner’s work.
D. The learner’s confidence rating before practice.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Observation of the learner’s assembly and inspection of the completed product against criteria.

**Explanation as submitted:** Direct observation and product evidence connect to the required performance.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66
- [2025 TVET Glossary](https://www.tesda.gov.ph/Uploads/File/Glossary/2025%20TVET%20Glossary.pdf) — Technical Education and Skills Development Authority, Philippines; OFFICIAL_GUIDANCE; 2025 TVET Glossary
  - Source-level relevant sections: pp. 25–27, 37–40, 89, 103; competency standards, CBC, CBLM, CBT, assessment evidence and moderation
- **Concept-specific source locator from the Batch 695 source crosswalk:** PRC Annex A; TESDA Glossary pp. 25–27, 89.
- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Gather sufficient direct evidence and judge demonstrated performance against a standard.

**Study lesson reference**

- Competency-Based Technical and Vocational Teaching — 7. Performance evidence and assessment
- Material ID: let-tvted-competency-based-teaching; section ID: 7-performance-evidence-and-assessment
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): A competency decision should be based on sufficient evidence directly related to the standard. Observe the learner perform, inspect the product or service, and ask focused questions when they clarify a decision that was not visible. Use clear criteria in a practical rubric: required process, output quality, safety checks, and task conditions. A rubric describes observable levels; it does not disguise personal preference as a standard. If assessors disagree, compare the evidence and the standard rather than relying on seniority.

###### Question 9361

- **Question ID:** 9361
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Workplace Evidence and Assessment / Evidence-Based Performance Assessment

**Stem**

A finished product meets the dimensions, but no one observed whether the learner used required safety steps. What should the assessor do?

**Options**

A. Assume safety was followed because the product is correct.
B. Gather evidence of the missing safety criteria before making a complete competency decision.
C. Mark every criterion as met based on the product alone.
D. Ask the learner whether they think they were safe and stop.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Gather evidence of the missing safety criteria before making a complete competency decision.

**Explanation as submitted:** The product cannot establish an unobserved process criterion; sufficient evidence must cover the standard.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

###### Question 9362

- **Question ID:** 9362
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Workplace Evidence and Assessment / Evidence-Based Performance Assessment

**Stem**

A learner explains a repair procedure accurately but has not performed it. The unit requires practical repair. What is missing?

**Options**

A. A longer written explanation of the same procedure.
B. A score for listening during the trainer’s demonstration.
C. Direct performance evidence under the required conditions.
D. A peer rating of whether the explanation sounded clear.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Direct performance evidence under the required conditions.

**Explanation as submitted:** Knowledge explanation can supplement but does not replace the practical performance required by the unit.
