###### Question 9363

- **Question ID:** 9363
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Workplace Evidence and Assessment / Evidence-Based Performance Assessment

**Stem**

Two assessors disagree about a candidate’s result. What should they compare first?

**Options**

A. Their relative years of experience.
B. The candidate’s popularity with the group.
C. The order in which they met the candidate.
D. The recorded performance evidence and the standard’s criteria and conditions.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. The recorded performance evidence and the standard’s criteria and conditions.

**Explanation as submitted:** Moderation focuses on a shared interpretation of evidence against the same standard.

###### Question 9364

- **Question ID:** 9364
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Workplace Evidence and Assessment / Evidence-Based Performance Assessment

**Stem**

An assessment task is interrupted by a power outage before the final test step. How should the evidence be handled?

**Options**

A. Record what was completed and arrange a fair opportunity to gather the missing evidence under suitable conditions.
B. Mark the whole competency successful based on the partial task.
C. Mark the learner unsuccessful without considering the interruption.
D. Ask a peer to complete the final step for the learner.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Record what was completed and arrange a fair opportunity to gather the missing evidence under suitable conditions.

**Explanation as submitted:** An interrupted task provides incomplete evidence; a controlled follow-up can make the decision fair.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A
  - Source-level relevant sections: Annex A, pp. 1–3; Annex B, pp. 4–8; Annex C, pp. 9–10; Annex D, pp. 11–12; BTVTEd material, pp. 65–66

**Study lesson reference**

- Competency-Based Technical and Vocational Teaching — 7. Performance evidence and assessment
- Material ID: let-tvted-competency-based-teaching; section ID: 7-performance-evidence-and-assessment
- BoardPrep lesson passage (instructional paraphrase; verify the linked authority above): A competency decision should be based on sufficient evidence directly related to the standard. Observe the learner perform, inspect the product or service, and ask focused questions when they clarify a decision that was not visible. Use clear criteria in a practical rubric: required process, output quality, safety checks, and task conditions. A rubric describes observable levels; it does not disguise personal preference as a standard. If assessors disagree, compare the evidence and the standard rather than relying on seniority.

###### Question 9365

- **Question ID:** 9365
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Workplace Evidence and Assessment / Evidence-Based Performance Assessment

**Stem**

A trainee demonstrates a skill correctly once, but the standard requires performance in two stated conditions. What is the best decision?

**Options**

A. Accept the first performance because it was correct.
B. Gather evidence in both required conditions before deciding whether the standard has been met.
C. Ask the trainee to describe the second condition instead of performing it.
D. Change the conditions after seeing the first performance.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. Gather evidence in both required conditions before deciding whether the standard has been met.

**Explanation as submitted:** The decision must reflect all required conditions and evidence, not an incomplete sample.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

##### Concept: Practical Performance Criteria

###### Question 9366

- **Question ID:** 9366
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Workplace Evidence and Assessment / Practical Performance Criteria

**Stem**

A practical rubric for a food-preparation task must assess safe handling. Which criterion is most observable?

**Options**

A. The learner appears responsible throughout class.
B. The learner shows a positive attitude toward food.
C. The learner separates ready-to-eat food from raw ingredients using the specified safe workflow.
D. The learner completes the work in a style the trainer likes.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. The learner separates ready-to-eat food from raw ingredients using the specified safe workflow.

**Explanation as submitted:** An observable criterion describes an action that can be checked against a task requirement.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

- **Core proposition to verify against those sources (taxonomy description, not a quotation):** Write observable, competency-aligned criteria for process, product, conditions, and safe practice.

###### Question 9367

- **Question ID:** 9367
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Workplace Evidence and Assessment / Practical Performance Criteria

**Stem**

A rubric says “excellent work” without describing quality levels. What is the best revision?

**Options**

A. Add more adjectives such as very excellent and outstanding.
B. Score work by comparing each learner with the fastest trainee.
C. Replace criteria with a single overall impression.
D. Describe observable differences in accuracy, completeness, and required process for each performance level.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Describe observable differences in accuracy, completeness, and required process for each performance level.

**Explanation as submitted:** Useful descriptors clarify what evidence distinguishes performance levels.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9368

- **Question ID:** 9368
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Workplace Evidence and Assessment / Practical Performance Criteria

**Stem**

A project rubric scores decoration heavily even though the competency is accurate technical measurement. What should be changed?

**Options**

A. Increase the weight of measurement accuracy and retain only presentation criteria connected to the stated outcome.
B. Keep the rubric because decorative work is easy to compare.
C. Remove all accuracy criteria so the task feels creative.
D. Add a behavior score for how quietly learners worked.

**Proposed correct answer:** A. Increase the weight of measurement accuracy and retain only presentation criteria connected to the stated outcome.

**Explanation as submitted:** Assessment criteria should align with the competency and not allow unrelated polish to dominate.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

###### Question 9369

- **Question ID:** 9369
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Workplace Evidence and Assessment / Practical Performance Criteria

**Stem**

A trainer wants to assess both the final weld and required preparation steps. How should the rubric be organized?

**Options**

A. Use one score for the final weld and infer every process step.
B. List separate observable criteria for preparation, safe process, and product quality.
C. Score only the preparation because it occurs first.
D. Use peer preference as the main quality criterion.

**Proposed correct answer:** B. List separate observable criteria for preparation, safe process, and product quality.

**Explanation as submitted:** Separate criteria make each part of the required performance visible and reviewable.

###### Question 9370

- **Question ID:** 9370
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Workplace Evidence and Assessment / Practical Performance Criteria

**Stem**

A rubric’s descriptors use “usually” and “sometimes” without defining what is observed. What is the most helpful improvement?

**Options**

A. Keep the wording because assessors can use personal judgment.
B. Replace the descriptors with a score from one to five only.
C. Anchor each level in observable frequency, accuracy, or completion evidence relevant to the task.
D. Add a longer paragraph about learner motivation.

**Proposed correct answer:** C. Anchor each level in observable frequency, accuracy, or completion evidence relevant to the task.

**Explanation as submitted:** Observable anchors show what assessors should see at each level. Defining the evidence makes scoring easier to explain and compare across performances.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

###### Question 9371

- **Question ID:** 9371
- **Academic-review status:** PENDING
- **Board / subject / topic / concept:** Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (LET) / Technical-Vocational Teacher Education / Workplace Evidence and Assessment / Practical Performance Criteria

**Stem**

A trainer is asked to assess a skill not listed in the unit standard because an employer prefers it. How should it be handled?

**Options**

A. Add it silently as a pass criterion for all trainees.
B. Ignore the unit criteria and assess only the employer preference.
C. Treat one employer’s preference as a new regulation.
D. Verify whether the skill is a required outcome and document any additional formative feedback separately.

**Proposed correct answer:** D. Verify whether the skill is a required outcome and document any additional formative feedback separately.

**Explanation as submitted:** Additional feedback may be useful, but a competency decision must use the valid controlling standard.

**Authoritative sources and locators**

- [PRC Board Resolution No. 20, s. 2025, Annex A: LEPT alignment and specialization syllabi](https://www.prc.gov.ph/sites/default/files/2025-20%20Annex%20teachers.pdf) — Professional Regulatory Board for Professional Teachers / Professional Regulation Commission; OFFICIAL_EXAM_SPEC; Resolution No. 20 s. 2025 Annex A

## Review completion and import

The companion JSON manifest is the machine-readable identity source. Import decisions by the string question_id and require an exact identity_sha256 match before accepting any decision. Never import by list position or stem alone. An authenticated review service must supply reviewer identity; a typed reviewer name in this document is not authentication. Store accepted decision events append-only with reviewer identity, timestamp, decision values, and the matched identity hash. Keep all unmatched or changed-content decisions in a conflict queue for manual resolution.
